# Paperole review UI: interaction spec (M2a, UI v2)

Current UI examples and data paths use Paperole. Historical implementation snapshots (§§26, 29 and 33), dated verification records and migration references keep the names used at the time; §34 defines the current bundle ids, folders and icons. `<repo>` denotes the checkout root, regardless of its local directory name.

2026-10-15; UI v2 (`design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md`, built and integrated 2026-10-17, then fixed after the interaction and visual reviews the same day) throughout, chiefly §1–5, 8–13 and 16–17; UI v2.1 (`design/ui-v2.1/POLISH.md`, built 2026-10-18 in the order its §6 set and integrated the same day, §10.21 and §13): the mouse-first answer flow and list (an answer moves on in Review, dimmed rows, For the student folded, no key letters on buttons or fields; §1, 2, 6, 8, 9), the design language with the Sage accent (§2, 3, 5, 19), one sidebar and the reading count in the status line, a click on a bar marking its paragraph read (§1, 17, 18), the done card, the Changes tab, the Finish sheet, the first-run tip and the empty states (§1, 2, 11, 16, 17), and Focus and Jump (§20); the "?" key sheet was dropped (POLISH §6); then fixed after the interaction and visual reviews of the integrated build (2026-10-18, §10.22 and §13: double clicks that acted twice, the toast after a paper switch, a follow-up written from Focus, a note edit lost on a paper switch, the answer row in a narrow panel, the find's toolbar, and the sidebar's, status line's and Focus bar's colours and sizes); then adjusted after the advisor used it (2026-10-18, POLISH §7): Focus shows the selected card on the right instead of a bar under the page, with a page-of-text icon (§20); dimmed rows fade as a whole (§1); group headers that read as headers (§1); "Still loading…" when the engine is busy (§11); then (the advisor's decision, 2026-10-18) reading marks became optional, off by default, in a Settings window (§18 "Optional"), and the student's notes (`% R1-03 …` lines in the .tex) show in the thread, on the rows and on the Changes tab, with the notes not used in one line on the Comments tab (§2, §3, §16); then (UI v2.2 fixes, 2026-10-18, the advisor's questions of that day and BACKLOG O28–O32) the Comments ↔ Changes switch slides (§1 Panel header), links look like links (§19 `link`), and leftovers were fixed: no stale group header over Focus's card (§6), the status line's page after a card scrolls the PDF back (§1), a point note's selection ring and its label (§5), the notes popover's words (§3), trimming that never cuts a changed run (§3), an ignored note near an edit (§16), section names cut at their end (§16), Edit Note and Re-anchor… while the engine works (§11), the Finish sheet's drafts scroll bar (§17), the toast over a group's header (§8), the panel's first width (§1) and the sidebar's Papers header (§17); then (UI v2.2 card, `design/ui-v2.2/CARD.md` option C, the advisor's decision of 2026-10-19) the card reads as a conversation in turns, each round saying who did what, the student's words before the evidence, an edit a note linked elsewhere shown on the card, earlier rounds' long bubbles cut to whole sentences, folding in the middle (§2, §3, §4, §16, §20). This covers the Review screen of the Mac app (`Sources/Paperole`). It answers the advisor's M1 feedback: full old and new sentences on cards; selection between the PDF and the panel; smooth scrolling; mark colours and kinds; easier editing.

DESIGN.md decides intent, CONTRACT.md decides names and shapes, and this file decides interaction. Units are points, and page coordinates follow CONTRACT §3. The engine data the cards need is listed in §15.

**Kept from M1:** the app-owned drag selection (`Geometry.clampedPoint`, 3 pt threshold); margin id labels; the flash; the Changes tab and change bands; J/K/V/R/W, where the same verdict key again clears the verdict (the words of design/ui-v2 since 2026-10-17: V Resolve, R Follow up, W Keep as is; §8); the request queue; "a typed note is never lost silently"; the engine as the only writer; in-memory marks.

## 1. Layout
- **Widths** (POLISH §3 row 2, 2026-10-18: four panes became three, about 200 pt more for the page): sidebar (200–340, ideal 236; "Jump to…", Papers and Contents, §17, §18) | PDF (min 420) | panel (min 320, ideal 400, max 560). The window's minimum width is the sidebar's 200 plus the review's 760, 960 (`WindowLayout`), so the panel is never clipped (an M1 leftover); at that width the status line shortens its reading count ("10 to read") and the build's words give way first. The panel keeps its width when the window is resized or the sidebar comes or goes, and the page takes the room, as beside an inspector (POLISH §7; `PDFController.holdPanel`: its holding priority in the split view is above the PDF's, set once the window has settled; left equal, the split view shared each change out and the panel drifted by 40–50 pt over a Focus switch). Its divider still sets it. **It opens at its ideal 400 pt** (BACKLOG O30: it opened at the split view's share, 497 or 520 pt in a 1440 pt window), or at the width the advisor last dragged it to in this run (another paper's split view is a new one), within 320–560 and the PDF's 420 (`PDFController.openPanel`, `openingWidth`; set when the split view first settles). Only a drag of the divider outside Focus is remembered (`PDFController.dividerDragged`: a drag of the mouse in the window, not a live resize of the window, the split view as wide as before), not a width the window or the sidebar forced on the panel (review 2026-10-18: after a 960 pt window had squeezed it to 339 pt, the next paper opened at 339 in a wide window again). In Focus it goes no wider than it was when Focus began (`AppModel.focusPanelWidth`, the panel's maximum width in `PaperView` while Focus lasts; review 2026-10-18): in a window narrow enough that the PDF's minimum width had squeezed the panel below the width it holds, the panel sprang back to that width in Focus, wider than the page, and shrank again on leaving; now all the sidebar's room goes to the page, and leaving Focus gives Review its own panel width back. The outline column (160–260 pt, toggled in the toolbar) is gone: its rows are the sidebar's Contents.
- **Toolbar** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §3 and §6 addition 10; 2026-10-17): the paper's title (13 Semibold) with the subtitle under it (11, secondary): the student and the paper's next step, "Mei Lin · 7 comments and 2 edits to check", with the numbers of the panel's two tabs ("1 comment to check", "2 edits to check"; with none of those, "6 changed paragraphs to read" while some are and reading marks are on (§18; the engine's `summary` does not count them, so the app adds the shown open round's, `PaperSummary.unread`; interaction review 2026-10-18: it said "ready to finish" over "6 changed paragraphs to read"), and "ready to finish" with nothing left, "in progress" in round 1, "waiting for student" once the latest round is closed, "build failed"; while an earlier round is shown, the latest round's from `summary`). Then the round menu, `Round 3 ⌄`, the only place the round is named (§17), as plain text with its chevron (`.borderlessButton`; the mockup's; it drew as a bezelled pull-down). On the right: the Change marks toggle as an icon only, then **Focus**, named "Focus" beside its symbol as final.pdf and New Round… are (review 2026-10-18: the page glyph alone, next to final.pdf's page, read as one more document) (`doc.plaintext`, a page of text as Safari's Reader, drawn as `text.page` from macOS 15 and present on 14; the same symbol on and off, the toggle pressed while Focus is on; its help tag names F; POLISH §2.6, §3 row 5, §7: the arrows to the corners, `arrow.up.left.and.arrow.down.right`, read as full screen; §20), each with a help tag (`highlighter` since POLISH §2.6, as `text.redaction` read as a menu icon; the outline's `list.bullet.indent` toggle went with the outline column, POLISH §3 row 1: the sidebar's toggle is the system's `sidebar.left` at the top of the sidebar, and View › Show / Hide Sidebar, ⌃⌘S, `SidebarCommands`), the progress while the engine works, and Finish Round… (§17): a plain button, and the accent's, the window's one prominent button, once the round is ready to finish (from round 2 on, no comment or other edit to check and, with reading marks on, no changed paragraph to read; `AppModel.readyToFinish`; POLISH §3 row 1, §6 decision 10); for a closed round [final.pdf] (a click opens it; its menu: Show in Finder and Copy Email Text, §17), and [New Round…] once no round is open; last, the find in the paper (§32), whose room the title gives up (`toolbarReserve`: 460 pt for the items after the title, 585 once the latest round is closed and New Round… shows). The Comment and Box buttons are gone: the floating toolbar (§7), Return, ⌥-drag and B do them, and so does the menu bar's **Comment** menu (Comment on Selection (Return), Draw a Box Round a Figure, Table or Equation (B, or ⌥-drag); the keys are named in the titles only, as in the Reading menu). From macOS 15 the toolbar shows the title itself (`toolbar(removing: .title)`), so the round menu can follow it; macOS 14 shows the system title after the menu.
- **Status line** under the PDF (26 pt, 11 pt; not under the sidebar or the panel), on the panel's opaque grey (`Palette.panel`; POLISH §2.4: it was the translucent bar, white in Light Mode, tinted by the desktop in Dark), its words and the page in the palette's secondary (5.2:1) and the reading count with Next and Mark Read in the second label colour (visual review 2026-10-18: the system's secondary was 3.95:1 on the bar): the build, "✓ Built · 9 pages · 3 warnings · Details…", with a grey tick; red only when the build is broken ("⚠ Built with 2 errors", "Build failed, no PDF", under the failed-build view); Details… (a link, §19 `link`) opens the warnings, errors and log, headed by the engine and main file and the round's source; on the right the reading count, "▍12 changed paragraphs to read · ↓ Next · ✓ Mark Read" (POLISH §3 row 3; §18; only while reading marks are on), then the page shown, "p.1 of 9": the page a third of the way down the view, where the Contents' current section is read too (`PDFController.shownPage`, followed at every scroll of the PDF's clip view; BACKLOG O32: PDFView's `currentPage` did not follow a scroll it did not make, so after a card's reveal scrolled the PDF back to p.7 the line said p.8 until the PDF was scrolled by hand) (`PDFPosition`, which only the line observes, so scrolling redraws nothing else; the reading count observes only its own `ReadingStatus`). It replaces M1's build bar above the PDF. It stays in Focus (§20; POLISH §7: the docked bar that took its place there is gone).
- **Read only**: a closed round's panel has a neutral box under its tabs (34 pt, inset 12 pt, radius 7, the chips' quiet fill, a lock in secondary (ⓘ until the audit of 2026-10-20, §25); the mockup's 06f; visual review 2026-10-18: it ran across the panel above the tabs): "Round 2 · closed Oct 9 · read only", with [Back to Round 3] while an earlier round is shown (§17); when the words do not fit beside the button, "· read only" goes (the lock says it), never cut to "read o…". It replaces M1's "Round N is closed" line and the accent-coloured banner across the paper view.
- **Panel header**: [Comments 7 | Changes 2], each number what is left to do there in an open round: carried comments without an answer (To check and Not found; `AppModel.commentsToCheck`), and unrequested edits to check (§16); no number when none is left or the round is closed. The tabs look as the system's segmented control at its large size (POLISH §2.3, §3 row 6): a quiet track across the panel (`Palette.chipFill`, radius 8, 28 pt), the shown tab on a raised segment (white with a soft shadow and a 5% hairline; white at 17% in Dark Mode), 13 pt, the shown tab's name in the label colour and the other's in the second label colour (`PanelTabs`). **The switch slides** (UI v2.2, the advisor, 2026-10-18: the system's control and the list under it switched as blocks, abruptly): the raised segment slides to the tab clicked, and the content slides the same way in the shared-axis pattern of tabbed content, in 0.25 s, ease-out (`TabMotion`): the page left moves an eighth of the panel's width (at most 56 pt) toward the tab it leaves for and fades out over the first 35%, the page shown comes from as far on the other side and fades in from 30% to the end, so the two lists' words barely overlap; with Reduce Motion the switch is at once. A switch during a switch turns round from where the pages are. Each tab's page holds its own top lines, the Comments tab's line of notes not used (§3) and the Changes tab's sentence (§16), so they slide with it and nothing above the lists moves. Both pages live as long as the panel (`PanelPager`, `PagerView`): each keeps its scroll position, rows and expanded card or change across switches (until UI v2.2 the list shown was made again at each switch and centred its card), and a switch lays out no list; only the pages' layers move, with Core Animation, so no list or page is laid out and nothing in them is updated during the slide; the raised segment's slide is the tabs' own SwiftUI animation, drawn each frame on the main thread (it stalls in a window hidden behind others, where the pages still slide). The shown page takes the clicks, the pointer and the scroll wheel from the start; the other is hidden once it has gone (a hidden list keeps no row hovered and does not place the shared toast, `PanelTableView.viewDidHide`). Still measured once (`MeasuredOnce`, §10.10), though its text is SwiftUI's own now, which the panel's stacks do not ask CoreUI to size. The changed words on the cards' threads are underlined unless **View › Underline Changed Words** is off (on by default, saved in UserDefaults); it was a `U` button beside the tabs until POLISH §6, 11. The M1 summary line is gone: the groups say it (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §3).
- **Groups** (2026-10-17, design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §2 and §6 decision 6), by whose move it is, each under a floating header (`CardGroups`; `SectionHeader`). Over them a line's menu, "By status ⌄", arranges the list by tag or by section instead, or shows only one tag's comments (§27). **The header** (POLISH §7, 2026-10-18: at 12 pt Semibold in the second label colour it was smaller than the rows' notes and read as one of them; after Linear's and GitHub's status groups, Things' headings and Reminders' Show): 34 pt (it was 26) on the panel's opaque grey with a hairline (`Palette.rule`, 1 px) under it across the panel, so it also stands apart when it floats over the rows; the group's sign, 12 pt Semibold, starting at the rows' content edge, where their chips' signs and their notes start (26 pt from the panel's edge, `SectionHeader.lead`; the bars hang left of it, as under a row), in a 23 pt column, the widest sign's 18 and 5 before the name, so every header's name starts at the same place (review 2026-10-18: the sign sat over the bars and the name between the notes and the ids, lining up with nothing; `HeaderAlignmentTests` measures the drawn ink) (To check `circle` in amber, the list's one amber, grey as Left open in a closed round; For the student `envelope` (it goes to the student); Closed this round `checkmark.circle`; both in the secondary colour); the name 14 Semibold in the label colour (the rows' note is 13, the card's title 15), no capitals; a folded group's count in a capsule (11 Semibold, tabular digits, the second label colour on the chips' quiet fill), folded or shown; the quieter words after it (12, secondary), which give way first in a narrow panel; Show › / Hide ⌄ on the right (12 Medium, secondary), ending where the rows' page numbers end. 10 pt of panel above each header but the list's first (and under the done card, which has its own margin; `PanelTable.spaced`), so two folded groups read as two sections, each with its rule. A folded group's **whole header** is its Show / Hide (`HeaderHost`, AppKit's click and pointer, which a floating group row needs): a click that ends on it folds or unfolds the group once (a double click's second click does nothing), and the pointer over it gives it the rows' hover fill (a stronger one while pressed), with the arrow pointer. A header that does not fold takes no click and shows no fill. Headers never dim. VoiceOver reads the name, count and quieter words as a header, a folded group's as a button with "Shows the group" / "Hides the group". **No header floats while the table changes in a way that can scroll the list** (`PanelTable.beginUpdate` / `endUpdate`, around every row and height change: a J/K step, a click, an answer, a card that grows or shrinks, a resize): the table resizes itself range by range inside a change, a collapsing card before the one opening below it, so when a change could leave the list shorter than its scroll, the list scrolled in the middle of the table's update, and when that brought a floating header's place back into view the table gave the header a second view; its next update threw "The row at 0 is not floating" and the app quit (visual review 2026-10-18: the demo at the panel's 400 pt, R1-19, R2-07, then R2-08's mark clicked in the PDF; also through Jump and in a narrower window). The headers stop floating for that change only, when it can scroll the list (rows that shrink or go at the list's end), and float again before anything is drawn; a step elsewhere in the list changes nothing. The new card's top then stays where it was on screen as far as the list allows, as before.
  1. **To check**: carried comments without an answer, Not found included, in document order. The header has no number (the Comments tab has it; POLISH §4.4: each number once). In a closed round: **Left open**. Shown only when it has cards.
  2. **For the student** (3) goes in the Round 3 final.pdf (the count in its capsule, the last words quieter; "in the Round 2 final.pdf" in a closed round; there was an email until 2026-10-26, §32): the follow-ups, then the new comments, what final.pdf asks for. Folded by default into that one line, with Show › / Hide ⌄, as Closed this round (POLISH §6; the panel remembers Show while the app runs): J and K skip it while it is folded, selecting one of its cards another way (a click on its mark, a new comment, ⌘Z) shows it, and Hide with one of them selected leaves no card selected. In an open round it is there even when empty, as a plain header without a count, and while the round has no new comment it ends (when shown) with the one-line hint "To comment, select text in the PDF and click Comment." (mouse first, POLISH §6; it said "press Return"). An open round with no comments at all lists nothing but the empty state (§11).
  3. **Closed this round** (4) with Show › / Hide ⌄ on the right: Resolved and Kept as is, folded by default (the panel remembers Show while the app runs); shown, one 30 pt line each (§2). J and K skip the group while it is folded; selecting one of its comments another way (a click on its mark, ⌘Z, a chip on the Changes tab) shows it. Hide with one of its comments selected leaves no comment selected, since a card that is not listed takes no keys (the Changes tab's folded group likewise, §16).
- **The done card** (POLISH §3 row 9, §6 decision 10): once nothing is left in the open round (from round 2 on, no comment or other edit to check and, with reading marks on, no changed paragraph to read, `AppModel.readyToFinish`, `PanelModel.ready`), a calm raised card heads the list, its content centred in it: a check circle, "Nothing left to check in this round.", "Finish when you are ready." and a plain [Finish Round 3…] (the Finish sheet, §17); no numbers, which the groups under it say. Finish Round… in the toolbar is then the window's one prominent button (§1 Toolbar). It waits until To check is empty in the list: the last card answered stays there while it is selected (below), and a card inserted above it pushed it down under the pointer (a list that short cannot scroll to keep it in place); it comes when the selection leaves that card (a row, J or K, a mark), or at once when the last thing done was reading a paragraph or checking an edit with no card held there.
- **Nothing moves under the pointer**: a card stays in the group it was shown in while it is selected, whatever its answer (`PanelModel.held`), and moves to its own group when the selection leaves it. An answer moves the selection on (§8), and the next card takes the answered card's place on screen (§6). The list then removes and inserts only the rows that move, and keeps the newly selected card where it is on screen (`PanelTable.show`, §6). An undo selects its card in the group of the answer it restores, so an undone Resolve is back among the cards to check, not in the folded group. Finishing the round ends the hold: the selected card is then listed in its own group of the closed round, so a card resolved with a click is not under "Left open".
- **Dimmed rows** (POLISH §6, §7): while a card is expanded, the other rows fade as a whole, at 66% opacity in Light Mode and 55% in Dark Mode (`RowLook.opacity`): bar, sign, id, word, page and note together, so each row keeps its own order and steps back, and the expanded card is the one thing at full strength; the pointer over a row shows it as it is. A dimmed row's note has about 5:1 on the panel in either appearance (the plain row's 14:1), still easy to read. Until POLISH §7 a dimmed row's text turned the palette's secondary grey: measured on background screenshots (Dark Mode) its note was luma 166 against 234 for the card's title and about as light as the card's paper text, and its id, word and page did not change, so the advisor did not see it as dimmed. Now (the same rows): Dark Mode note 141 against 234 for the same row under the pointer, on the panel's 29; Light Mode 108 against 36, on 246 (the 3% most inked pixels of the note's line). The colours keep their meaning (the signs, "position uncertain", "not found", "unsent draft"), only fainter. Each row has a look of its own (`RowLook`), set by the table for every row at once, the hidden row under the expanded card too, so a J/K step from one card to another changes no row: only the first card expanded (and the last one closed) redraws the rows. The Changes tab does not dim.
- **Hover** (2026-10-18: a background capture showed R2-05 and R2-02 both hovered long after the pointer had left them): the pointer over a row gives it the light fill and, while a card is expanded, its full strength. At most one row is hovered, and only the one a click at the pointer would reach (`PanelTable.item(at:)`): not one under the expanded card, a floating header or the toast. The list owns it, one id (`PanelTable.hovered`) that the rows' looks follow (`RowLook.hovering`), and tracks the pointer itself, with one tracking area over the list while its window is key; the rows have none (each row's own enter and exit, SwiftUI's onHover, missed exits when the window was not key or was covered, and when rows moved under a still pointer). Nothing is hovered once the pointer leaves the list, while the list scrolls (a live scroll until it ends, any other scroll until the list has been still for 0.15 s, `PanelTable.settleDelay`; the pointer's moves do not count meanwhile), when rows come or go (a card leaving its group, a group shown, Focus; not when only a row's content changes), when the window resigns key and when the app resigns active; the row under the pointer, where it is then, is hovered again once the list is still and when the window becomes key. So a window that is not key shows no hover. A J/K step redraws no row with the pointer off the list, and with the pointer resting on it only the row it leaves and the row now under it, once the rows have settled. The Changes tab's rows alike.

## 2. Card anatomy
Exactly one card is expanded: the selected one. All others are compact rows. The selection follows J/K, a click on a row, a click on a mark in the PDF (§6), the creation of a new comment, and `--select`.

**Compact row** (exactly 44 pt; design/ui-v2 2026-10-17): flat on the panel, with no fill, border or separator, rows touching; a 3 pt bar in the mark's colour 6 pt inside its left edge (10 pt from top and bottom), so its chip lines up with the card's; a light fill under the pointer. Line 1 (18 pt): the chip (in a row the sign and the id with no fill, the sign neutral grey and the id in the second label colour, only Not found's red: the group's header already says whose move it is, so sixteen amber chips no longer say it sixteen times; POLISH §3 row 7), the row's word for the round (12 pt, secondary), a speech-bubble sign (`bubble.left`, 11 pt, in the second label colour, neutral; `StudentNoteSign`; UI v2.2, CARD §3.9: `quote.bubble` looked too much like the comment sign `text.bubble`) when the student wrote a note on the comment in the shown round (§3 "The student's note"; the closed line, the Changes tab's rows and the lines about ignored notes have the same sign), and "unsent draft" (amber tag) when the card's follow-up field holds text that was not sent (§8). In To check the word is the shown round's event in the card's thread (§3), what happened to the paper (CARD §3.4): "revised" (also text moved with edits round it, which the card calls "moved and revised it"), "moved" ("moved to p.6") when the student only moved it, the move attached and no word changed ("moved it" on the card; review 2026-10-19: a move had said "revised"), "deleted", "restored", "new image file"; "unchanged" or "moved to p.6" when the round changed nothing (a note with words only keeps "unchanged", with the sign); "edit on p.6" when the only edit is one the student's note linked elsewhere (UI v2.2: "unchanged" said the student did nothing); "revised in R2" when an earlier round's section holds it (a fix made before is not "unchanged"; so the demo's R1-13, revised in R2 and answered with words only in R4, reads "revised in R2" with the sign; "moved in R2" when that section only moved it); "position uncertain" in amber with a warning sign; "not found · re-anchor" in red (the chip has the sign; "not found" in a closed round). In For the student it is the status, "follow-up" or "new", what final.pdf asks for (the mockup's rows; visual review 2026-10-17). In a closed round nothing is the advisor's move, so nothing is amber: a To check chip is neutral and "position uncertain" grey without the sign, in the rows and on the card. On the right: `p.5` (11 pt). Line 2: the note's first line (13 pt), truncated at the tail. With an empty note, the quote in quotation marks, in secondary italic serif. The colour dot with the kind glyph is gone from the row: the bar gives the colour, and the expanded card's `⋯` menu the kind. While another card is expanded the row fades as a whole, and the pointer restores it (§1).

**Closed line** (exactly 30 pt), in the Closed this round group: the bar at 45% (8 pt from top and bottom), the chip, the student's note sign when there is one, then "resolved" or "kept as is" and the closing remark's first line after " · " (12 pt, secondary, one line). A click opens its card, as a row's does.

**Expanded card** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §2, §3; 2026-10-17), padding 12 × 16, radius 10 (POLISH §2.4), top to bottom:
1. **Header** (20 pt): the chip and the status word (12 pt; To check in amber Medium, Not found in red, the others secondary). In a closed or earlier round a comment without an answer says **Left open** (secondary), as its group does, and so do the chip's help tag and VoiceOver: "To check" would ask for an answer that can no longer be given (audit, 2026-10-17; `Status.word(open:)`). On the right, 12 pt apart, in an open round the mark's colour dot (10 pt, its colour; a click shows its swatches and kinds in a popover, §8, §32), `p.5`, which reveals the mark, and `⋯` (16 pt; the colour dot and ⓘ went into it, POLISH §3 row 8, §6 decision 4), a menu: Edit Note, Colour ›, Kind ›, Source and Edits… (the old ⓘ: a popover at `⋯` with the source location (`file:line`), how the engine placed the mark ("Placed by: diff"), this round's edits (links that reveal them) and the picture files they replaced; `EditState.info`), Re-anchor…, Copy ID, Delete…. Keys 1–6 still recolour. No confidence is shown, as a number or otherwise.
2. **The note** is the card's title (POLISH §2.3, §6 decision 6; `NoteTitle`): its first sentence 15/20, Semibold, or Medium when it is in Chinese or Japanese, and the rest 13 Regular on the lines under it; when the first sentence is longer than about 80 Latin characters (a CJK character counts twice), the whole note is one 14 pt Medium block, so a long note never becomes a big bold one. A sentence ends at 。！？, or at . ! ? before a space and a capital or a CJK character (not at "Fig. 1" or "e.g. the"), or at the first line break. It is one Text (a J/K step lays out one selectable text, as before). It is what the advisor said when the comment was made (sent in its round's final.pdf, then never edited), so your first turn has no bubble; it is never cut, however long (CARD §1.3: the last sentences of a long note are usually the request). A new comment's note is edited by clicking it (§8); on hover it shows a light background and a pencil. A carried comment's note is selectable text.
3. **The conversation** (§3, §4): the turns on the rail, each led by its round and who did what, ending with this round's follow-up field where the rail ends (§8) or this round's answer as your last turn: "R3 · you followed up" / "you resolved it" / "you kept it as is", then the follow-up or closing remark in the grey bubble with "Goes out in the Round 3 final.pdf" or "Closing remark · in final.pdf, not a to-do" and [Edit] (§8); in a closed round "sent Oct 9" (the day the round was finished) beside the label and no Edit.
4. **Answer row**, for carried comments in an open round only (§8), 12 pt below the thread: [✓ Resolve] [↩ Follow up] … [Keep as is] (quieter, on the right; 26 pt, radius 7, 13 pt, POLISH §2.3–2.4; in a narrow panel a button that does not fit goes to the next line, 6 pt below, Keep as is still at the right edge, and so do the choice row's, `AnswerRow`: interaction review 2026-10-18, a card with a draft ran "Resolve + note", "Keep as is + note", Discard and ⋯ out of a panel under about 395 pt), with no key letters (POLISH §6, mouse first: their help tags name V, R and W), or in its place the choice row after a bare V or W on text. Under the buttons of a card to check, **the one first-run tip** (POLISH §3 row 14), on a quiet accent fill (`Palette.accentTint`, 13% / 28%) with a lightbulb: "Answer with Resolve, Follow up or Keep as is. The next comment to check then takes this one's place." and [Got it]; it shows on every card to check until Got it or the first answer, then never again (remembered as `tipAnswerSeen`; not by a `--background` or fixture run; `Tips`, which only the tip observes, so a J/K step pays nothing once it is gone). When the card is taller than the panel and runs past its bottom edge, the row stays 8 pt above that edge, on the card's colour with a hairline above it, and the thread scrolls under it (`PinState`, set by the panel's table as it scrolls; never above the card's first 120 pt). A new comment in an open round has no answer row: its one turn ends with the line "Goes out in the Round 3 final.pdf · click the note to edit" under its text (§3; Delete… is in the `…` menu; the line's help tag names ⌫).

The card is the panel's one raised layer: white with a soft shadow on the panel's `#F6F6F4`, or `#2A2C2A` with a 7.5% hairline on the panel's `#1C1D1C` in Dark Mode (`Palette.card`, `Palette.panel`; POLISH §2.2, §19; before it, `#2C2C2E` on `#1C1C1E`), 4 pt below the row above it and 8 pt above the next. It has no bar at its edge since POLISH §3 row 8: the mark's colour is the 3 pt rule beside this round's text (§3), at 45% on a closed card (Resolved, Kept as is). No accent outline.

**Chip** (20 pt, radius 5; POLISH §2.2, §3 row 8): the status icon (10 pt Semibold) and the id (11 Semibold, tabular digits). A decided status on a quiet fill with a neutral icon; **To check outlined** in amber (1 pt at 62%, no fill), so it is told from the yellow and orange marks by its shape (its amber is ΔE00 9.5 and 6.9 from their inks, 9.9 and 9.4 from their fills in Dark Mode; §19); Not found tinted red. In a closed round To check is neutral too. In a row the sign and id have no fill (the compact row above). Status never relies on colour alone.

**Status words** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §1.4; the ledger keeps `verified`, `reopened`, `wontfix`). Amber means only "your move", red only an error; a decided status is neutral grey (`#6B7280`, Dark Mode `#A8ADB7`) and told apart by its icon. Revised, unchanged, moved, deleted and restored are events of the thread, not statuses.

| status | ledger | colour | icon | PDF glyph | word |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| new this round | — | neutral | `plus` | `+` | New |
| carried, no answer | `null` | `#B45309` / `#F59E0B`, outlined chip | `circle` | `○` | To check |
| carried, no answer, not found (`orphan`); counts as To check | `null` | `#C42121` (`#DC2626` until POLISH §6, 13: 4.46:1 on the panel) / `#F98B8B`, tinted chip | `exclamationmark.triangle` | `⚠` | Not found |
| followed up | `reopened` | neutral | `arrow.uturn.backward` | `↩` | Follow-up |
| resolved | `verified` | neutral | `checkmark` | `✓` | Resolved |
| kept as is | `wontfix` | neutral | `minus` | `–` | Kept as is |

## 3. The conversation: turns over the thread's sections
2026-10-17 (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §2 and §6, examples a–i of `mockup.html`): the thread replaced the When commented, Follow-up and Now blocks. Since UI v2.2 (2026-10-19; `design/ui-v2.2/CARD.md` option C, as the advisor decided: turns in one column, the student's words before the evidence, the student named as the paper's record has it) the card reads it as a conversation in **turns**, each saying after its round who did what: "R3 · you followed up", "R4 · Ouyang Mingxuan replaced the image · 1 edit" (CARD §2.2: the timeline of sections hung R3's follow-up under R1's text and split the student's answer into two labels, the note after the picture it explained). Two pure functions build it. `CardContent.thread(comment, round:)`, from `history[]` (each round's `context`, `anchor_state`, `verdict`, `verdict_note`, attached `changes`, `figures` and `student_note`, CONTRACT amendment "Thread cards") and the shown round's record, still decides which rounds revised the text, what each shows and what is underlined ("Sections", "Text"); `CardContent.turns(comment, sections:, round:, student:)` only splits and names them (`Turn`), and the view reads nothing else. Nothing new comes from the engine but `history[].change_pages` (§15).

**Sections** (`thread`). The first round (the comment's creation) is a section: "R1 · as commented" ("R3 · selected text", "selected area", "note placed near this text" or "note on an empty area" for a new comment, which has only this one). A later round is a section of its own when the engine says the student changed something there, never from comparing the printed text (the shown sentences vary with the attached changes, cards.py `record_context`, so a comparison would make sections in which nothing changed):

| the round | section | label |
|---|---|---|
| `changed`, or changes attached | yes | "R3 · revised"; "new image file" for a figure or box whose picture file changed (the shown round: a change with `figure`; an earlier round: one its `history[].figures` names, or, from an engine that does not name them, changes attached to an anchor that did not change). Changes a student's note alone attached, outside the commented text (`student_note.elsewhere`), are not counted here |
| `unchanged` | no: a tag on the section above | "unchanged in R2", "unchanged in R2 and R3" |
| `moved`, nothing attached | no: a tag | "moved to p.6 in R2" ("moved in R2" on the same page) |
| `moved` with changes | yes | "R2 · moved" (a section moved to the appendix with edits around it); a figure whose picture file changed too: "new image file" with a tag "moved to p.5". It **only moved** (`ThreadSection.onlyMoved`) when no word changed: in the shown round each attached change is a move or reads the same before and after (not in `CardContent.edits`) and nothing is underlined; in an earlier round, whose changes the state names but does not hold, no word differs from the text above (a crop has none to compare) |
| `gone` | yes | "R3 · deleted": the text around the cut, quiet, with a caret at `cut`, never underlined; with nothing printed near it, "Nothing is printed near the cut." |
| `gone` again | no: a tag ("still deleted in R4"), unless changes are attached ("revised near the cut") | |
| after `gone`: `moved` | yes | "restored" (the engine's `moved` of text typed in again, anchor.py `_restored`) |
| `orphan` | yes | "no text found", with "Not found in R3. Choose Re-anchor… to place it." (the link in an open round); again: a tag "still not found in R4" |
| after `orphan` | yes | "revised", or "found again" |

These labels and tags are the sections' own (the `thread` debug item lists them); since UI v2.2 the card shows the turns below, which name the same rounds by who acted.

**Turns** (CARD §3.2; `Turn.Kind`), in the order things happened; in one round the student's (or the engine's) turn comes first and yours after it, as you spoke after reading that round's paper:
- **Your first turn**: "R1 · you commented on" ("you marked" with no note; a new comment's, its only one: "R4 · you selected text", "you selected an area", "you placed a note near this text", "you placed a note on an empty area"), the page it was written on when it is not the card's, as the verb's object ("R1 · you commented on p.4", "you marked it on p.4"; until the audit of 2026-10-20 " · p.4"); then the text you marked, the mark's tint at full strength, or the crop with its box drawn solid. With the title it says what you asked and about what, so it never folds. A new comment's, in an open round, ends 12 pt under its text with "Goes out with the Round 4 email · click the note to edit" (11 pt, secondary), in the turn's column (CARD §3.2; until the review of 2026-10-19 it was at the card's foot, at the chip's edge).
- **The student's turn**, for a round the engine says changed the commented text (a section of its own), or in which the student's note linked an edit elsewhere, or the student only wrote words. The verb says what happened to the paper (CARD §3.4): "revised", "replaced the image" (" · moved to p.5" when it moved to another page too), "deleted it", "revised near the cut", "restored it", "moved and revised it" (" · p.6" only when not the card's page, so never in the shown round), "moved it" when it only moved (above; the same page rule; "moved and revised it" said the student rewrote a sentence that was only moved), "edited elsewhere", "answered · no edit". Then the count, " · 1 edit": the round's attached edits, those the note linked elsewhere included ("revised · 2 edits"); in the shown round a link that shows the first in the PDF (one in the commented text first); in an earlier round plain words, its edits not being in this PDF, and for an edit elsewhere its page, " · 1 edit on p.6" (`history[].change_pages`); no count when there is none, nor for the edit the verb already is: "deleted it" (CARD §3.3; the engine attaches the deletion) and "moved it" count only an edit the note linked elsewhere. In a round where the engine takes the turn (not found, found again) or that is otherwise quiet, a note with an edit attached is "revised · 1 edit", not "answered · no edit". In the drawn label " · 1 edit" is joined by no-break spaces, so a long name wraps before the verb's last word ("moved and" / "revised it · 1 edit"), never between "·" and the count or inside the link. Then the student's words (below), then the evidence, that round's text or crop as before; in the shown round then the edits the note linked elsewhere (below). Its dot is green when the paper changed, here or elsewhere, hollow for words only and for "moved it".
- **The engine's turn**: "R3 · text not found", the first round the anchor is lost, with "Not found in R3. Choose Re-anchor… to place it." under it in red (the link in the shown open round); "R4 · found again" (" · p.7" when not the card's page) with that round's text. Found with the text changed is the student's "revised".
- **A quiet line**: an earlier round in which nothing happened (unchanged; moved with nothing attached; still deleted; still not found) and the student wrote nothing: "R2–R3 · unchanged" (rounds in a row with the same nothing, no turn between them, are one line), "R2 · moved to p.6" (the page when it is not the card's, else "moved"), "R3 · still deleted", "R3 · still not found"; 11 pt Regular, its round Medium, all in the secondary colour, a small hollow dot. **The shown round's** is a line of its own, never merged, in the labels' own weight with a full hollow dot (a red ring when still not found): "R4 · unchanged since you commented" after your first turn ("unchanged since you marked it" after "you marked"), "unchanged since your follow-up" after your follow-up, "still unchanged" after an earlier "unchanged" line, "unchanged" after the student's turn or an earlier "moved" (it had moved, so not "still"); "R4 · moved", "still deleted", "still not found". That nothing happened this round is the card's news, often the reason for another follow-up, so it is not the faintest line on the card.
- **Your turn** of an earlier round: "R3 · you followed up" ("you resolved it", "you kept it as is") with "sent Oct 17" on the right (the date that round was finished; "emailed" until 2026-10-26; secondary, as every date, POLISH §2.2), then your words in the grey bubble, or the label alone.
- **Your answer this round**, the last turn: "R4 · you followed up" ("you resolved it", "you kept it as is"), then the follow-up or closing remark in the bubble with "Goes out in the Round 4 final.pdf" or "Closing remark · in final.pdf, not a to-do" and [Edit], edited in place (§8); in a closed round "sent Oct 17" on the right and no Edit. With no answer, the follow-up field ends the conversation where the rail ends, with no dot (§8).

**Labels and dots** (CARD §3.3). A label is 11/16: the round Semibold in the label colour; who acted, "you" or the student's name as the paper's record has it (`PaperInfo.student`; "the student" when it is empty; never a pronoun), Semibold in the second label colour; the rest Medium in the secondary. Only a date is on the right, so with a 15-character name a label is one line in a 400 pt panel; a longer one wraps under itself and is never cut. **Position uncertain** (below 0.75, `Comment.lowConfidence`; a note placed by a click is exempt, carried or new, its place being a guess by design): the turn that holds the shown round has the amber tag "position uncertain" beside its label, or under it when it does not fit (`FlowRow`), and under its text "The faint tint shows where the mark sits now, and it may be on the wrong words. Check it on p.4; if it is wrong, choose Re-anchor…" (the link in an open round); an anchor on no page says so the same way. The dots, on a 1 pt rail 18 pt in (`Railed`), say what kind of turn it was, four kinds: neutral grey filled (`Palette.neutral`), you; green filled (`Palette.change`), the student changed the paper; hollow (tertiary), nothing changed (words only, found again, the shown round unchanged), 5 pt for an earlier quiet line; a red ring, not found. The fold's line has › in a dot's place; the field has none, and the rail stops at it. Green still means only that the paper changed: the student's words are in the same grey bubble as yours, as "kept as is" in a green bubble would read as "fixed".

**Text.** A section shows its round's `context.text` (§15), the whole sentence(s) that hold the anchor as that round's PDF printed them, in New York 13/20 with a 2 pt grey rule at its left; the shown round's own section one size up, 14/21, beside a 3 pt rule in the mark's colour (45% on a closed card): "now" by type, not by a box (POLISH §3 row 8, §6 decision 5; a round that is only a tag on an earlier section does not make it "now"); never LaTeX. The paper's words are quieter than the advisor's (`Palette.paperText`).
- **The mark** is a tint of the comment's colour whatever its kind, so an underline on a card always means an edit: 36% (Dark Mode 30%; 40% until POLISH §6, 12) in the first section, and a faint 14% (16%; it was 20%) on the words `sel` marks in later ones, so the advisor sees where the mark sits now. The PDF keeps the kind.
- **Changed words** are underlined in green, `#16A34A` (Dark Mode `#4ADE80`), against the last section above that shows text (a deleted section is skipped, so a restored one compares with the text before the deletion): `WordDiff.changes` (M1's word LCS, punctuation split off). Words removed with nothing in their place leave a small green triangle. Words that only one side shows before the first word the two share, or after the last, are the shown extent, not an edit, and are not marked, unless they are inside that side's mark (the student cut the end of a marked paragraph: a caret). The shown round's attached changes are marked wherever they are, by their own words (`changes[].context.now.sel`, carried into the section's text through the word alignment, `WordDiff.carried`): a sentence the student added before the commented one is underlined. A move, or a change whose words read the same before and after, marks nothing (`CardContent.edits`): an "R3 · moved" was underlined whole as if rewritten. Both the underlines and the triangles follow the panel's toggle.

**The student's note** (2026-10-18, the advisor's decision; CONTRACT amendment "Student notes"). A student explains a change, or why something was left as it is, with a comment line in the .tex, `% R1-03 …`; the engine reads the lines added or edited in a round at its ingest and stores the round's note on the comment (`student_note`: text, file, line, the changes it attached and its lines). It is the student's turn of that round, the words before the evidence, as a commit message before its diff (CARD §3.5–3.6). The words are in the grey bubble yours are in (`WordsBubble`; radius 8, 8 × 12 inside, SF 13/18, selectable), exactly as written, with no quotation marks added; its footer, right-aligned, 11 pt secondary (tertiary is under 3:1 on the bubble), has the note sign (`bubble.left`, `StudentNoteSign.symbol`) and where the note is, the file's name and line, `rate_by_hour_panels.tex:12`, the full path in the help tag: lines in a row as a range, `main.tex:103–104`; two places in one file, `main.tex:56, 60`; more, the first and how many others, `main.tex:56 +2`, the help tag listing each (`CardContent.notePlace`, from `student_note.lines`). A bare `% R1-03` beside an edit has no bubble: the sign and its place on one 11 pt secondary line, the help tag saying "A comment line, % R1-03, next to this edit points at it."; one with no edit next to it says nothing and is not shown. The edits a note attached are the comment's attached changes. Those in the commented text (which the engine also attached by the anchor) count as the engine's signal above (a round with one is a section, "revised") and are in the shown round's count and its underlines. Those outside it (`elsewhere`: a new paragraph in the introduction that answers R1-03, say) revise nothing there (review fixes 2026-10-18: a section of an unchanged sentence read "revised" and had stray words underlined): they make no section, no "revised" or "new image file", and their words are not marked in its text, which the turns above it show as they were. The turn says "edited elsewhere · 1 edit" with a green dot, and in the shown round each such edit follows the words as the Changes tab shows it (CARD §3.7, BACKLOG O32: the card had only "· 1 edit", not the sentence the student added; `ElsewhereAttachment`): its kind chip (`KindTag`), its section, cut at its end, and its page, then the shown round's block of `ChangeBlockView` without its label, at this round's size beside a 3 pt rule in the comment's colour when the commented text printed nothing new, 13/20 beside the grey rule when it did, its words underlined green as on that tab; a 1 pt frame (`Palette.rule`), radius 8, 7 × 10 inside, the rows' light fill under the pointer, the whole frame a button that shows the edit in the PDF; two at most, then "+1 more edit", a link that shows the next. An earlier round's edit elsewhere has no frame (the state has no context for that round's edits), only its count and page. The note's own words never decide a turn, a dot or an underline. Earlier rounds' notes stay in their turns, read only, also in an earlier round's view. The row's sign (§2) is for the shown round's note only.

**Notes not used** (the engine's `note_warnings`: an unknown ID, a comment already closed, an ID not in the form R1-03, a note in a file the build does not read, a note in a .bib, .sty or .cls file; an ID that the advisor made later in the round says "There was no comment R2-01 yet when this source was read"): never dropped silently, and never the advisor's move. One calm line at the top of the Comments tab, under the tabs (under a closed round's read-only box), not in Focus: the note sign (`bubble.left`), "2 student notes were ignored" (12.5 pt, secondary) and **Details…** (a link; `NoteWarningsLine`), whose popover, "Student notes ignored in Round 3", says "These lines in the source matched no open comment." (in an open round "Tell the student in a follow-up if one matters."; BACKLOG O32: "Lines such as % R1-03 …" set a line of source in the text's own font; the lines themselves are in monospace below, `NoteWarningsList.intro`) and lists each: `main.tex:20` (11 pt monospace), the line as the student wrote it (12 pt monospace) and the engine's sentence ("There is no comment R1-09, so this note is ignored."), selectable; the list shows about four whole and scrolls beyond (at most 440 pt; integration 2026-10-18: at 340 pt a fourth note's sentence was cut at the edge with no sign of more); in the label colours, since the popover of a window that is not key is a lighter grey on which the secondary was faint in Dark Mode. No amber, no count on a tab, nothing in the Finish sheet.

**Length.** A section shows up to 480 characters. Longer text shows the marked span (or `cut`) with 160 characters on each side, snapped out to word boundaries, with `…` at each cut. A marked span over 400 characters shows its first 240 and last 160 characters, joined by ` … `. A cut never falls inside a changed (underlined) run: the piece takes the whole run, out to its words, and two pieces that then meet are one (BACKLOG O32: "varying the retrieval … B.3)" cut a rewritten clause in two, so one edit read as two). "Show all" expands the section and resets when the card collapses. The engine caps `text` at 1200 characters.

**Bubbles** (CARD §1.3). Only earlier rounds' bubbles have a limit, your earlier follow-ups and the student's earlier notes, which you read again as a reminder: up to 320 characters (a CJK one counts twice, as in the title's rule) they show whole; past that, the whole sentences within 200 (the title's sentence rule) and "Show all · 3 more sentences" (Show all a link, the count secondary); a first sentence longer than that is cut at the last word within it (in CJK, at a character) with "…" and "Show all". Show all becomes Show less, and both reset when the card collapses (`EditState.showAll`). A pure rule on characters (`CardContent.clamped`), so no view measures text for it and it does not change with the panel's width. The title (§2, never cut, however long: its last sentences are usually the request), this round's words (the student's, which often end with the question for you, and yours), the field and the paper's text (above) have no such limit, and in Focus nothing is cut (§20). VoiceOver reads the whole words.

**Folding** (CARD §3.8). Past 5 turns (quiet lines and the field not counted) the turns between the first and the last 3 fold into one line, at least 2, the quiet lines among them with them: › in a dot's place, "R2 · Ouyang Mingxuan revised, you followed up" (the rounds Semibold in the label colour, the turns it holds Regular in the secondary; with a long name it wraps under itself, › beside its first line, so no verb it lists is cut, as a turn's label; visual review 2026-10-19) and "Show 2" on the right (secondary, not the link colour). The whole line is the button (`FoldLine`), with the rows' light fill under the pointer; a click opens the turns in place, the line then reading ⌄ and Hide; the card collapsing folds them again. One level only. The first turn never folds; while the others do, its text is cut closer, to 80 characters each side of the mark, snapped to words as the Length rule does (none on a side with fewer), so the line under it stays near. Underlines still compare with the last text above, folded or not ("revised" on the fold's line says there was a version in between). In Focus nothing folds (§20). Until UI v2.2: past 4 sections the earliest folded, leaving the last 3 ("R1–R2 · as commented, revised, 1 follow-up"), which hid the text you had marked.

**Fallback.** With no context (an old ledger, or an engine failure), the shown round shows the anchor's PDF `text`, all of it marked; failing that, the source quote in monospace. The first section shows the creation round's LaTeX quote only when no round came since.

## 4. Crops in the thread
**When.** A section is a crop when the comment is a box or its round's `context.display == "image"`: box comments, display math, `tabular`, TikZ/pgf, algorithms, and figure content outside `\caption`. Captions stay text, and text never gets a screenshot.

**Look** (`CropLook`; the advisor's feedback of 2026-10-20: a formula or a table on a white box read as a screenshot of it, and only figures need a picture). **A figure keeps its paper**: the area as printed, on white, in a hairline frame, in either mode. **Display math, a table, an algorithm or a box on words is the page's ink on the card**, with no paper and no frame, and no backing of its own (no plate, fill, border or shadow; the advisor's feedback of 2026-10-21: a box's tint behind a whole formula or table read as a separate background, unlike the rest of the UI): it sits on the card's surface as the card's words do, and only a mark's tint under the words it marks is drawn there (**The mark**, below). It is still that round's PDF, so the paper's macros and fonts are exactly what was printed (typesetting the LaTeX again would need a math engine, a dependency, and would not be what the student's PDF prints). Which: a comment by the environment its anchor is in (`comments[].env`, without `*`): `figure`, `wrapfigure`, `subfigure`, `subfloat`, `sidewaysfigure`, `tikzpicture`, `pgfpicture`, `picture`, `overpic`, and `center` or `minipage` round an image display (a range in a table is in its `tabular`) are figures, any other environment is ink; without one, a box on no words (a picture drawn at shipout, an area of the page) is a figure, a box on words or a range in a `\[…\]` display ink. A change: a replaced picture file (`figure`), an edit in a figure (`float` "Figure 4", "Fig. 2"), an `\input` of one (`about` "Figure 'plot' removed") or LaTeX that draws one (`\includegraphics`, `tikzpicture`, `figure`, `subfigure`, `wrapfigure` in its old or new text) is a figure; a table (`float` "Table 2", `about` "Table 'results' added") and anything else ink. A note on an empty area of the page is that area as printed. **The ink** (`InkMap`): the area is drawn on white, then the paper is taken out ("colour to alpha": each pixel becomes the least coverage of an ink that, laid on white, gives its colour), so black text is opaque black, white cells and a white box behind a formula vanish, a grey row is a faint black and a pink a fifth of red. In Light Mode that is the page's own ink. In Dark Mode each ink's HSL lightness is turned round with its hue and chroma kept (every channel moved by 1 − max − min), then multiplied by the panel's label colour (#E6EBE6): black text becomes the card's words' colour, red stays red, a dark blue becomes light blue, a grey row a faint light band. **The mark** (UI audit 2026-10-21: an outline on an ink crop crossed the next line's glyphs and read as a strike-through): on a figure, an outline in the comment's ink, after the page; on an ink crop, a **tint under the ink** (drawn with destination-over, so it never covers a glyph), the comment's tint as its marked words have it on a card (`MarkColor.tint`: 36%, 30% in Dark Mode; a box's the faint 14%, 16%, as it is the whole area, and **none when the box is most of its crop**: more than half its area, as a box round a whole formula or table (86–93% in the papers tried), `CropCache.picksOut`; a box on a symbol or a few words of a wider crop keeps it), fainter still in a later round's section (`faint`, × 0.45); a change's area in its colour (the changes' green at 18%, 22%, or slate teal at 16%, 22%: `ChangeModel.inkTint`); a deletion point in the changes' green, lighter in Dark Mode (#4ADE80). **Lines** (the outline, the deletion line, its dash and caret) are as thick on screen whatever the crop's scale: 1.5 pt (1 pt) ÷ the scale it is shown at, in page points (`CropStyle.line`; the audit: a hairline on a shrunk table, a heavy frame on an enlarged formula).

**Rendering.** **Source**: the app crops that round's build PDF (`rounds[].pdf`), read-only and off the main thread, with `PDFPage.draw` into a bitmap. **Density**: each crop is drawn at the width it is shown at, from the page's vectors (`CropStyle.density`: shown width ÷ area width × the screen's scale, rounded up to half a pixel a point, at least 1, at most 8 and 4096 pixels along its longer side), so it is sharp on a wide card and on a Retina screen, and drawn again when the card is wider or the window moves to another screen; a few points of width draw nothing again. Until the width is known (the first layout) and while a new density is drawn, the last image of that crop and look is shown (`CropCache.any`), so nothing flashes. **Cache**: an `NSCache` of at most 96 MB of bitmaps, keyed by round, PDF, page, rects, clip, text extent, deletion, faint, look, appearance (an ink crop only), the mark's colour and tint (a comment can be recoloured), density and the screen's scale; the latest of each crop and look in a second one of 32 MB. A crop view's own last image stands in only for the same crop in the same look (another density is fine): after a re-anchor or a switch of appearance the old area or the other mode's ink is never shown, and a crop that cannot be drawn says "R… PDF not available" (the code audit: a stale image stayed for good). **Crop area**: the rect union plus 6 pt, widened to the column (`text_x`), or the engine's clip. **Display, a figure**: from the left, at most the panel's width and 112 pt tall in the shown round's own turn, 64 pt in the others (UI v2.2: the shown round only a quiet line, the earlier crop above it stays 64 pt; until then it grew to 112), never letterboxed; the outline solid in the first section and at 40% later (`CropRequest.faint`); the Changes tab's at most 160 pt tall. **Display, ink** (UI audit 2026-10-21: a column-wide table squeezed into 64 or 112 pt had 4 pt glyphs, and one formula was 1.6× larger in the shown round than the round before): at its print size up to 1.25×, as wide as the card lets it, **at one scale for every ink crop of a card** (`InkCropLayout`: the card's width over the widest ink area of the card, at most 1.25; a change's crops the same, `ChangeModel.inkWidest`), so a formula is the same size in each round and a narrower one is not blown up; one taller than 220 pt as printed shows its first 112 pt, clipped, with **Show all** / **Show less** under it (the thread's own fold, `showAll` keyed by the section's id + ".crop", a change's by its block's). A table wider than the card is still scaled to the card's width (a column-wide table on a 330 pt card at about 0.7×); the click shows it at 2×.

**Behaviour.** **Placeholder**: it has the final size (known from the rect), so nothing moves when the image arrives; an ink crop's is bare (a figure's a faint grey), so no plate flashes behind a formula. A missing PDF shows "R1 PDF not available". **Sections**: a round whose picture file changed is a section of its own, the student's turn "replaced the image" (§3); a round with the same image is a quiet line. **Clicks**: any crop opens at 2× in a popover, drawn for that size, **as printed**, on its paper with the mark in its ink: the original of an ink crop (help: "Click to see it larger, as printed"; a figure's: "Click to see it larger"); the Changes tab's shown-round crop shows the edit in the PDF instead; `p.5` reveals the mark. A hover switch to the printed look in the crop's corner was tried and left out: it covered the formula's corner (often its number) and could not be checked without a real pointer.

## 5. Mark styles
**Palette.** A colour is picked per comment. The default is the last one used, and yellow at first. **Fill** (highlights) is drawn at alpha 0.35. PDFKit composites annotations over the page, so this is normal blending; it matches final.pdf. **Ink** (underline, strikeout, boxes, sticky notes) is drawn at full opacity. **Measurements** are on white paper. *grey* is the share of ink in grey print. *text* is the contrast of black text under the fill.

| # | name | fill | grey | text | ink | ink vs white |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | yellow | `#EAB308` | 22% | 8.0:1 | `#A16207` | 4.9:1 |
| 2 | teal | `#2DD4BF` | 22% | 8.0:1 | `#0F766E` | 5.5:1 |
| 3 | blue | `#60A5FA` | 27% | 8.5:1 | `#1D4ED8` | 6.7:1 |
| 4 | pink | `#F472B6` | 30% | 8.5:1 | `#BE185D` | 6.0:1 |
| 5 | purple | `#A78BFA` | 29% | 8.6:1 | `#6D28D9` | 7.1:1 |
| 6 | orange | `#FB923C` | 26% | 8.3:1 | `#C2410C` | 5.2:1 |

The second colour was green (`#4ADE80`/`#15803D`) until the M2a review: it hid the green change bands and changed-word underlines under a green mark, and its ink was the Verified chip's colour, so green is kept for changes (and, since design/ui-v2, only for them: Resolved is neutral, §2). A ledger's `green` reads as teal.

**Kinds** (page space, y up; `r` is a line rect): **highlight**: the rects in fill. **underline**: a 1.2 pt ink line at `r.minY + 0.15·r.height`, clear of the change band below the rect. **strikeout**: a 1.2 pt ink line at `r.minY + 0.45·r.height`. **note**: a sticky note at a point, drawn as a 9×9 rounded square in fill with a 1 pt ink border. A legacy note on text is a dotted ink underline. **box**: a 1.5 pt ink outline (radius 2) with fill at alpha 0.10. **gone**: M1's caret, in ink.

**Status** shows on the card chip (§2), and on the PDF id label as a glyph before the id (`+` New, `○` To check, `↩` Follow-up, `✓` Resolved, `–` Kept as is; §2); the label is filled with the comment's own ink, because status colours next to marks of the same hue were read as mark colours (M2a review). **Closed this round** (Resolved or Kept as is): the mark is drawn at 35% of its alpha and the label as an outline on the white page, its id in the label's own colour (§25, `MarkAnnotation.labelStyle`; until 2026-10-20 the label was at 50%), so open marks stand out (fills 7–11% grey, inks 40–48%). **Label type** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §3, 2026-10-17): "○ R1-02" in 8.5 pt Semibold with tabular digits, 3 pt either side, radius 2 (M1's was 7 pt Bold). **Placement** (`Geometry.labelPlace`): in the page margin on the mark's side, level with its first line, never over text, a margin ruler's numbers or another label: outside all of the page's text, else between the body text and the ruler, else past the ruler at the page's edge, each tried at four sizes in turn (`MarkAnnotation.labelFonts`): 8.5 pt; where that does not fit, M1's 7 pt Bold "○ R1-02", still with its status glyph and a thin space after it (a ruler 45 pt from the text, as in ACL review mode, where an 8.5 pt label does not fit; without the space "○R1-02" read as "OR1-02"); 6 pt with the glyph; and 6 pt without it only where nothing else fits (a ruler close to the text, as in ICML submissions and ACM review mode); a second label on the same line that has no room beside the first goes a line below or above it. **New this round**: the mark at full strength. **Selected** (POLISH §2.2, 2026-10-18): one 2 pt accent contour round the union of its lines, 1–3 pt outside them, with the paper's white in the 1 pt between it and the lines (so the outline never touches a mark, a label or the blue of a selection), the lines meeting halfway between them and the edges stepping at their ends (`SelectionOutline.contour`; a mark that goes on in the next column, below a figure, or from the end of one line to the start of the next without overlapping it gets one contour per run; the same round a point note's square), and the same ring round its label; a point note whose label sits beside or over its square (a note in the margin) gets one ring round both, as the two rings would touch (BACKLOG O32: the square's ring was drawn across the label's sign and first letters) (1 pt white, 2 pt accent; it was a 1.5 pt contour and a 1 pt ring). The white covers the top of a change band under the mark's last line while it is selected (the band is drawn below the line, §16; the card underlines those words), drawn over the page in a view of its own like the flash (§6), as shape layers with round joins, so selecting redraws no page. It replaces M1's box round each line.

**Changes** stay as in M1: a `#86EFAC` band when attached and a slate teal `#355F6C` band at 70% when unrequested (purple `#7C3AED` until 2026-10-16, §16), both on the toolbar toggle. A deletion's × is now 1.6 pt, ±4 pt, in `#15803D`; M1's was too faint.

## 6. Selection between the PDF and the panel
**Click on a mark.** A left click that moves less than 3 pt (the tracker's no-drag branch) clears the text selection, then hit-tests the marks at mouse-up. A drag always selects text, never a mark. A double click on a mark is one click: its second click (within the double-click time, 5 pt from the first) is not passed to the PDF view, whose word selection brought up the floating Comment toolbar over the text and dropped the choice row (interaction review 2026-10-18).

**Hit areas** (page space), each at least 8×8 view points: a label's rect + 2 pt; each line rect of a text mark + 2 pt; 12×12 around a sticky note's point; 10×14 around a gone caret; a whole box + 3 pt.

**Order of hits:** a label wins (labels never overlap); then text, sticky and caret marks, before boxes; within a group, the smaller area first, then state order.

**Cycling.** If the selected comment is among the hits, the next hit is selected (wrapping), so repeated clicks cycle through overlapping marks. Faded closed marks are clickable.

**Miss.** If no comment is hit, a change band (`minY−4…minY+1`) or × selects that change; on the Comments tab it also switches to the Changes tab (§16, which also gives the order of hits there). Otherwise the card selection stays.

**Cursor.** A pointing hand over labels; an I-beam over text marks, since the text stays selectable.

**After a hit**, the comment is selected without moving the PDF, the Comments tab shows, the card expands, the panel scrolls (below), and the mark flashes. In Focus the panel shows that card alone (§20).

**Panel scroll.** One `onGeometryChange` on the expanded card gives its frame.
- **Goal**: the card fully visible, with 16 pt margins. If it already is, the panel does not move.
- **J/K, a PDF click, `--select` and creation**: centre the card. A card taller than the viewport minus 32 has its top placed 16 pt below the panel top.
- **A row click**: scroll the least amount, top first (`scrollRowToVisible`), so the row under the pointer stays put.
- **A card first in its group** is placed with its group's header, as if the header were its top (`PanelTable.frame(of:header:)`; not in the slot mode below): a card placed 16 pt under the floating header leaves its own header in view, not half under the other (behaviour review 2026-10-19: "For the student" peeked out from under "To check").
- **R, Return, or Follow up with the field empty** (§8; `ScrollMode.end`): the cursor goes into the follow-up field, at the card's end, so a card taller than the panel moves the least that shows its end, the field above the answer row (behaviour review 2026-10-19: the cursor went into a field several hundred points below the view); another card as for a row click.
- **An answer moving on** (§8; `ScrollMode.slot`, POLISH §6): the new card's top goes where the answered card's top was (under the floating header when that was above the view; not when the answered card was below it), the answered card leaves for its group with the rows above kept in place (`PanelTable.show`), and then the least movement, as for a row click, shows the new card whole. A next card earlier in the list (wrapping) is brought to that place, as far as the list's top allows.
- **Focus** (§20): the panel shows the selected card alone at its top, so a selection scrolls nothing; when Focus ends, the whole list comes back with the card where it was on screen, and the panel centres it (or the change).
- **Switching tabs** keeps each list where it was (§1 Panel header): neither list scrolls.
- **Animation**: ease-out over 0.2 s. Each step stays within the content as it is then, a new scroll replaces one still running, rows that come or go above shift it with them (`ScrollAnimator.shift`), and Focus, which shows the one card at the top, ends it (`stop`): Focus begun while the scroll to the card was running (the demo's `card R2-07; focus on`) went on scrolling the one-card list past its end, under the To check header, which then covered the card's top (BACKLOG O32). A jump of more than 2 viewports (rows × 50 pt) is instant, because animating across unrealised lazy rows stutters. With Reduce Motion, every scroll is instant.
- **Expand and collapse**: one transaction, no height animation, before the scroll.
- **A new engine state** (after V, an edit, …): the selection is kept and the panel never scrolls. The exceptions: a newly created comment is centred, and an answer, from a key or a click, selects the next card to check in the answered card's place (above, §8).
- **Rows that come or go** (a card the selection left moves to its group, Show or Hide, a new comment): the expanded card keeps its place on screen when it is in view, else the first listed item in view that stays (§1).

**PDF scroll** (selection from the panel or J/K): **When**: only if the mark is outside the view inset by 30 pt. The mark then lands a third of the way down (M1). **How**: animated over 0.25 s with the clip view's animator when the distance is at most 1.5 view heights; otherwise `go(to:)`. **Flash**: then 2 pulses of a 2 pt accent outline, 0.15 s on and 0.15 s off. With Reduce Motion, a static outline for 0.6 s. **J/K**: the outline, scroll and flash follow one turn of the run loop after the new card (§10.10). **Manual scroll**: scrolling the PDF by hand never changes the selection.

## 7. Creating
**Floating toolbar.**
- **When**: 120 ms after mouse-up, without animation, whenever an open round's PDF has a text selection (double and triple clicks included) or a pending box.
- **What**: a subview of the PDFView (not a window), on the floats' opaque colour (`Palette.float`, its hairline `Palette.floatLine`, as Jump; POLISH §2.4: its popover material blurred the scrolling page; visual review 2026-10-18), radius 8, 30 pt tall.
- **Contents**: 6 swatches (14 pt; the current one has a 2 pt ring); the kinds `highlighter`, `underline` and `strikethrough` (the current one in the accent's text colour, `Palette.accentText`, on a 15% accent fill: the Sage accent itself is too dark on the toolbar in Dark Mode, §19); [Comment] in the accent colour (its help tag names Return; no key glyph on it since POLISH §6).
- **Placement**: centred 8 pt above the first selected line, or 8 pt below the last line when less than 38 pt is free above. It stays 8 pt inside the view.
- **Hides**: when the selection clears; on scroll or zoom (Return still works); when the editor opens; on Esc. A find in the paper (the find over the PDF, Jump's last row, ⌘G; §32) shows it again once its scroll has settled, 0.4 s after (interaction review 2026-10-18: a match that needed a scroll was left with no Comment).
- **Clicks**: a swatch or kind click sets the pending style and saves it as last used (UserDefaults `markColor`, `markKind`).

**Editor.** Return, Comment, or right-click › Comment… (§35) opens the M1 popover with the pending style. It gains a style row (swatches and kinds), which has the last word on style. Its title says where, "New comment · p.7" (the page secondary), and under it the words selected in the PDF, in the paper's serif (New York 12.5, the second label colour) beside a 2 pt rule in the comment's colour, which follows the swatches; at most three lines, cut in the middle (POLISH §3 row 4; it was one secondary line in quotation marks). The note may be empty (its caption says so). ⌘↩ saves and Esc cancels, named in the help tags of Save and Cancel; M1's draft rules are unchanged; "Discard this note?" is in primary text (red is for errors). After the save, the new card is selected and centred. **A note being written is never left behind** (audit, 2026-10-17): while the popover holds text that is not saved, picking another paper or round, Finish Round…, and Comment on it… for another change do nothing but say so over the PDF, "Your new comment on p.7 is not saved · click Save or Cancel in it first" (mouse first, POLISH §6; it named ⌘↩) (they beeped, and Comment on it… first moved the selection and the PDF to the other change); an untouched popover just closes. Save goes to the paper and round the note was begun on, or refuses with an alert, never to another paper shown meanwhile. Closing the window asks first, as quitting does (§8).

**Box.** **Drawing**: ⌥-drag draws a rubber band (1 pt dashed accent outline, 8% fill), kept to the start page, with a crosshair cursor while ⌥ is held. **Trackpads**: B or Comment › Draw a Box… arms box mode for one plain drag; Esc disarms it. **Pending box**: a box of at least 8×8 pt becomes pending (dashed outline, toolbar without kinds). Return opens the editor with kind box, and a plain click elsewhere drops it.

**Sticky note.** Right-click › Add Note Here (M1; §35), or ⌥-click without a drag. A box under 8 pt counts as a ⌥-click.

## 8. Editing
Only in an open round.

| | new comment | carried comment |
|---|---|---|
| note | inline | no: it was sent to the student. Edit Note is disabled with help "Sent in R1 — write a follow-up in the card's field instead" |
| follow-up, closing remark | — | the follow-up field; this round's words, editable until the round is finished |
| colour | yes | yes |
| kind | yes (text kinds only; a box stays a box) | only by re-anchoring |
| re-anchor | yes | yes |
| delete | yes, after confirmation | no |

**Inline note edit.** **Opening**: click the expanded card's note, press Return (card selected, no PDF selection), or choose Edit Note. The editor uses the same font and grows from 3 to 12 lines. **Buttons** (POLISH §6, mouse first; they replace the caption "⌘↩ saves · Esc cancels"): Cancel and Save under it, as links on the right, their help tags naming Esc and ⌘↩; a click on them leaves the cursor in the editor, so Cancel never saves through focus loss. **Keys**: ⌘↩ saves. Esc cancels; if the text changed, the first Cancel or Esc shows "Your changes are not saved." and turns Cancel into Discard changes, and the second one discards. While an IME has marked text, Esc and ⌘↩ go to the IME. **Focus loss**: clicking another card, the PDF or the toolbar commits the edit, as Finder rename and the Xcode inspector do. An unchanged note sends nothing. **Saving**: the editor shows "Saving…" in place of the buttons. If the engine refuses, it stays open with the text, and its card is selected again. **Quitting** saves it first, as focus loss does, and quits once the engine has it (it stays open, and the app with it, when the engine refuses). "Esc again to discard" is in primary text (red is for errors).

**Colour and kind.** **Opening**: a click on a new comment's mark in the PDF (its toolbar, §32), the card's colour dot (any comment of the open round), `⋯` › Colour and `⋯` › Kind (the card's style button went into `⋯`, POLISH §3 row 8), or keys 1–6 on the selected card. **Contents**: the colours (checkmark and key number); the kinds (checkmark). **Effect**: a change is sent at once, and ⌘Z undoes it (below).

**Answers: Resolve, Follow up, Keep as is** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §1.4, §2 and §6, 2026-10-17; they replace Verify, Reopen and Won't fix and the "Clear the reopen?" dialog). The keys and the ledger values stay: V `verified`, R `reopened`, W `wontfix`. What each key or button does is the pure function `VerdictPlan`.
- **Resolve** (V): the student dealt with it and the advisor is satisfied: they fixed it, deleted the passage, or gave a reason the advisor accepts. **Keep as is** (W, the quieter button): the student did not deal with it and the advisor no longer requires it (on rereading the original is fine, or a change of mind). Both close the comment and fade its mark; they differ only in the record and in what the student reads (resolved, no change needed). The buttons' help tags say this.
- **Follow up** (R): not done yet. The comment stays open, and the follow-up goes out in the round's final.pdf.
- **The follow-up field** is always there on a carried comment without an answer in an open round, at the end of the conversation, where the rail ends, with no dot (§3), one line high and growing to 8, with the placeholder "Write a follow-up for the student…" (no "R" in it since POLISH §6, and no key caption under it while it has the cursor; Follow up's help tag names ⌘↩). Only R, Return or a click puts the cursor in it; selecting a card (J/K, a click on a row or a mark) never does, so V, R, W, J and K stay single keys. In it: ⌘↩ sends (Follow up, the text as the verdict note; an empty field follows up without a note), Return adds a line, Esc leaves with the text kept, and a click elsewhere keeps it too. While an input method has marked text, Esc and ⌘↩ go to it. Each card has a field of its own, so neither the cursor nor typing undo crosses to the next card.
- **Buttons stay in their places.** With text in the field, Follow up becomes the default button where it stands (⌘↩ sends) and the other two read "Resolve + note" and "Keep as is + note", with the caption "A new request belongs in Follow up"; a click on either closes at once with the text as the closing remark.
- **Unsent drafts.** Text left in the field is an unsent draft: the field shows an amber "unsent draft" tag, "kept on this Mac" and, on the right, Discard (POLISH §3 row 8), and the card's row the tag. It is never sent silently and never lost: it is written at every change to a small file per paper in `~/Library/Application Support/Paperole/drafts` (Paperole Dev: `~/Library/Application Support/Paperole Dev/drafts`; by round and comment id; `DraftStore`), never to the ledger or the student's folder, so a crash or a forced quit keeps it.
