It is deleted when sent or discarded (also from the Finish sheet, which lists each one with Send as follow-up and Discard, and an Undo for a discard there, §17), when the round is finished, and once a later round is open. Quitting with unsent drafts asks once ("You have 2 unsent drafts", with the ids and papers) [Quit] [Cancel]; with a new comment's note not saved in the popover (§7) it asks "Your new comment on p.7 is not saved", "Quitting discards its note. Click Cancel, then Save in the comment.", and names the drafts after it ("…nothing goes to the student until you send it."; both named ⌘↩ before POLISH §6). An inline editor (a note, this round's words) is saved before the question, so it is never asked about. A `--background` run never asks. Test runs keep drafts elsewhere (§13). **Closing the window** quits the app (it is the last window), and goes the same way (POLISH §0): its close button and File › Close ask as quitting does, before anything closes, and Cancel leaves the window and the popover's note as they were (`QuitOnClose`, the window's delegate in front of SwiftUI's; the close button calls it directly, because a new-comment popover holding a note vetoed the window's own close silently, before the delegate was asked). It used to close the window first, which closed the popover and lost its note before anything asked.
- **A bare V or W on text** never turns it into a closing remark. On a card with an unsent draft, or with a follow-up sent this round, it shows the choice row in place of the buttons: "Close R1-02 with your draft as a closing remark?" (or "your follow-up"), [Resolve + note] [Resolve without it] Cancel, with the keys in their help tags (Return, ⌫, Esc; Cancel's says ⌘↩ sends the draft as a follow-up instead, as Cancel and then Follow up do; no key glyphs or key caption since POLISH §6). Return closes with the text as the closing remark; ⌫ closes without it (the text is dropped; ⌘Z brings it back); Esc changes nothing; ⌘↩ sends the draft as the follow-up instead (on a follow-up already sent it changes nothing). A click on Resolve on a follow-up card asks the same way. From the keyboard, "Resolve + note" is Esc, V, Return. The row answers only the open round's card it is on: a PDF selection made before it is cleared when it shows, and it goes when a PDF selection or box is made, re-anchoring starts, or another round is shown (interaction review 2026-10-17: its Return closed the open round's comment from an earlier round, placed no anchor, and opened no comment editor).
- **The same key again clears**, without a dialog; the follow-up or closing remark returns to the field as an unsent draft (R puts the cursor there). On a Resolved or Kept-as-is card the other closing key switches and keeps the remark; R clears it and puts the remark in the field.
- **This round's words** (a follow-up sent, or a closing remark): Return on the answered card, or Edit, opens them in place, with Cancel and Save under them as under a note. Return adds a line, ⌘↩ saves, Esc cancels at once, and a click elsewhere saves. An answered card without words gets an empty editor, to add a closing remark. A click on an answer button while the editor is open saves the words first and then answers from them (Resolve on a Resolved card then puts the edited words in the field); words left in an editor whose card was cleared meanwhile become its unsent draft. Quitting saves the editor first, as a click elsewhere does.
- **Moving on** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §6 decision 3; POLISH §6, 2026-10-18, which replaces "a click never moves on"): any answer that gives a verdict, from a key or a click alike (V, W, ⌘↩ on the card or in the field, Resolve, Follow up with text, Keep as is, the "+ note" buttons, the choice row's buttons or its Return, ⌫ or ⌘↩), selects the next card to check once the engine has it: the next one below in To check, else the first (wrapping). The new card takes the answered card's place on screen (§6), so nothing jumps, and the answered card moves to its group (§1). There is no Next button; a click on any row selects it as before. **A double click answers one card**: for 0.4 s after an answer moved on, the new card's answer buttons (and its draft's Discard) ignore clicks (`ClickGuard`): the second click of a double click lands on the card that took the answered card's place. A second click on the card while the engine is still answering it is ignored without a word (`AppModel.answerPending`, from the answer's request to the engine's reply; review 2026-10-18: it said "Still loading…", which invited one more click, and in Review that click answered the card that then took the answered card's place). A key then, or a click on anything else that needs the engine, gives nothing and says so, "Still loading… try again in a moment." (§11; O27, POLISH §7: the buttons were disabled then, and the keys did nothing without a word). Clearing an answer, R, and an answer the engine refused stay on the card; so do an answer when no other card is left to check, or once the selection has moved meanwhile, and the Finish sheet's Send as follow-up. When no other card takes its place (the last card to check, the selection moved meanwhile, an answer cleared), the answered card's own buttons ignore clicks for the same 0.4 s: the engine answers in under 70 ms, so a double click's second click found the button enabled and cleared the answer it had just given (interaction review 2026-10-18). Discard in the field does the same: the answer row moves up into the tag row's place, so a double click on Discard never closes the comment as well. **Focus** (§20) answers in place, never moving the PDF or the selection: `AppModel.answersAdvance` is false there, the one switch; ⌘Z there leaves the PDF where it is too. A follow-up is written in the card's field in Focus's panel and sent from there, staying on the card (POLISH §7; until then Focus had to be left for the field, and a `focusReturn` brought it back).
- **The toast.** An answer that gives a verdict shows "✓ R2-02 resolved" ("kept as is", "followed up"; POLISH §2.5's sign: ↩ before "Undone · …") with an **Undo** button (a link in the toast's text colour on a quiet capsule of its own, so it reads as a button; its help tag names ⌘Z; POLISH §6: the toast was not clickable; visual review 2026-10-18) for 2.5 s, as an opaque dark pill (a light one in Dark Mode; POLISH §2.4, it was 92%) at the bottom of the panel, raised above the answer row while that is pinned there (§2; as soon as it is pinned, with no scroll after it: the toast's place is worked out after the pin's, review 2026-10-18), and above the group's header that it would cover there (BACKLOG O28: Undo over "Closed this round", where the answered card had just gone; `ToastPlace.bottom`, set by the shown list's table), that one only: lifted again above each header it then covered, it could end far from the foot over a card's row (review 2026-10-18: 141 pt up, over R1-13); in Focus 12 pt under the one card, by the buttons just clicked (§20; review 2026-10-18: at the panel's foot it was some 550 pt below them; it was over the page while Focus had no panel); VoiceOver reads it. It also says "Still loading… try again in a moment." when an action waits for the engine (§11), without Undo. The pointer on Undo keeps it shown, and it goes 1.5 s after the pointer leaves, so the mouse can reach Undo. The rest of the toast takes no clicks: a click on its words reaches what is under it, a row or the card (integration, 2026-10-18: the whole pill took clicks, so in Focus, where it was over the page, a mark under it could not be clicked, and the pointer resting there to try kept the toast shown). Editing this round's words or clearing an answer shows none, and clearing takes the shown one away (its Undo would name an answer that no longer is); another paper or round takes it away too (its Undo acted on the paper shown, and reopened a comment the toast never named; interaction review 2026-10-18). Undo turns into Redo where it stands, so for 0.4 s after either the other ignores clicks: a double click undoes once.
- **Undo.** ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z (no editor focused) undo and redo the answers given in the open round, including the draft each one took or left, and the marks recoloured or given another kind (since 2026-10-26, §32: "Undone · R2-01 is yellow", "… is an underline"; a deleted comment's steps go with it): the app keeps the previous verdict and note and sends them to the engine again (the engine erases the note when a verdict is cleared, `comments.py` `set_verdict`). The card is selected, in the group of the answer it restores (§1), and the panel's toast says "Undone · R1-02 is To check" with a Redo button (help ⇧⌘Z) ("Redone · R1-02 is Resolved" with Undo; "Nothing to undo"). The toast's Undo and Redo do what ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z do. The history is per paper and round, in memory only: it is kept while another paper or an earlier round is shown, and dropped when the round is finished. Typing in a card's field drops that card's answer steps, so neither undo nor redo puts a step's draft over the text typed since.

**Re-anchor**, from `…` or the Re-anchor… link of a section (§3): an accent bar over the PDF reads "Re-anchoring R1-03: select the text or ⌥-drag a box · [Place] [Cancel]". The old mark is drawn dashed. The toolbar shows only [Place R1-03] (help: Return). Return or Place sends `reanchor`; Esc or Cancel leaves. On success, the new mark flashes and the card's last section shows the new place. On a refusal, the engine's message shows in red in the bar, and the mode stays.

A box sets kind box, and text on a box comment sets highlight. J/K and the verdict keys are off while re-anchoring.

**Delete**, from `…` › Delete…, the mark's toolbar (§32) or Delete/⌫ on a selected new comment. One panel-level dialog: "Delete R3-02?", "Its note will be lost. The ID will not be reused.", [Delete] (default, destructive) [Cancel] (Esc). Afterwards the next card is selected.

## 9. Keyboard map
While a text field or editor has focus, only its own keys act (Return adds a line in all of them). Text that is only selectable (a card's note, its paper text, a bubble, the Changes tab's text) does not hold the keys when a click makes it the first responder: ⌘C copies its selection, and the keys below act (`AppModel.routesKeys`; interaction review 2026-10-17, where a click there stopped every key). Contexts are checked top-down.

| context | keys |
|---|---|
| a new comment's note open | ⌘↩ save · Esc cancel (twice if changed) |
| this round's words open (§8) | ⌘↩ save · Esc cancel |
| cursor in the follow-up field | ⌘↩ send (Follow up) · Esc leave, the text kept as an unsent draft |
| cursor in the find's field (§32) | Return next match · ⇧Return previous · Esc clear the find, the keys back to the PDF |
| re-anchor mode | Return place · Esc leave |
| text selection or pending box, open round | Return editor · 1–6 colour · H/U/S kind (not for boxes) · Esc clear the selection |
| choice row shown, open round | Return close with the text · ⌫ close without it · Esc cancel · ⌘↩ send as a follow-up instead (each but Esc then moves to the next card to check, §8) · J/K leave the card (and the row) |
| a new comment's mark shows its toolbar (§32) | Esc puts the toolbar away (then the keys below) |
| always | J/K next/previous card in the list, skipping a folded group (or change, on the Changes tab) · ⌘Z / ⇧⌘Z undo / redo an answer or a mark's restyle (open round, not re-anchoring) |
| change selected, Changes tab, open round | V marks it checked and selects the next unrequested change still to check; again clears it · Return comments on it (§16) |
| card selected, open round | V Resolve · R the cursor into the follow-up field · W Keep as is; the same key again clears (§8) · ⌘↩ send the card's unsent draft · an answer from these keys moves to the next card to check (§8) · Return: a new comment's note, the follow-up field, or this round's words on an answered card · 1–6 recolour · Delete/⌫ delete a new comment |
| open round | B arm a box (in the PDF) · ⌥-drag box · ⌥-click sticky note |

N, C and Shift-C are the reading keys while reading marks are on (Settings, §18; off, they are not the app's keys and pass on as any other letter); F switches Focus on and off in every context but the choice row's (§20; named in View › Focus (F), as the Reading menu names N and C, not a menu key equivalent); ⌘K (View › Jump to…) opens Jump, ⌘F (Edit › Find in Paper…) puts the cursor in the toolbar's find, and ⌘G / ⇧⌘G (Edit › Find Next / Previous in Paper) go on with its query (§20, §32). In Focus, R and Return on a card (and editing a note or this round's words) put the cursor in the card's field or editor in Focus's panel, without leaving Focus (POLISH §7; they left Focus while it had no panel, §4.3). T stays reserved (tex diff). No button or field shows a key letter or key glyph (POLISH §6, mouse first: every action has a visible control, and the keys are not the design's focus); each button's help tag names its key, and the menus name theirs (`KeyHintTests` reads the sources for `Button`, `Label` and `Text` literals naming a key).

## 10. Performance rules for smooth scrolling
1. **List**: `ScrollView { LazyVStack(pinnedViews: .sectionHeaders) }` with `ForEach(cards, id: \.id)`. Stable ids keep the scroll position across state updates.
2. **Row height**: compact rows are a fixed 44 pt; only the expanded card varies.
3. **`CardModel`** (Equatable): the chip, words, note, the thread's sections with their `AttributedString`s, crop requests and flags, and (UI v2.2) its turns with their labels, the fold and the edits a note linked elsewhere. It is built once per applied state in `AppModel`, never in `body`, including diffs and trimming; 100 cards take under 10 ms.
4. **Card views** are `Equatable` and use `.equatable()`. Their inputs are the CardModel and `isExpanded`. Busy and editing state reach only the expanded card. Cards do not observe `AppModel`. Actions come from one stable `CardActions` in the environment.
5. **Separate state**: busy, progress, hasPDFSelection and toolbar state live outside the panel's observed state (a separate object, or `@Observable`), so they never re-evaluate the list.
6. **Geometry**: no per-card GeometryReader or preference key. There is one `onGeometryChange` on the expanded card and one on the viewport, and (2026-10-20, §4) one on each crop, which only an expanded card or the Changes tab's card has, for the width it is drawn at.
7. **Modifiers**: sheets, dialogs and popovers attach once, at panel level, keyed by id. Compact rows have no `.help` and no `textSelection`. The list has no `.animation()`.
8. **Crops** are rendered off the main thread at the density of the width they are shown at (§4), and cached.
9. **Budgets** (`--debug-scroll-perf`, 80 cards): a full scroll: p95 frame interval ≤ 1.25 × the refresh interval, and no frame over 50 ms after the first second; body evaluations: at most 2 per card and 2 for the list; a J step: at most 3 card bodies, with the new card laid out within 2 frames (`key_ms`, the key's own turn of the run loop, which can be measured with the display asleep).
10. **J/K** (2026-10-16, BACKLOG O1): the key's turn lays out and draws only the new card; the PDF part (the mark's outline, scroll and flash) runs on the next turn, and a held key moves the PDF once, to the last card. A new selection redraws no page: the selected mark's outline is a view over the page with shape layers, like the flash. The tab picker is measured only when it changes (`MeasuredOnce`): SwiftUI asked its NSSegmentedControl for its size up to 200 times a step, whenever anything in the panel moved.
11. **The thread** (2026-10-17): the expanded card is sized at its own height (`fixedSize` vertically), so its nested stacks size each part once rather than for each height the table's measure tries; the rail's dots and lines are overlays, not an HStack beside the text, which measured the section's text again for every width it tried (a J step went from 24 to 60 ms with it). Only the answer row observes `PinState`, so scrolling redraws nothing else. `scroll` p95 8.3 ms in both (the 120 Hz refresh).
12. **Groups** (2026-10-17): a J/K step that moves no card between groups costs what it did; one that does (the selection leaves a card answered with a click) removes and inserts only that card's rows (`PanelTable.show`), so the rows that stay keep their views: a `reloadData` moved every visible row's hosting view into a new row view, which rebuilt its SwiftUI graph. J and K never measure a folded group. A step that moves a card, timed with `key … ms` (the key's turn done at once): 28–59 ms (mean 48) against 30–57 ms (mean 46) for the same 14 steps without an answer, within the noise.
13. **The other screens** (2026-10-17, design/ui-v2 step 4): The status line's page number is the only view that follows the PDF's scrolling.
14. **After UI v2** (2026-10-17, the integration of steps 1–4, release build): The O1 figures before UI v2 (2026-10-16: p50 about 16–18 ms, p90 about 26–28 ms) were taken under conditions that were not recorded, and no build from before UI v2 is left to run beside this one. The steps' own interleaved before/after runs (background instances) found about +1.5–2 ms for step 1's follow-up field and no change for steps 2–4 (a step before step 2 was p50 22.6–22.9 ms there), so most of the gap is probably the measuring conditions, not UI v2. Either way the 2-frame budget of item 9 is not met at 120 Hz (8.3 ms a frame).
15. **After the review fixes** (2026-10-17, release build): Still over item 9's budget at 120 Hz; a fair check needs a foreground run with the display awake.
16. **The mouse-first answer flow and list** (UI v2.1, 2026-10-18, release build; the run lists For the student, as before it folded, so the steps are the same): Three more pairs on the final build (a row's hover cleared when it is expanded; load 1.9–2.3): p50 23.8–25.7 against 24.4–25.0 ms, p90 41.6–46.9 against 40.7–45.4 ms; over all 8 pairs the p50 difference has a median of −1.5 ms (from −3.8 to +2.1), and p90 nets to about zero (from −3.8 to +6.2 ms): pair to pair the noise is a few ms either way. 1 card body a step and no list body; row bodies after the first step as before (0–2 a run, rows made as the panel scrolls). The first step (no card expanded before it) draws every row once more, to dim it (16 row bodies; that step is left out of the percentiles). A help tag on the follow-up field added about 3 ms at p90 in a first build (5 pairs, p90 median 43.2 against 39.9 ms) and was left out: Follow up's help names ⌘↩. `scroll` could not be measured (the display asleep: its display link did not tick, in either build).
17. **The design language** (UI v2.1 phase 1, 2026-10-18, release build): The final build, 11 pairs: `key_ms` p50 21.0–27.5 ms (median 24.1) against 21.9–26.2 ms (24.4); the pair differences from −3.7 to +3.9 ms, median +0.4, mean 0.0; all 341 steps of each pooled: p50 24.5 against 24.3 ms, mean 27.1 against 27.1 ms, p90 46.5 against 44.1 ms (the runs' p90 medians 45.2 and 44.1). 1 card body a step, no list body, rows after the first step as before (0–2 a run). Two first builds, 13 pairs, ran about 1 ms higher at p50 (pair differences median +1.1): the note as two Texts (a first sentence and the rest, each selectable) and a chip with a fill under an outline; the title is one Text now (`NoteTitleView`), the chip one shape, the card's shadow the old one, and a dynamic colour of the palette makes its two colours once, not at every use (`NSColor(light:dark:)`). The larger type (a 15 pt title, the shown round's text at 14 pt) makes the expanded card taller, which the pooled p90 may show. `scroll` could not be measured (its display link did not tick, in either build).
18. **Layout and reading** (UI v2.1 phase 2, 2026-10-18, release build; POLISH §5: the section the PDF is in is not in the list's data). The sidebar's Contents rows each observe their own look (`CurrentSection`, `SectionLook`), so a new section redraws the row it leaves and the row it enters; the sidebar's list, its papers and the panel never redraw while the PDF scrolls, and the follower that keeps the row in view is the only other observer. J/K (`keys`, background runs interleaved with the phase-1 build, which shows the outline column; 8 pairs, load 1.9–3.8 on 8 cores): `key_ms` p50 19.2–25.8 ms (median 23.3) against 22.0–25.8 ms (24.3), the pair differences from −2.8 to +3.1 ms, median −1.6, mean −1.2; p90 30.4–42.4 ms (median 35.0) against 39.1–51.5 ms (44.6), lower in 7 of 8; 1 card body a step, no list body, rows as before (0–2 a run), and no sidebar or paper body; the steps' PDF moves redraw 41–45 Contents rows a run (two a section change), after the key's turn. `scroll` with the display link could not be measured (asleep); on the timer, which times each step's scroll, layout and drawing at once (`work_*_ms`, like `key_ms`), `Fixtures/state-many.json` (60 listed cards): p50 4.5–6.0 ms, p95 11.9–13.0 ms, no sidebar body (no earlier build has the timer, so there is no pair to compare).
19. **The other screens** (UI v2.1 phase 3, 2026-10-18, release build). The done card, the empty state and the hint are rows of the table measured at the column's width (`PanelTable` `.done`, `.empty`, `.hint`), listed only when they apply, so a J/K step never measures them; the first-run tip is observed by itself (`Tips`), and the perf runs dismiss it (for this run only), as it is after the first answer, so the steps compare with earlier builds'. The Changes tab (`--tab changes`, the card without its bar and its R3 text at 14 pt; 4 pairs, one under a load of 10): pooled p50 19.6 against 20.6 ms, p90 37.9 against 37.5 ms, pair differences median −1.9 ms, 1 card body a step. `scroll` on the timer (`Fixtures/state-many.json`, 3 pairs): work p50 5.6–5.7 against 5.4–5.8 ms, p95 13.0–13.5 against 12.4–14.3 ms, no card or list body; the display link did not tick (asleep).
20. **Focus and Jump** (UI v2.1 phase 4, 2026-10-18, release build; POLISH §5: in Focus the hidden panel does no work; Focus's bar and this cost were replaced by its one-card panel after the advisor's use, item 23). Focus takes the panel out of the window (not hidden: SwiftUI then tears its table down), so nothing observes the comment list; the bar observes its own `FocusBarModel` (set only while Focus is on) and the card's `EditState`; Jump observes `JumpModel` only. Review J/K (`keys`, background runs interleaved with the phase-3 build, 12 pairs, load 1.9–7.2 on 8 cores): a first build measured the inline editor on a layout of its own each time (below) and ran higher (6 pairs: pair differences of the run p50 median +1.8 ms); with its heights cached by width, 6 pairs: run p50 19.0–23.8 ms against 19.9–22.5 ms, pair differences from −2.9 to +3.9, median +0.45, mean +0.5; pooled (186 steps each) p50 22.4 against 22.0 ms, mean 23.6 against 23.7 ms, p90 34.1 against 33.0 ms; 1 card body a step, no list, bar, sidebar or paper body, rows as before (0–2 a run). `scroll` on the timer (`Fixtures/state-many.json`, 2 pairs): work p50 3.6 and 5.8 against 5.4 and 5.7 ms, p95 13.0 and 12.5 against 12.2 and 12.5 ms, no card or list body. **The inline editor's measure** (`InlineTextEditor.sizeThatFits`) resized the shown text's container while SwiftUI measured, which made its scroll view lay out again and ask SwiftUI to measure again; with the card's stacks trying several widths this looped for good (a background run froze once the panel came back after Focus with a draft on the card). It is now measured on a text layout of its own (`Coordinator.measure`), its heights kept by width until the text changes.
21. **The UI v2.1 integration** (2026-10-18, release build, against the 22:14 build of 2026-10-17 from before UI v2.1; background runs, display asleep). The first step from nothing selected draws the 16 rows once to dim them (left out of the percentiles), and the steps' PDF moves redraw 36–43 Contents rows a run, after the key's turn. Focus (`keys --focus`, 3 runs): p50 5.2–7.0 ms, p90 9.8–10.1 ms, 0 card bodies, 1 bar body a step. `scroll`: the 22:14 build waits for a display link that does not tick while the display sleeps (it has no timer), so it measures nothing; on the timer (`Fixtures/state-many.json`, 3 pairs against the phase-2 build, the first with the timer) work p50 4.7–5.7 against 5.2–6.1 ms, p95 12.1–12.7 against 11.8–13.1 ms, no card or list body. `pdf` (the sidebar, 2 runs): 802 steps, 37–40 section changes, 79–85 Contents row bodies, and no sidebar, paper, panel row, card or list body. The tabs are still measured once, and nothing stays over the page (the toast, the floating toolbar and Jump come and go). Item 9's frame budget still needs a foreground run with the display awake.
22. **After the UI v2.1 reviews** (2026-10-18, release build, against the integration's build of the same morning; background runs, display asleep). A first batch, before the answer row cached its buttons' sizes (`AnswerRow` measured each button for both its size and its placing), had pooled p90 38.2 against 34.0 ms. Focus (`keys --focus`, 3 runs): p50 5.2–5.9 ms, p90 8.9–10.3 ms, 0 card bodies, 1 bar body a step. Sidebar (`pdf`, 2 runs): 802 steps, 38–39 section changes, 80–82 Contents row bodies, no sidebar, paper, panel row, card or list body. Scroll, on the timer proxy (`Fixtures/state-many.json`, 3 pairs): work p50 5.6–6.2 against 4.1–5.7 ms, p95 12.0–12.9 against 12.3–14.0 ms, 43 row bodies each, no card or list body. The status line and Focus's bar are opaque now, which costs nothing on these paths; the two-frame budget (item 9) still needs a foreground run with the display awake.
23. **After the advisor's use** (POLISH §7, 2026-10-18, release build, background, display asleep; the advisor found J/K and scrolling fast enough, so no foreground run). Focus no longer has a bar: its panel is the comment list showing one row, so a J step there removes one row, inserts one (with its compact row, hidden under the card) and lays out the new card, as a Review step lays out a card. Focus (`--focus`) p50 24.0 / 26.0 ms, p90 37.8 / 36.5 ms, 1 card body, 1 row body and 1 list update a step (it was p50 5–6 ms with the bar, which drew no card). Dimming is now an opacity on each row, set when the first card is expanded (as the colour was), so a J step changes no row in Review; the group headers are 34 pt rows as before, with no new observer.
24. **After the review of the advisor's changes** (2026-10-18, release build, background runs, light and dark). "Still loading…" is in the panel's toast, which in Focus now sits under the card (`ToastPlace`): in Focus its top is about 25 pt under the clicked button instead of about 550 pt below it (780 pt across the page when it was over the PDF). A double click on Resolve: the second click, 0.03 s later while the engine still had the first answer, was ignored (`answerPending`), the toast said "R1-21 resolved", the selection moved on once. R, Return and Follow up with an empty field put the cursor in the field while the engine was held busy. At 960 × 700 (a closed round): Review panel 339 pt, PDF 420 pt; Focus panel 339 pt, PDF 620 pt, the page 0.677 → 0.881 (×1.30; before the fix the panel went to 520 or 400 and the page ×1.05); leaving gave 339 / 420 back. At 1440: 520 pt throughout, PDF 682 → 919 pt. The headers' signs start at 26 pt with the rows' chips and notes, the names at 49 pt, Show › / Hide ⌄ ends with the page numbers. Focus p50 22.5 ms, p90 30.3 ms.
25. **Reading marks optional** (2026-10-18, release build, background, display asleep, load about 3.5–4.5 on 8 cores). `AppModel.readingMarksOn` is published, but only Settings changes it, so a J/K step pays nothing for it.
26. **The tab switch** (UI v2.2, 2026-10-18, release build, background, display asleep, load about 2.5–4 on 8 cores). Both lists stay in the panel, so a switch makes no table and lays out no list; its own turn (`--debug-script tab`, `ms`: from the switch to the next turn of the run loop) on a copy of the student-notes demo (6 cards, R2-07 expanded, 8 switches a run, 2 runs each, interleaved with a build of the sources before v2.2 given the same timing): 35.6–61.5 ms (the first switch the most) against 94–175 ms, which made the list again and centred its card. A time profile of 60 switches: about 18 ms of main-thread work a switch, a third of it SwiftUI's update of the views that observe `AppModel` (the sidebar's list diffed its rows), then constraints and layout; nothing during the slide, which is Core Animation's. J/K (`keys` on `Fixtures/state-many.json`, 60 listed cards, 2 pairs): `key_ms` p50 20.8 / 20.0 ms against 21.0 / 19.7 ms, p90 32.3 / 31.3 against 33.8 / 34.4 ms; 119 card bodies over the 120 steps, 60 row bodies (the first step's dimming) and no list body in both builds.
27. **The card's turns** (UI v2.2 card, 2026-10-19, release build; background runs of `--debug-scroll-perf keys` interleaved with a build of the sources before it, each pair in turn order, load 2.3–5.0 on 8 cores). The turns, their labels (`AttributedString`s), the fold, the cut bubbles and the edits linked elsewhere are made with the card model, once per applied state; the view walks them, with no measuring of its own (the length limit is on characters, the label one `Text`), so a J/K step still lays out one card. In Focus (the demo, 2 pairs): p50 24.3 and 26.1 against 30.7 and 27.9 ms, p90 40.0 and 41.1 against 39.8 and 37.5 ms, 1 card body, 1 row body and 1 list update a step in both.
28. **Crops in the panel's ink** (§4, 2026-10-20, release build; background runs of `--debug-scroll-perf keys` on a 15-card round, 6 of them with crops (four in ink, a figure, a table's deletion point), interleaved with a build of the same sources with the crops as before: white paper at a fixed density 2; display asleep, load 1.6–5.5 on 8 cores; 4 pairs). `key_ms` p50 21.4–23.6 ms (median 23.1) against 22.0–24.3 ms (23.0), the pair differences −1.5 to +1.3 ms (median −0.5); p90 33.7–36.2 against 33.2–40.9 ms; 29 card bodies over the 30 steps in both, no row body after the first step, no list body. Each crop's own geometry callback, the second body of its picture when the drawn image arrives, and the drawing off the main thread (now at the shown density, with the paper taken out and Dark Mode's ink per pixel) leave a step as it was.
29. **The tag filter and the arrangements** (§27, 2026-10-21, release build, background, display asleep). The line over the list observes its own `ListBar`; the list follows `PanelModel.groups`, which carries the filter and the arrangement, so a J/K step changes neither: it lays out the new card and moves no row (`expand` makes the groups again only when the card the selection leaves goes to another group or was listed only while it was selected, or the new card was not listed).

## 11. Empty, loading and error states
| state | shown |
|---|---|
| no paper listed | The page "Add a student's paper" (a document icon, then "Create it, then start Round 1 with their LaTeX folder or .zip. Paperole builds it, and you comment on the PDF. Import… brings back a backup."), with [New Paper…], the window's one prominent button, and [Import…] (POLISH §3 row 15; `NoPaperView`, `EmptyPage`; since §28 it is Import…: a review folder, listed where it is, a zipped one, an export or a backup. Settings' Import…, File › Import… and the sidebar's Add Existing… are the same action, §28). With papers listed and none open: "No paper open", "Choose one in the sidebar, or add another student's paper.", the same buttons plain. Its lines are broken by hand: a Text sized to its height there (`fixedSize`) shifted the sidebar up under the title bar and hid its footer. |
| a paper with no rounds | The same page: "No rounds yet", "Choose the student's LaTeX folder or .zip to start Round 1. …", [Start Round 1…] (prominent; the toolbar's is plain). When the engine refuses Round 1 (two files that could be the main file, or any other ingest error while there is no round), the sheet "Start Round 1" opens with the engine's words, the chosen source kept, an empty Main file field with the keys, and Engine "Automatic" (§30). |
| a paper that cannot be opened | Its state call failed (a ledger that does not read): "This paper could not be opened" (`exclamationmark.circle`), the engine's words, and [Try Again]; no alert, and no round menu while there is no state (§30). |
| open round, no comments | The empty state in the comment list, centred, and nothing else: a highlighter icon, "No comments yet", and the next steps in words a mouse can follow (POLISH §3 row 15, §6: no key caps): "Select text in the PDF, then click Comment." / "Hold Option and drag round a figure, table or equation." / "Pick the colour on the bar over the selection." (it was one hint line naming Return and ⌥). |
| nothing left in the open round | The done card on top (§1). |
| closed round | The panel's banner "Round 3 · closed Oct 17 · read only" (§1; with [Back to Round 4] for an earlier round, §17). No editing or verdicts; marks stay clickable. |
| engine busy | M1 toolbar progress. Navigation never waits (J, K, a click on a row or a mark). An answer or comment action is refused and says so in the panel's toast, at the panel's foot by the card's buttons, where an answer's own toast shows (no Undo; review 2026-10-18: over the PDF it was far from the click, up to 780 pt away in Focus, and wore the reading bar): "Still loading… try again in a moment." (O27, POLISH §7; `AppModel.refusedWhileBusy`): V, W, ⌘↩, the choice row's Return and ⌫ (its Esc, which needs no engine, acts), ⌘Z, ⇧⌘Z, 1–6, Delete, Return on an answered card, and the card's buttons: Resolve, Follow up with text, Keep as is, the choice row's, the Changes tab's Mark checked and Comment on it…, Edit (the link under this round's words stays enabled since 2026-10-24) and ⋯ › Add Closing Remark / Add Follow-up Note, and a Finish Round… that is not the toolbar's (the done card, Jump, Your Move). **Edit Note and Re-anchor… start while the engine works** (BACKLOG O30, UI v2.2; they said "Still loading…"): opening the note's editor (a click on the note, Return, ⋯ › Edit Note) and re-anchoring (⋯ › Re-anchor…, the section's link, with a box drawn to re-anchor to: `PDFController.canDrawBox`) need no engine; only their saving waits, queued behind the request the engine is working on ("Saving…", or Place sent in its turn). Putting the cursor in the card's field needs no engine and is never refused (R, Return on a card to check, Follow up with the field empty, as a click in the field; review 2026-10-18: they were refused, the click was not), so a follow-up can be written while the engine works; sending it waits. A click on the card whose own answer the engine is working on is ignored without a word (§8). They are not kept for later (the reply can change what they would act on), and the answer buttons stay enabled, so a click is answered rather than lost (until then they were disabled, and the keys did nothing without a word; the engine is busy for well under a second after each answer, and for a second or two rebuilding prose.json on the first open of an older review folder). The ⋯ menu's colours and Delete…, Finish Round… and the toolbar's other engine buttons are still disabled; a note being saved shows a spinner in its card's header. |
| build failed | The M1 view, with the status line under it in red (§1). For ``File `X' not found`` (e.g. a table file the student did not send), the headline is "Missing file: X — ask the student to resend it, or Retry with a corrected folder." |
| built with errors | The status line in red, "⚠ Built with 2 errors" (§1); the review continues. |
| no context, crop failure, engine refusal | The §3 fallbacks, the §4 placeholder, and editors that stay open with their text (§8). |

## 12. Accessibility
- **Rows**: a compact row is one element, e.g. "R1-03, to check, changed, page 5. Note: …" ("left open" in a closed or earlier round; ", unsent draft" when it has one); with the shown round's student's note it adds ". Student's note: …" (". The student pointed an edit to it" for a bare one); a closed line adds ". Closing remark: …". A change row says "no comment asked for" (it said "unrequested") or "answers R1-02", and ". student's note on R1-02: …" when a note linked it.
- **Toast**: the panel's toast after an answer, undo or redo is read out (§8); its Undo or Redo is a button.
- **Reading**: Next, Mark Read, N, C and a click on a bar post the count left to VoiceOver ("Marked read · 8 to read"), which the status line shows instead of a toast (§18); a Contents row reads as its title, ", changed paragraphs to read", its open comments and ", shown" for the section the PDF is in.
- **Turns** (§3; CARD §3.9): each turn is one element, with no gendered pronoun: "Round 4, Ouyang Mingxuan replaced the image, 1 edit. Note: Re-plotted …, plot.tex line 12", "Rounds 2 to 3, unchanged", "Round 4, unchanged since your follow-up", "Round 3, you followed up, sent Oct 17: Still no times.", then the round's text; a cut bubble reads whole (`CardContent.spoken`); a turn whose count is a link has the action "Show the edit in the PDF". The fold's line is a button, "R2 · … revised, you followed up, 2 turns folded"; each edit-elsewhere frame is a button of its own after its turn (not merged into it, whose label would have hidden it), reading its kind, section, page and words, and showing the edit.
- **Not by colour alone**: status has an icon and a word, kinds have glyphs, and swatches have names and a selected state.
- **Find, the mark's toolbar and the round's card** (§32): the find's field is "Find in Paper", its arrows "Previous match" and "Next match", and a new match or none is announced ("3 of 12", "“boat” is not in the paper"); the mark's toolbar is a group named "R2-01’s mark" holding the swatches (each its colour's name, the current one selected), the kinds, Edit Note and "Delete R2-01…"; the card's colour dot is "Mark colour: Yellow". On the round's card the rings are hidden, each tag row reads as one ("Clarity, 5": the squares are its count drawn) and the drawn pages read "A mark on 12 of 14 pages" (`PageStrip.spoken`).
- **Every action has a visible control** (POLISH §6, mouse first) **and a menu or key path**, so PDF marks need no VoiceOver access of their own.
- **Reduce Motion**: no scroll or flash animation.
- **Focus and Jump** (§20): Focus's panel is the card's, read as in Review; with nothing selected, its words say what to click. The Jump panel is "Jump", its field "Jump to", each row a button whose label is its words.
- **Group headers** (§1): each is a header ("Closed this round, 4"); a folded group's is also a button, "Shows the group" / "Hides the group".
- **Contrast** (POLISH §2.2, §19): secondary words 5.2:1 on the panel (6.9:1 in Dark Mode), dates included; accent text 6.2:1 (8.5:1); errors 5.4:1; tertiary only for placeholders. No text under 11 pt (the old outline's counts and the unsent-draft tag were 10 and 10.5; `.caption` is 10 pt on macOS).
- **Increase Contrast**: no special handling yet (the system's own controls follow it; design/ui-v2's 1 pt border on the raised card is not built).
- **Hit targets**: swatches ≥ 18×18; PDF hit areas ≥ 8×8 view points; a bar 8 view points wide (never on the text beside it, §18).

## 13. Debug hooks and tests
**`--appearance light|dark`** shows a test run's window in that appearance, whatever the system's (screenshots of either, §19); nothing is saved. **`--reading on|off`** turns reading marks on or off for the run (§18 "Optional"), whatever the user's setting, which it never changes; without it a `--background` or `--fixture` run (`swift test` too) has them off. **`--focus`** starts in Focus once the paper's PDF is shown (§20; for `--debug-scroll-perf keys` in Focus, which then measures the panel's one-card list). **`--debug-script "<item>;…"`** renames M1's `--debug-drag` runner; `--debug-drag` stays as an alias. It runs after the PDF has settled and writes one JSON value per item to `--debug-out` (stderr by default). Pages are 0-based and points are contract points. Events are in-process NSEvents or `performKey`, so no Accessibility permission is needed. `--debug-quit` quits when the script ends.

| item | does | writes |
|---|---|---|
| `page,x1,y1,x2,y2[,clicks]`, `clear`, `return` | as in M1 | as in M1 |
| `menu page,x,y[ <title>]` | right-click (§35), then, with a title, that item (in a submenu too) | `{"menu": [titles, "✓ " before one on, "—", [submenu title, [items]]], "picked", "selected", "selection", "editor", "editorMode", "after": {"selected", "selection", "page", "find", "boxArmed"}}` |
| `zoom [in\|out\|actual\|fit\|<percent>]`, `zoom by <factor> page,x,y` | View's zoom items or the status line's menu; ⌘-scroll at that point (§35) | `{"zoom" (null: Fit Width), "status", "scale", "auto", "fit", "focus", "page", "third", "pointer": [[x, y] before, after]}` |
| `click page,x,y` | a click without a drag | `{"click": id\|null, "expanded", "tab", "panelScrolled", "flash"}` |
| `optclick page,x,y` / `box page,x1,y1,x2,y2` | ⌥-click / ⌥-drag | `{"editor"}` / `{"box": rect, "toolbar"}` |
| `key <j k v r w b h u s f 1-6 return esc delete up down cmd-return cmd-z cmd-shift-z>` (`escape`, `backspace` are aliases; `up` and `down` reach the Jump field) | the key, to the focused editor or through §9's routing | `{"key", "handled", "selected", "editing", "choice", "ms"}` (`ms`: the key handled and the window laid out and drawn at once, as in the key's own turn) |
| `type <text>` | `insertText` into the first responder | — |
| `typeslow <cps> <text>` | as `type`, one character at a time at `<cps>` characters a second, each pause ±30% from a fixed seed, so a run repeats (promo takes, design/promo/STORYBOARD.md §9); the text is the rest of the item with its inner spaces, or in `"double quotes"` with its spaces at either end too (never a `;`); the next item one pause after the last character | `{"typeslow", "typed", "start", "end"}` (`typed`: the characters a text field took; `start`, `end`: the first and last one's `ProcessInfo.systemUptime`) |
| `card <id>` / `note <id>` / `cardmenu <id> <title>` | the row's click (its action; `rowclick` sends the mouse's own events), the note, or a `⋯` item (Colour and Kind are its submenus; `Source and Edits…` opens the popover at `⋯`) | `{"selected", "editing", "reanchor"}` |
| `button <resolve\|followup\|keep\|discard\|gotit> [<s>]` / `field` | a click on the selected card's answer button (or the field's Discard, or the first-run tip's Got it): the button's action behind the same click guard (`ClickGuard`; the card's buttons record no frames, which the J/K path would pay for, so no mouse event is sent); the next item `<s>` seconds later (0.6 by default: `button keep 0.3; button keep` is a double click across the move on) / in its follow-up field (§8) | `{"selected", "editing", "reanchor", "ignored", "tip", "toast"}` (`ignored`: the click came within 0.4 s of an answer moving on to the card, `ClickGuard`, or while the card's own answer was with the engine, `answerPending`; `tip`: the tip still shown; `toast`: the panel's toast) |
| `rowclick <id>` | a click on that row of the shown tab's list, scrolled into view first, as the mouse sends it (the mouse-down and -up to the row's view, which its tap gesture needs) | `{"selected", "editing", "reanchor", "clicked", "expanded"}` |
| `toolbarclick` | a click on the floating toolbar's Comment (§7), as the mouse sends it | `{"clicked", "editor", "editorQuote", "toolbar"}` |
| `editorclick <save\|cancel>` | a click on the new-comment editor's Save or Cancel (§7), as the mouse sends it (Cancel on a changed note asks first: `confirmDiscard`) | `{"clicked", "editor", "selected", "confirmDiscard"}` |
| `toastclick` | a click on the panel toast's Undo or Redo, as the mouse sends it (§8) | `{"clicked": [x, y, view], "toast", "action", "selected", "verdict", "undo", "redo"}` |
| `hover [<id>\|off\|away\|resignkey\|becomekey\|resignactive]` | the comment list's pointer, simulated (§1 Hover; a background run's window is never key, so the list reads this pointer instead of the real one): moved onto that row's middle, scrolled into view first (the row it was on gets no exit of its own); off the list with an exit (`off`) or with none (`away`); the window resigning or becoming key, the app resigning active (their notifications, as AppKit posts them); nothing: as it is | `{"hovered": [the rows drawn hovered], "hit": the view a click at the pointer reaches}`, plus `{"id", "dimmed", "quiet"}` for a row |
| `cardclick <x>,<y>[,up]` | a click at that point of the expanded card (points from its top left), as the mouse sends it: on its note or paper text, selectable text (§9); `up`: the mouse-down and -up to the view under it at once, as its SwiftUI buttons need (Resolve at `70,<card height − 33>`) | `{"selected", "editing", "reanchor", "firstResponder"}` |
| `quitcheck` | — | what quitting would ask about a new comment's unsaved note and unsent drafts: `{"title", "detail"}` or `null` |
| `finishsheet [send <id> [<s>]\|discard <id> [<s>]\|undo <id>\|show <id>\|left <comments\|edits\|paragraphs>\|cancel\|finish]` | opens Finish Round… (§17), or acts in it: a draft's Send as follow-up, Discard (each as its click, with the sheet's 0.4 s guard), a discard's Undo, or Show, a Left for you row's Review or Show, Cancel, Finish Round, or (`done`) the card's Done once finished; the next item `<s>` seconds later (2 by default) | `{"shown", "sections", "left": [[item, title, count]], "goes": [[item, title, count]], "reading": {current, flash}, "followUps", "new", "toCheck", "closed", "drafts": [[id, text]], "rows": [[id, unsent\|sent\|discarded]], "unrequested", "unread", "card", "finished", "error", "round", "roundClosed", "selected", "tab", "toast", "notice"}` (`toast`: the PDF's, which says why the sheet did not open; `card`: the round's card so far, as `summary` gives it; `finished`: the card the sheet turned into, or null; `notice`: the window's line and its buttons) |
| `summary [<n>\|export <path.png> [title] [student] [light\|dark]\|copy\|done]` | Round N Summary… (the shown round's, or round n's), its card written as Export Image… writes it (no panel), its Copy Image (a `--background` run's own pasteboard), or its Done; the next item 1 s later | `{"shown", "round", "words", "wrote", "figures": [[value, of, label]], "tags": [[name, count]], "praise", "untagged", "marks": [[page, y, column, kind]], "pages", "dateLine", "student", "facts", "exported": {path, bytes, width, height}, "copied": [types]}` (§32) |
| `find [<text>]` · `findkey return\|shift-return\|esc` · `findclick next\|previous` | ⌘F, then the text typed into the toolbar's find as the user types; the field's Return, ⇧Return or Esc; the ‹ › arrows (§32); each result once its search is in | `{"query", "count", "index", "counter", "searching", "highlighted", "inShownDocument", "selection", "selectionPage", "visible", "ring", "toolbar", "firstResponder", "field"}` |
| `markbar` · `markbarclick <colour\|kind\|edit\|delete>` · `dot [<id>\|close]` | the toolbar by a new comment's mark (§32); a click on one of its buttons as the mouse sends it (the result 0.8 s later); the selected card's (or that card's) colour dot clicked, or its popover closed | `{"shown", "id", "color", "kind", "kinds", "frame", "lines", "selected", "toolbar", "swatches"}`, after a click also `{"clicked", "editing", "confirmDelete", "undo", "redo", "toast", "style"}` |
| `resume [ask\|cancel\|yes] [<path>]` | Resume Round N (§31) for the paper shown, or for that paper as its context menu: `ask` (the default) as the read-only box's button or a menu item, which shows the confirmation; `cancel` its Cancel; `yes` its Resume (asked first when it was not); the next item 0.5 s later (`yes`: 3 s) | `{"asking", "message", "button", "roundMenu", "paperMenus": [[path, round]], "round", "latest", "roundClosed", "resumable", "finishes": [{at, final, email}], "level", "tab", "toast", "alert"}` (`button`: the read-only box shows Resume Round N; `roundMenu`: the round the round menu and More offer); `finishsheet` also gives `before`, a resumed round's line |
| `change <id>` / `changecomment <id>` | clicks a Changes row / its Comment on this change (§16) | `{"selected", "expanded", "panel", "unrequested", "answers", "notes": [[comment id, the student's note]]}` / `{"editor", "quote", "editorQuote", "box", "selected"}` (`editorQuote`: the selected words the editor shows on top, §7) |
| `tab <comments\|changes>` | the panel's tab control | `{"tab", "selected", "marks", "ms"}` (`ms`: from the switch to the next turn of the run loop, its own turn done, §10.26) |
| `motion <factor>` | the tab switch's motion that many times slower (`TabMotion.slow`), so screenshots catch it in flight; `motion 1` back | `{"motion"}` |
| `fold [student\|closed\|linked]` / `foldclick […]` / `headerclick […]` | a folded group (by default the shown tab's Closed this round, or its changes linked to comments): toggled as Show / Hide does (§1) / a click on its Show or Hide / a click on its name (the whole header is the button), each as the mouse sends it (its header scrolled into view first) | `{"showStudent", "showClosed"}` / `{"showLinked"}`, and `clicked` ([x, y, the view hit]) for `headerclick` |
| `groups` | — | the comment list's groups (§1): `{"toCheck", "forStudent", "closed": [ids], "showStudent", "showClosed", "selected", "toast", "dimmed": [ids], "cardTop", "ready", "tip", "rows", "headers", "studentNotes": [ids whose row has the student's note sign]}` (`cardTop`: the expanded card's top in the panel, which an answer's move on keeps, §6; `ready`: nothing left, `PanelModel.ready`; `tip`: the first-run tip shown; `rows`: what the list shows, in order: "header: …" (with " · N" for a count), "done", "empty", "hint: …", "focus hint" and the cards' ids, not the gaps; `headers`: `[name, count, quieter words, sign, Show\|Hide]` each) |
| `doneclick` | a click on the done card's Finish Round…, as the mouse sends it (§1) | the `finishsheet` result plus `clicked` |
| `nopaper [all]` | no paper open (the page of §11); `all`, in a `--fixture` run only, also no paper listed (a fixture run never saves its list) | `{"paper", "papers"}` |
| `shot <path.png>` | `screencapture -l` of the app's own window (as `tools/shot.sh --pid`) | `{"shot"}` |
| `swatch <name>` / `kind <name>` | the toolbar control, or the selected card's colour or kind (as 1–6 or `⋯` › Colour) | `{"color", "kind"}` |
| `dump` | — | `{"selected", "expanded", "editing", "draft", "drafts", "focusRequest", "choice", "verdict", "verdictNote", "undo", "redo", "toast", "firstResponder", "toolbar": {shown, color, kind, frame}, "reanchor", "panel": {offset, viewport, card: [minY, maxY], pin}, "pdf": {page, visible, status}, "toastPlace": {below, bottom}, "subtitle", "roundLabel"}` (`pin`: how far the answer row is moved up, §2; `status`: the status line's page, 0-based, §1, where `page` is PDFView's `currentPage`; `toastPlace`: where the panel's toast goes, §8; `subtitle`: the toolbar's, §1) |
| `thread` | — | the expanded card's thread (§3): `{"id", "status", "row", "fold", "studentNote", "sections": [{"label", "rounds", "tags", "edits", "uncertain", "revised", "kind", "text", "trimmed", "crop"}]}`, the text with `[[marked]]` and `__changed__` words and `‸` carets (`crop`: "R1 p.3", then " faint", " deletion" and, for a crop in the panel's ink (§4), " ink"; the sections' `said`, `students` and `entries` are gone since 2026-10-24: what was said and the student's notes are in `turns`); since UI v2.2 also `"turns": [{"label", "dot", "date", "link", "section", "uncertain", "words", "place", "elsewhere"}]`, the conversation the card shows (§3; `words`: an earlier round's cut ones with "[+N]" sentences left; `link`: the change the count shows), and `"attachments"`, the edits a note linked elsewhere ("c05 added § Q3 … p.7") |
| `notewarnings [show\|hide]` | shows or hides the Details… popover of the Comments tab's line of notes not used (§3) | `{"line", "shown", "warnings": [[kind, file:line, text, message]]}` |
| `showall` / `window <w>,<h>` | opens the expanded card's fold and each Show all, of its sections and of its cut bubbles / resizes the window's content (a card taller than the panel) | — |
| `round <n>` | picks round n, as the toolbar's round menu (§17) | `{"round", "latest", "earlier", "open", "pdf", "comments", "changes", "selected", "marks", "toast"}` |
| `papers` | — | the papers list: `[{"path", "step", "badge", "badgeShown"}]` (§17; `badgeShown`: false on the open paper) |
| `sidebar` | — | the sidebar (§17, §18): `{"papersShown", "header", "othersToCheck", "contents", "message", "current", "currentRowVisible", "papersHeaderVisible", "listSelected", "bodies": {sidebar, paper, section}}` (`header`: the folded Papers header's words; `current`: the section the PDF is in; `papersHeaderVisible`: the list's first row, Papers' header, in the sidebar's view; `bodies`: the body counts so far) |
| `papersfold` | folds or unfolds Papers, as its header's chevron does | the `sidebar` result |
| `setting reading on\|off` | Settings' reading marks, as its toggle (§18; a test run never saves it) | the `reading` result plus `{"menus", "dots", "subtitle", "ready"}` (`menus`: the menu bar's titles; `dots`: the Contents rows with an olive dot; `ready`: `AppModel.readyToFinish`) |
| `settings [general\|overview\|reading\|tags\|data]` | the Settings window's five tabs (toolbar tabs, as the scene draws them, §23, §28, §34) in a titled window of its own on that tab, ordered behind every other window (a test run cannot open the Settings scene without bringing it to the front), for `tools/shot.sh --pid … --all` | `{"settings", "on", "size"}` |
| `data [root <folder>\|none]`, `data export [student:<name>\|<paper folder>]`, `data exportto <out.zip>`, `data import <file or folder> [<folder>]`, `data choose <n> <choice>`, `data settings on\|off`, `data importnow`, `data close`, `data history <paper folder>`, `data putback <n>`, `data remove <paper folder>\|student:<name>`, `data undo`, `data comments csv\|json <out> <paper folder>\|student:<name>`, `data tags export\|import <file>`, `data backup on <folder>\|off\|now`, `data check`, `data restore <paper folder>`, `data clear`, `data cancel`, `data scroll top\|bottom` | Data & Storage, the Export and Import sheets, Put Back an Earlier Ledger…, Remove from List and its Undo, the backups and the daily check without a panel (§28; `DataModel.debug`, its comment lists each item); the next item once the work has finished | `{"status": working\|done\|failed\|none, "message", "url", "root", "papers", "missing", "tags", "drafts", "thumbnails", "notice", "export": {rows, summary, name, everything, phase} \| null, "import": {header, items: [[title, group, choice, choices, detail]], destination, settings, action, report, stopped, error} \| null, "history": {versions, selected, error} \| null, "backups": {on, folder, volume, step, last, left, failure, files, sameDisk, scheduled, due}, "damaged"}` |
| `setting fold on\|off` | Settings' folding of a long appendix, as its switch (§23; never saved) | the `nav` result |
| `icon [automatic\|light\|dark]` | Settings › General's app icon style, as a click on it (§26, §34; a test run never saves it) | `{"style", "system", "resource", "dock", "named", "alert", "about", "distinct", "remembers"}` (`resource`: the .icns shown; `dock`, `named`, `alert`, `about`: the Dock's image, the image named NSApplicationIcon, an alert's icon laid out but never shown, and the About window's option, each the icon shown, drawn at 64 px; `distinct`: the other form, light or dark, looks different) |
| `migration` | whether this run would move the app's data to its new name (`Migration.gate`; never in a test run) and the places it uses (`DataHome`; §34) | `{"gate", "support", "caches", "reviews", "notice"}` |
| `appearance light\|dark` | the app's appearance changed while it runs, as the system's changes (§26: an Automatic icon follows it) | the `icon` result |
| `ovpart show\|fold appendix\|references` | a folded part shown at its real length, or folded again (§23) | the `nav` result |
| `tagfilter <tag>\|untagged\|off`, `groupby status\|tag\|section`, `list` | the comment list's tag filter, its arrangement (§27; a test run never saves it), or nothing | `{"filter", "grouping", "shown", "options": [[id, name, count]], "title", "caption", "selected", "listed", "rows", "headers", "toCheck", "next"}` |
| `listmenu [<title>]` | the list's menu as its button builds it; with a title, that item chosen first (§27) | the list result and `"menu": ["# Arrange By", [title, checked, count], "—", …]` |
| `listmenuclick` | a click on the list menu's button, as the mouse sends it; the menu is kept, not opened (it would come up over the viewer's work) | `{"clicked": [x, y, view], "opened": {"under": [x, y, w, h], "menu"}}` |
| `panelwidth <w>` | the review's divider moved so the panel is w pt wide (screenshots) | none |
| `studenttag <tag>\|untagged\|off`, `studenttagopen <path>\|<id>` | a row of the shown student's Topics by round clicked / a line of its list clicked (§27) | `{"student", "tag", "title", "meta", "papers": [[title, [[id, status, round]]]]}` / the `nav` result 3 s later |
| `paper <path>` | selects that paper at the level shown (since §21 a click in the list shows its overview: `paperopen`) | `{"paper", "round", "popover", "toast"}` |
| `paperclick <path>` | selects that paper's row in the sidebar's own table (after the Papers header), which hands it to the list's selection binding, as a click on the row does (a synthetic mouse-down on SwiftUI's list selects nothing) | `{"paper", "listSelected", "popover", "toast", "hit"}` (`listSelected`: the row the list shows selected, 2 s later) |
| `close` / `closemenu` | writes the results so far, then clicks the window's close button / closes it as File › Close does (`performClose`); in a `--background` run the app then quits without asking | `{"delegate", "question"}` (what quitting would ask); `{"closed", "popover", "asked"}` 1 s later only if it is still running |
| `mark <name>` | creates the file `<debug-out>.<name>`, so a script outside acts at that point (a screenshot with `tools/shot.sh --pid`: `shot`, `screencapture` from the app itself, wrote no file from an instance started with `open -g -n`); it holds the mark's `ProcessInfo.systemUptime`, the clock of ScreenCaptureKit's frame times (design/promo/pipeline/capture/marks.py) | `{"mark", "uptime"}` |
| `focus [on\|off]` | Focus on, off, or toggled, as the toolbar's button does (§20); `focus on` while on only reports | `{"focus", "sidebar", "panel", "panelWidth", "panelMax", "panelItem", "rows", "tabs", "status", "selected", "sidebarShown", "pdf": {width, scale, auto, fit, third}, "widths", "before", "bodies"}` (`panel`: the shown tab's list in the window; `panelMax`: the panel's width cap in Focus, `AppModel.focusPanelWidth`; `panelItem`: its split view item's width; `rows`: what it shows, as `groups` does (in Focus the card's id or "focus hint"); `tabs`: the panel's tabs in the window (`PanelTabs.shown`); `status`: the status line's reading count; `widths`: the PDF view's width at once, a turn later and at 50, 150 and 400 ms, so an instant switch has its last width a turn later; `before` / `third`: the point a third of the way down the PDF before and after). The `bar` and `focusclick` items went with Focus's bar (POLISH §7): in Focus the card's buttons are `button` and `cardclick` |
| `busy on\|off` | holds the engine busy without a request, as while it works (O27, §11), or lets it go | `{"busy"}` |
| `jump [<text>]` | opens Jump (as ⌘K) and types the text into its field (its old query selected, so replaced) | `{"shown", "query", "highlighted", "firstResponder", "groups": [[title, [[lead, title, detail, trailing]]]]}` |
| `jumpclick <n>` | a click on the n-th row (from 0, across the groups), the mouse-down and -up to its view (a tap gesture needs both) | `{"clicked", "shown", "selected", "tab", "focus", "round", "outlineCurrent", "selection", "toast", "find", "firstResponder"}` (`selection`: the PDF's text selection, where a find put it) |
| `nav` | — | the navigation (§21): `{"level", "paper", "showsOverview", "back", "forward" (the places, "overview:paper"), "canBack", "canForward", "overview": {status, paper, selection, strip, scroll, saved, rounds, sentence}, "review": {round, selected, tab, pdf, page}, "title", "subtitle", "reviewHidden", "sidebarShown", "contents", "firstResponder", "pending", "steps", "forwardSteps", "place": {label, page, y}, "chip": {label, faded}, "flash", "backHelp", "forwardHelp"}` (`reviewHidden`: Review's split view hidden in AppKit; `selection`: `section:<id>@<n>`, `round:<n>`, `page:<p>@<n>` with p from 1, `comment:<id>`; `steps`, `forwardSteps`: each place's step label or null, `chip` null without one, §36) |
| `paperopen <path> [review]` | the sidebar's click on a paper (its overview), or double click (Review) | the `nav` result |
| `back` / `forward` `[<s>]` | ⌘[ / ⌘] | the `nav` result <s> seconds later (1.5 by default) |
| `link <page> <n>` | a click on the n-th link within the PDF on that page (both from 0), as the mouse sends it (§36; with the round's link table it previews, §37) | `{"link": {words, to}, "nav", "preview"}` |
| `preview [<what>]` | the reference preview (§37) after `<what>`: `<page> <n>` a click on the n-th link of the round's link table on that page (both from 0; the engine's source links too), `again` / `double` a click on its link again / the second click of a double click, `go` its Go to ›, `picture <i>` a click on its i-th picture, `pin` its pin, `drag <dx> <dy>` its title row dragged, `cited <i>` its i-th "Cited on" page, `copy` Copy Reference, `scroll` the PDF scrolled 40 pt | `{"table", "preview": {kind, title, page, box, entries: [[label, [w, h], drawn]], cited, count, footer, continues, side, gap, frame, inView, widthShare, heightShare, scale, pageScale} or null, "pinned": [titles], "nav", "clipboard" (copy)}` (`side`: above or below the link; `gap`: from its line, never below 0; `drawn`: its crop in the cache) |
| `sidebutton back\|forward` | a mouse's side button posted to the window, through its monitor (§36) | the `nav` result |
| `allrounds` / `continue` | the round menu's All Rounds / the overview's Continue Round N | the `nav` result |
| `ovclick` / `ovopen <item>` | a click / a double click on an overview item (`section:<id>@<n>`, `round:<n>`, `page:<p>@<n>` with p from 1, `comment:<id>`, `none`) | the `nav` result, with `timing` (§21 Debug hooks) |
| `ovtext` | — | what the overview shows, in words and numbers, and `loadMs` (§21 Debug hooks) |
| `ovscroll <y>` / `ovwait` | the overview scrolled to y from its top / waits (at most 10 s) until its data is in | the `nav` result |
| `wait <s>`, `quit` | — | — |

`editing` comes from the window's first responder: `note:<id>`, `remark:<id>` (this round's words), `field:<id>` (the follow-up field), `popover`, `text` (any other text view), or `null`; so a field that took the cursor by itself shows.

**Background runs.** `--background` keeps a test instance out of the user's way: never activated, no Dock icon or menu bar, its window created behind all others (README "Test runs"). Its unsent drafts go to `--drafts <folder>` (relaunching with the same folder finds them again), else to a temporary folder of its own, never to Application Support (a `--fixture` run, `swift test` included, likewise); it never asks before quitting. It reads and writes no window frame or split view size in the user's defaults: from the start of the run, naming a window frame or a split view does nothing (`BackgroundRun.keepUsersFrames`). Clearing the names once the app had launched came too late: SwiftUI names its window's frame before then, and AppKit saved it at once when it had to move it onto the current screens (seen 2026-10-17; checked since by seeding an off-screen frame in a throwaway defaults domain, a copy of the binary outside the bundle, which a resize and a quit, by `kill -TERM` or `--debug-quit`, left as it was). `screencapture -l` captures that window whole, also from another Space. Launch arguments are never taken as files to open (`NSTreatUnknownArgumentsAsOpen` off): a flag without a value, such as `--background` or `--debug-quit`, made AppKit take the next value for a file, and SwiftUI then opened no window until the app was activated.

**Locked screen.** While the screen is locked the app is not activated and SwiftUI opens no window, so a debug run (`--debug-script`, `--debug-scroll-perf`) that finds no window after 2 s makes one itself with the same content (`AppDelegate`). Its screenshots work; frame timings need the display awake (`caffeinate -u`).

**`--debug-scroll-perf scroll|wheel|keys|pdf`** runs once the panel is shown and the crops have rendered, on the list of the tab shown (`--tab changes` for the Changes tab). **`scroll`** moves the panel's NSScrollView 24 pt per `NSView.displayLink` tick, to the bottom and back; **`wheel`** does the same with a 24-pixel scroll-wheel event per tick, through the view's own scrolling; **`pdf`** moves the PDF 24 pt a tick, at most 400 ticks down and back (the sidebar's Contents follows it; `sections`: how many times the PDF's section changed). When the display link does not tick within 0.5 s (the display asleep), these step on a 60 Hz timer instead and time each step's scroll, layout and drawing at once: `timer: true`, `work_p50_ms`, `work_p95_ms`, `work_max_ms`. **`keys`** presses J through every card, then K back, every 150 ms. **Output**: `{cards, frames, p50_ms, p95_ms, max_ms, over_50ms, bodies: {row, expanded, list, sidebar, paper, section}}`, with per-step `ms` and `bodies` for `keys`. The body counts come from debug counters in `body`. **Fixture**: `Fixtures/state-many.json` (80 synthetic comments on the round-2 fixture PDF, no advisor data) makes runs repeatable.

**Example checks** (each launches the app, then quits it): **Answers** (§8; check `verdict` and `verdict_note` with the engine's `state` afterwards): `card R1-02; key r; type …; key cmd-return; dump` sends a follow-up; `key r; type …; key escape; key j; dump` keeps an unsent draft (`drafts`) and sends nothing; `key r; type …; key escape; key v; dump` shows the choice row with the verdict still null, then `key return` closes with the text as the closing remark or `key backspace` without it; `key v` on a follow-up card shows the choice row (`sent: true`); `key r` on it clears and puts the note in the field; `key cmd-z` / `key cmd-shift-z` undo and redo. **Groups** (§1, §8; `groups` after each step): `card R2-01; key v; wait 1.5; groups` selects the next card to check and lists R2-01 under `closed`, with the toast "R2-01 resolved"; `card R1-18; button resolve; wait 1.5; groups` kept R1-18 selected and in `toCheck` until `key j` (before UI v2.1; a click now moves on as a key does, §8); `key r; type …; key esc; key w; key return` closes with the text and moves on; `key cmd-z` selects the card again in `toCheck`, with `showClosed` still false; `card R1-21; key r; type …; key esc; key j; key k; dump` finds the draft on R1-21. A draft typed, then `kill -TERM` of the instance's own PID and a relaunch with the same `--drafts`, is still there. `editing` stays `null` after `card` and `key j`/`key k`. **`click`**: on a highlight it returns that id. A second click on two overlapping marks returns the other. On an unmarked spot it returns `null` and keeps the selection. **`card` / `key j`**: `card` on a visible row gives `panelScrolled: false`. `key j` to an off-screen card centres it to within 2 pt (`dump`). **Create**: `<drag>; key 3; key return; type x; key cmd-return` makes a blue comment. The same steps after `box …` make a box comment. **Edit**: `note <id>; type z; card <other>` saves the note. `note <id>; type z; key esc; key esc` leaves it unchanged. **Re-anchor**: `cardmenu <id> Re-anchor…; <drag>; key return` moves the anchor. **Changes tab** (§16): `tab changes; key j; key v; dump` marks the first change checked and selects the next one still to check (on a checked one V clears it and stays); `change <id>; key return` opens the new-comment editor on its words (`editing: popover`). **Finish** (§17): `card R2-08; key r; type …; key esc; finishsheet` lists the draft under `drafts` with the email in `preview`; `finishsheet send R2-08` sends it (`followUps` up by one, the draft gone, `Follow-ups (1)` in `preview`, no card selected); `finishsheet show R1-21` closes the sheet and selects R1-21; `finishsheet discard <id>` drops a draft; `finishsheet finish` closes the round (`roundClosed`) and copies the email (`copied`). **Comment menu**: `menubar Comment/Draw a Box Round a Figure, Table or Equation (B, or ⌥-drag); dump` arms box mode (`boxArmed`). **Phase 0** (POLISH §0): `tab changes; changecomment c04` (`\input` renamed) and `changecomment c08` (a figure removed, no place) open no editor and say "This edit prints no words in the PDF to select. Select text near it to comment there."; `paperclick <other copy>` switches papers, and with a new comment's note typed (`changecomment c02; type …`) it leaves the paper and the list's selected row on the first copy, with the toast; `close` then gives the question "Your new comment on p.7 is not saved", and the `--background` instance quits; `card R2-08; button resolve; card R2-08; key return; type …; close` saves the remark before quitting (the ledger has it); in a note editor a click on Cancel keeps the cursor in it and only arms Discard changes, which then discards; Save saves.

**Unit tests (`swift test`)** cover the pure functions: `MarkHit.hits(at:)` (order, cycling, minimum size), `CardContent.thread` (the §3 table: sections by the engine's signals, tags, labels, what was said and its bubbles, underlines against the section above and the shown extent at either end, the attached edits' words, deleted and restored, position uncertain and the click-note exemption, crops and the faint box, fallbacks; the mockup's examples a–i, a shown extent that grows with an attached edit and shrinks again, a deletion typed in again), `CardContent.turns`, `fold`, `clamped` and `notePlace` (`CardTurnsTests`, UI v2.2: each row of CARD §3.2 and §3.4 and the mockup's cases, the figure with a follow-up, a kept-as-is note, revised with a note, an edit elsewhere now and in an earlier round, the quiet lines and the shown round's four wordings, moved, deleted and restored, not found and found again, the student before you in a round, your answer, an earlier round's view, new comments and "you marked", no count, "the student", bare and multi-line notes, folding by turns, the length rule with CJK, no gendered pronoun; after the reviews of 2026-10-19, a move with only the move attached ("moved it", hollow, the row "moved" or "moved in R2"), the shown round's quiet line after "moved" and after "you marked", "deleted it" with no count, an edit attached where the engine took the turn, and the drawn label's count kept together by no-break spaces; and, on an off-screen card, that Focus neither folds nor cuts and that the fold's line wraps with a long name), trimming, the rendered tint whatever the kind, the word diff with split punctuation and its ends outside the marks, `ScrollDecision` (none, minimal, centre or a tall card's end for R; animated or instant), `CardGroups` (the groups by status, the selected card held in its group, a folded group skipped, the next card to check) and, on the synthetic fixture, J/K skipping the folded group, a closed card's selection showing it and a held card going back when left; the panel table's 30 pt lines, touching rows, and the expanded card kept in place when rows above it leave, the §9 routing and key names, `VerdictPlan` (keys, buttons, the choice row), `AnswerHistory` (undo and redo; typing drops a card's steps), `DraftStore` (in a temporary folder; a fixture run's elsewhere), the status words, and the palette (the papers list's badge grey since POLISH §3 row 7); `SelectionOutline` (one contour round several lines, runs that do not overlap or go on in the next column apart), `Geometry.labelPlace` at three sizes, `ChangeModel` (the thread's labels and dots, grey strikes, the comment's colour on a linked change), `ChangesHeader`, `FinishSummary` and the email preview's styling, and the window subtitle; and, with an expanded card in an off-screen window, that the follow-up field takes the cursor only when asked. UI v2.1 (`FlowTests`): `ClickGuard`; For the student left out of the listed cards while folded; on the table, the next card in the answered card's slot (below it, and an earlier one brought up), a J/K step keeping its row in place, rows dimmed only while a card is expanded with no look changing on a J/K step, the Changes list not dimming, and a dimmed row drawn differently from a plain one and from a hovered one; the row under the pointer (`RowHoverTests`): one at most and only where the pointer is (a move from row to row needs no exit), none over the expanded card or a floating header, cleared by the list's exit, a scroll (the pointer's moves ignored until it settles, then the row under the still pointer; a live scroll until it ends), rows that come or go (not a content change), the window resigning key and the app resigning active, found again when the window becomes key, and a J/K step changing no look with the pointer off the list and only the two whose hover changed with it on; on the fixture, an answer moving on (the answered card to its group, the slot mode, clicks on the new card ignored for 0.4 s, keys never), no move when none is left or the selection moved, For the student folded by default (J/K skip it, a mark click shows it, Hide drops its selection), the toast's Undo, the toast kept while the pointer is on Undo and taking clicks only there (a click on its words reaches the view under it), and Comment on it… on an edit without words; `QuitOnClose` (the window stays, every other delegate message forwarded); and no key letters on buttons or fields (`KeyHintTests`). UI v2.1 phase 1 (`DesignTests`): the AccentColor asset is Sage and build_app.sh compiles and names it; contrast (white on the accent, secondary 5.2:1, accent text, errors, To check; tertiary below 4.5:1); CIEDE2000 (Sharma's reference pairs) between the accent and every colour that means something, and the known closeness of To check's amber to the yellow and orange marks; the app's surfaces at LCH chroma ≤ 2; the Dark Mode tints; `NoteTitle` (sentences in English and Chinese, abbreviations, a line break, the long case); Finish Round… ready only when nothing is left; and `SelectionOutline` with its white gap (`StepTests`). UI v2.1 phase 2 (`SidebarTests`): the Contents' numbers (digits, an appendix run from A, "A Note…" and "Stage 2" left alone) and names; the status line's words and buttons, open, all read, closed and in round 1; the bolder bar of the paragraph Next went to; a bar's click area (8 view points, never on the text); the window's minimum width; a new section redrawing only the rows it leaves and enters (`ContentsRow` bodies in an off-screen window); and, on the reading fixture, the Contents and the status line, a paper switch clearing them, a click on a bar marking it read (and nothing on a read one), Next and Mark Read without a toast or with the mouse-first hint. UI v2.1 phase 3 (`ScreensTests`, `FinishTests`): on the fixture, the done card heading the list only once nothing is left, its button opening the sheet, waiting while the last card answered is held in To check, and gone in a closed round; the empty state alone in an open round without comments and the hint's mouse words; the done card and the empty state measured (the table); the tip under a card to check, not on an answered card, until Got it, and not remembered by a test run; the editor taller with the selected words, at most three lines; the Finish sheet's Left for you (each to-do only when some are left, singular and plural) and Goes to the student; the Changes header without its number, in the label colour; the reworded quit question. UI v2.1 phase 4 (`FocusJumpTests`): Jump's search (the groups in order with the find row last, a comment found by its id, its note, the words it is on, or what was written this round, every word needed, sections by number or name, rounds, commands, no query giving the rounds and commands, at most eight comments, the snippet round the word, the query's words marked) and its model (the highlight wrapping, the query kept); on the fixture, Focus answering in place and only with a PDF (leaving when a state comes without one), the bar following the selection only in Focus (replaced after POLISH §7, below), no panel scroll in Focus and a centred card when it ends, R and editing a note leaving Focus (likewise), Mark checked staying in Focus and moving on in Review, F in the key map (not in the choice row), showing the sidebar ending Focus, a J step redrawing the bar (`FocusBar` in an off-screen window), the page 1.3 times larger in the layout of its new width with the place a third of the way down kept both ways, Jump's rows and commands, and the find in the paper with Next and Previous wrapping; and the inline editor measured apart from its shown text (`InlineEditorMeasureTests`). After the review fixes also: the choice row's keys yield to a closed round, re-anchoring, a selection or a box; selectable text does not hold the keys; on the fixture, the choice row going with the open round and with a selection or re-anchoring, a remark being edited saved before an answer, a cleared card's remark kept as its draft, Hide dropping its selected card, no amber in a closed round; the row words; a move not underlined (`CardContent.edits`); four label sizes; and the hint row measured again after a resize. After the UI v2.1 reviews (2026-10-18; `FlowTests`, `FinishTests`, `StateDecodingTests`): the answer row wrapping with Keep as is at the right edge (`AnswerRow.frames`); the Finish sheet's handled rows staying in the drafts' order, a refused send shown unsent, and Goes to the student leaving out empty groups; the subtitle and the papers line saying the changed paragraphs to read when nothing is left to check (not in round 1); and, on the fixture, the last card to check ignoring a double click's second click and a cleared answer taking its toast away, the toast's Undo and Redo ignoring a double click's second click, a paper switch taking the toast and saving an open note edit, a follow-up written after leaving Focus going back to Focus on the same card (and another card dropping the return), the sheet's Discard keeping its row, ignoring a second click within 0.4 s and undone there, Mark checked moving on in the checked change's place with a guarded second click (not in Focus), and finishing showing the folded group of the selected card.
