After the advisor's use (2026-10-18, POLISH §7; `AdjustTests`, `FocusJumpTests`, `FlowTests`): Focus's panel listing only the selected card (the comment list and the Changes list), "focus hint" with none, the next card alone after J and the clicked mark's card, the whole list again when Focus ends, the card at the top of a list that was scrolled down, R and editing a note keeping Focus, a follow-up written in Focus staying on its card, the first-run tip waiting for Review, and the Focus symbol (`doc.plaintext`, which exists, also in Jump); the group headers' signs, counts, quieter words and Show / Hide (both tabs; no count on To check; Left open grey), the gaps above all but the first header and under the done card (`PanelTable.spaced`), 34 pt headers and 10 pt gaps, a click on a folded group's name folding it once (a double click's second click ignored, through `HeaderHost`'s own tracking), no click on a header that does not fold, and the hover fill only on one that folds; a dimmed row drawn faded in Light and Dark Mode, its note and its page alike, and restored under the pointer (measured on the drawn row); and every answer and comment action refused while the engine is busy with "Still loading…", nothing sent then or afterwards, J still moving and the choice row's Cancel acting. Student notes (`StudentNoteTests`): the engine's keys decoding and older states without them; each round's note in its section after the round's text, in a tag round's section above, in earlier rounds, a bare note, the fold's count; the row's sign; the card drawing the note; a linked edit on the Changes tab and the warnings line and list. After the review fixes (2026-10-18): an edit a note alone linked outside the commented text making no section and no underline there, with "· 1 edit" on the shown round's note; an earlier round of a box "new image file" only by `history[].figures` (the old guess without them); and the Changes tab naming the note's line next to the edit. UI v2.2 fixes (`PanelV22Tests`): no header left over Focus's one card, also when Focus begins during the scroll to the card (`ScrollAnimator` within the content); the status line's page after a programmatic scroll back; one ring round a point note and its label; a trim never cutting a changed run (and two pieces meeting over one); the ignored note nearest an edit within 3 lines in its file; places cut at their end; Edit Note and Re-anchor… while the engine works (Delete still waits); the link colour's contrast and distance and every inline link on `LinkStyle`; the notes popover's words; the pager (one page shown and taking the clicks, an animated switch moving layers only and hiding the page left, a switch turned round, each list keeping its place, a hidden list keeping no hover and not placing the toast); the panel in a window with both lists from the start, the slide, and Reduce Motion at once; the toast above the one header it would cover, and over a pinned answer row at once; the drafts list's scroll bar; the panel's opening width and only a divider drag remembered (a real split view squeezed by its window, then its divider dragged with mouse events); the sidebar going to its top. The floating header crash (`FloatingHeaderUpdateTests`, 2026-10-18): a step whose collapsing card comes first, one that leaves the list shorter than the view, rows going at the list's end and a card getting shorter there never scroll the list inside the table's update while a header floats (scrolls seen from the table's own row data), the headers float again after, and the list ends where it should. Crops in ink (`CropLookTests`, 2026-10-20, §4): the look by environment (math, tables, algorithms ink; figures, drawings and the boxes round a figure's pictures as printed), without one (a box on no words as printed, a box on words ink), a change's (a replaced picture file, Figure/Fig., an `\input` of a figure, LaTeX that draws one; Table), carried on a thread's and a change's crops, a note on an empty area as printed; the paper taken out (white none, black opaque, grey a faint black, pink a fifth of red, any colour laid on white again the same) and Dark Mode's ink (black the label colour, white none, hue kept and lightness turned round for six colours, a navy light) on a padded bitmap; the density (not laid out, a wide card on Retina, half-pixel steps, the floor, 8 and 4096 px); the mark's colours (a figure the same in either mode, the fill on a dark ink crop, the deletion greens); and the pipeline on a synthetic PDF page (paper white and the mark in its ink; ink transparent where the page prints nothing or white, its black and red, a grey row faint; Dark Mode's light black, red, faint light band and no black box; the size at the density asked for; a later round's mark at 40%).

**Live checks** use private papers that stay outside the repository and never enter its tests. Screenshots are taken only with `tools/shot.sh --pid` of a `--background` instance.

## 14. Deviations from the brief
- **Kinds on selected text** are highlight, underline and strikeout. A sticky note is placed at a point (right-click or ⌥-click), as in Preview and Acrobat.
- **The shown round's attached edits are underlined wherever they are** (§3; not a move, nor an edit a student's note alone linked outside the commented text, `elsewhere`), also outside the mark at either end, where the comparison with the section above leaves words alone: in one review the student answered "motivate this first" with a sentence before the commented one, which was otherwise not marked. Earlier rounds have only their change ids, so the rule for the ends holds there.
- **A crop's click zooms** in every section (the old Now crop revealed the mark), as printed (§4); `p.5` reveals it.
- **A row click scrolls minimally**, so the row under the pointer stays put. Centring is for J/K and PDF clicks.
- **An inline note edit commits on focus loss**, the Finder-rename and Xcode-inspector convention; Esc discards. The new-comment popover keeps M1's rule.
- **Carried comments can be recoloured, but their kind and note stay.** Colour is the advisor's own sorting aid; kind and note were sent to the student.
- **The status colours are text and signs on the card**, not fills under white text: To check `#B45309` / `#F59E0B`, Not found `#C42121` / `#F98B8B` and the neutral grey reach 4.5:1 on the card in either mode (`PaletteTests`).
- **The student's note is in the grey bubble the advisor's words are in** (§3; UI v2.2, CARD §3.5): the advisor first suggested "a quiet outlined quote labelled Student · R3", but an outlined note right above the outlined follow-up field read as a second field (2026-10-18), and quotation marks hung in the indent set it apart as no one's turn. Who said what is the turn's label and dot; green stays for changes to the paper.
- **Crops come from the app**, drawn read-only from each round's build PDF, not from engine crop files. Nothing is written.

## 15. Engine-side data the cards need
1. **Colour and kinds**: comments get `color` (yellow, teal, blue, pink, purple or orange; missing means yellow; `green` from M2a ledgers reads as teal); `kind` adds `underline` and `box`; `comment` takes `color`; `edit_comment` takes optional `note`, `kind` and `color`. `note` and `kind` are allowed only for comments created in the open round, and `color` for any comment in it.
2. **`reanchor(paper, id, page, rects, text, kind?)`**: For a new comment, it replaces the creation anchor, located as `comment` does. For a carried comment, it replaces the open round's record with method `advisor` and confidence 1.0. The state is `moved` if the printed text equals the previous round's, otherwise `changed`. Changes are re-attached, and later rounds carry from this record (DESIGN §5).
3. **`context`** on each round record: `{"text", "sel": [[a, b], …], "cut": int|null, "display": "text"|"image", "page", "rects"}`. It is computed at create, carry and re-anchor, and stored in the ledger. **`text`**: the whole sentence(s) around the anchor as that round's PDF prints them (`texdiff.sentence` via `to_pdf`), with no margin numbers, hyphens joined and whitespace collapsed. A heading, caption or list item is taken whole. At most 1200 characters, cut around the anchor. **`sel`**: the anchor's PDF words located in `text`, matched on letters and digits. **`cut`** (gone): the deletion point; `text` then holds the sentences on either side. **Orphan**: `context` is null. **`display`**: `"image"` for a box, display math, `tabular`, `tikzpicture`, `algorithm`, figure content outside `\caption`, or when no PDF word matched.
4. **State**: comments gain `color` and `context`; `history[]` entries gain `page`, `context` and (2026-10-17) `changes`, the changes attached in that round; `rounds[]` gains `pdf` (the absolute build PDF path, for crops); for older ledgers, contexts are computed once per engine process, as `text_x` is, so `state` stays around 0.1 s.
5. **Box comments**: `kind: "box"`, with the box as `rects` and the words inside it (or "") as `text`. The anchor is the innermost figure, table or equation environment at the box centre (`selection`, 1.0). Failing that, it is the nearest line (`line`, 0.5, `position_guessed`). A box comment is never refused for having no words.
6. **Verdict note**: `verdict` with the current verdict and a new `note` updates the note; it does not clear the verdict.
7. **final.pdf and import**: final.pdf burns each mark into the page in the comment's colour and kind (a highlight's fill at opacity 0.35), with its text in a review margin, so Preview and print show it (CONTRACT amendment "Final PDF in the margin style"). final.pdf has no PDF annotations (2026-10-16): a reader showed each note a second time, in a popup beside its margin box; the outline's `Comments (k)` lists the comments instead (CONTRACT amendment "final.pdf without annotations"). Import maps Underline and Squiggly to underline, Square and Circle to box, and colours to the nearest palette entry.
8. **M2**: a `reply` per comment for the open round.
9. **Student notes** (CONTRACT amendment "Student notes", 2026-10-18): `comments[].student_note` and `history[].student_note`, `{text, file, line, changes, elsewhere, lines}` or null (the advisor's `note` is unchanged; `elsewhere` and `lines: [{file, line, changes}]` since the review fixes, read as [] when absent); `changes[].student_notes`, the comments whose note of the shown round attached the change; top-level `note_warnings`, `[{kind, ids, file, line, text, message}]`; `history[].figures`, the attached changes of that round that replaced a picture file (nil from an engine before it: the thread guesses as before); `history[].change_pages` (2026-10-19, for the card's turns), the page each attached change of that round is on, so an earlier round's edit elsewhere reads "· 1 edit on p.6" (nil before it: the page is left out). A state from before notes has none of them and reads as without notes (`StudentNote`, `NoteWarning`).


## 16. Changes tab
2026-10-15, after the advisor's M2a feedback: the tab stuttered, and its purpose was unclear (raw LaTeX, file:line, no grouping).

**Purpose.** The tab lists everything the student changed since the previous round. What matters are the edits linked to no comment (**unrequested**): nobody asked for them, so the advisor checks each one. Edits linked to comments are the evidence behind those comments' cards; they are judged there.

**Fix round, 2026-10-16** (reviews: the tab read as a list of everything, its count was of every edit, and a comment on an edit did not count): the tab is about the edits no comment asked for. The tab control reads `Changes 3` (design/ui-v2, 2026-10-17; it was `Changes · 3 to check`), nothing after `Changes` when none is left. An unrequested change is **handled** when it is checked or a comment of this round asks about it (`commented_by`, CONTRACT amendment "Fix round M2b"), or, unless it is a move, when every paragraph it touches is marked read (§18); handled rows are drawn at 60%.

**Header** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §3, 2026-10-17: each count said once; POLISH §3 row 10, §4.4): one sentence under the tabs, 12.5 pt secondary, its first words Semibold in the label colour: "**Other edits to check.** No comment asked for them. 3 more edits answer your comments." ("**An edit to check.** No comment asked for it." for one). The number of edits to check is the tab's, said once there, and the words are not amber (POLISH §6, 3; it was "2 edits to check." in amber, with "They were made since R2"). When none is left: "**All checked.** The 4 edits no comment asked for are checked, commented on or read. 3 more edits answer your comments."; with none unrequested: "**Nothing to check.** Every edit since R2 answers one of your comments." A closed round asks for nothing: "Edits made since R1: 2 no comment asked for, 3 answering your comments." In round 1 the list says "Round 1 has nothing to compare with.", with no changes "The source did not change.", and there is no sentence (`ChangesHeader`).

**The unrequested colour** (2026-10-16; the advisor found M1's purple `#7C3AED` uncomfortable) is a quiet slate teal, `Palette.unrequested`: `#355F6C`, and `#9CC3CF` in Dark Mode, for the kind words, the row bars and the hint (the number of edits to check is the tab's, in its plain text); the PDF band is `#355F6C` at 70% on the white page in either mode. As text it is 7.0:1 on white and 5.9:1 on `#ECECEC`, 8.8:1 on `#1E1E1E` and 6.8:1 on `#323232` (the purple was 2.9:1 in Dark Mode). Its nearest colours are the teal mark's ink and the neutral status grey (then Won't fix's), both ΔE00 ≈ 15 (the purple was 5.3 from the purple mark's ink); every other mark and status colour is at least 17 away, and the attached band and the changed and removed words at least 38.

**Groups**, with floating section headers as on the Comments tab (since 2026-10-17 in the v2 style there: no capitals, Show › / Hide ⌄; since POLISH §7 the same 34 pt header with its sign, 14 pt name, hairline and gap, the folded group's whole header its button, §1), without numbers, which the sentence above says:
1. **To check**, first; its sign the amber ○. It reads **No comment asked for**, with the grey ○, in a closed round, and also once every edit no comment asked for is handled (the sentence above says "All checked."), since then nothing on the tab is the advisor's move (code review 2026-10-24, UI-02: the amber "To check" stayed under "All checked."; `ChangeListController.groupRows`, the same `handled` rule as the tab's count).
2. **Answering your comments** (`text.bubble`), folded: its header has Show / Hide, and only the header is listed until shown. Selecting one of its changes (a click in the PDF, a chip) shows the group; Hide with one of them selected leaves no change selected.

Within a group, rows follow the PDF: page, then the left column before the right, then top. Checked rows stay in place.

**Compact row** (exactly 44 pt, as a comment row since design/ui-v2: flat, rows touching, a light fill under the pointer), with a 3 pt bar 6 pt inside its left edge in the unrequested colour, or in the colour of the comment it answers.
- Line 1 (18 pt): an edit no comment asked for leads with its kind as glyph and word, 12 pt in the unrequested colour (`pencil` Edited, `plus` Added, `minus` Deleted, `arrow.up.and.down` Moved, `photo` Figure), then the place (12 pt, secondary: `§ Limitations`, the heading the change is under, or `Table 2` for a change in a table or figure, named as printed, 2026-10-16; cut at its end, "§ Q3: How Does Information Availabi…", so the heading's start reads: BACKLOG O32, it was cut in the middle; on the expanded card too) and the chips of this round's comments that ask about it; an edit that answers comments leads with their chips instead (at most 2, then `+n`; it fixes the bare "For" of before), then the student's note sign when a note linked it to a comment (§3). On the right a grey ✓ when checked, and `p.8` (11 pt).
- Line 2, one line, serif: a short readable excerpt from the PDFs, never LaTeX. Edited, both sides text: the word diff of the two sentences (`WordDiff`), kept to the runs that touch the change's own words (`sel`, `cut`; all runs when none does, so a `sel` on the wrong words does not hide the edit), with 3 words around: old words struck through in grey, new words underlined (in the unrequested colour, or green for an edit that answers a comment), `…` where cut. Otherwise: the old words struck through, `→`, the new words. Added: the new words. Deleted: the old words struck through. Moved: the moved words. Figure: "New image file: <file>". A table or formula cell: its old and new printed text. An edit that prints the same words: "Same words in the PDF (formatting)". When the engine says what a change is (`about`, 2026-10-16), its line instead: an `\input` or `\include` line as what happened to the file it reads ("Section file sections/07_conclusion.tex renamed to sections/08_conclusion.tex", "Figure 'drop_two_panel' removed"), and an edit that prints no other text as "Formatting only: no printed text changed in §4.1" (secondary).
- Checked rows are drawn at 60% opacity.

**Expanded row** (the selected one only), a raised card like a comment's (§2: white with a soft shadow, the card colour with a hairline in Dark Mode, and since POLISH §3 row 10 no bar at its edge: the change's colour is the rule beside this round's text), 4 pt below the row above and 8 pt above the next, top to bottom:
1. **Header** (20 pt): the kind as a chip (its glyph and word on the chips' quiet fill; in the unrequested colour, or secondary for an edit that answers comments), the place; on the right `p.8`, which reveals it, and ⓘ, a popover with `main.tex:60 · replace` and the change's id (M1's footer).
2. **Why it is here** (12.5 pt): "No comment asked for this edit." in the unrequested colour ("… You marked it checked." or "… Its paragraphs are marked read." in secondary once settled, and in secondary in a closed round, where nothing is asked); "No comment asked for this edit; you asked about it in" and the chips of the comments that ask about it; or **Answers** and the chips of the comments it answers (they replace M1's bare "For"). The chips open those cards. An edit the student linked with a note (`% R1-03 …` next to it, CONTRACT amendment "Student notes") answers that comment the same way: it is not one of the other edits to check, and the thread ends with the note under the shown round's text, "R3 · student's note" (" on R1-03" when the edit answers several comments), then the words as on the comment's card: the same grey bubble, as written, no quotation marks, the note sign and the line in its footer, the full path in the help tag (UI v2.2, CARD §3.9; §3); the line is the note's line next to this edit (`student_note.lines`), not its first, when the note has several; a bare one has the sign and the line only, its help tag saying what it is.
   Under it, when a student's note the engine did not use (§3 "Notes not used") is within 3 lines of the edit in its file (`change.file`, `line`; the nearest one), one secondary line says so (BACKLOG O32): the student's note sign and "Ignored note 1 line above: % R2.08 same claim as in the abstract…" ("2 lines below", "on its line"), the line as written in monospace, cut at its end; its help tag has where it is and the engine's reason (`ChangeModel.IgnoredNote`, `IgnoredNoteLine`). The student perhaps meant it for this edit (the demo's `% R2.08` beside "restricting" → "which can restrict"), which the advisor can take up in a follow-up.
3. **The thread** (§3's sections, rail and type): **R1 · before** (a hollow dot) and **R2 · revised** (a filled green dot; "added", "deleted", "moved", "new image file"), the page named only when it is not the change's: the full sentence(s) as each round's PDF prints them, the round before New York 13/20 beside a 2 pt grey rule, the shown round's (and a move's or same-words section) 14/21 beside a 3 pt rule in the change's colour, slate teal or the colour of the comment it answers, as on a comment's card (POLISH §3 row 10, §3 above), the paper's other words quieter. An edit whose sides are both text marks the runs of the row's word diff; otherwise the change's own words (`sel`, `cut`). Before it, they are struck through in grey; after it, underlined in the unrequested colour, or green for an edit that answers a comment, and a small triangle marks where words were deleted, as on a card (an insertion point before it is not drawn: a triangle means words removed). Figures, tables and math are crops of each round's PDF (§4), with the point of an insertion or deletion drawn as the dashed line of a deleted table row. Long text is trimmed as on cards, with "Show all".
   - A move whose text reads the same on both sides is one section, "R2 · moved from p.7", unmarked.
   - An edit whose sentences read the same is one section, "R2 · same words in the PDF".
   - A side with no PDF text (an `\input` line, a whole file) falls back to the source, "R1 · before, in the source", in monospace, unless the engine says what the change is (`about`): then that line stands under the header, and there is no source section; a deletion with nothing printed near it reads "R2 · deleted", "Nothing is printed near it."; a figure or table whose `\input` was removed, added or moved is a crop of it in each round that prints it (a removed figure: before only).
4. **Actions** (open round only), 12 pt below, as the cards' answer buttons: [✓ Mark checked] and [Comment on it…] (their help tags name V and Return; POLISH §6); when checked, the first reads [✓ Checked], pressed in, and pressing it again clears it. Green is no longer used for checked (neutral, design/ui-v2).

**Actions.**
- **Click or J/K**: the row expands, the panel scrolls (§6, minimal for a click, centred for J/K), and the PDF scrolls to the change and flashes it. While it is selected, its lines keep the selection's ring (2 pt accent with the paper's white inside it, §5; a 1.5 pt outline before POLISH §2.2). The flash and the outline are views over the PDF (`OutlineView`), not annotations, so no page is redrawn.
- **Clicks in the PDF**: on the Changes tab, id labels first, then a change's lines (each + 2 pt with its band, at least 8 view points), then the comments' marks. On the Comments tab, the comments' marks first; a click on a change's band then switches to the Changes tab and selects it.
- **Comment on this change**: selects the change, then its new words in the PDF, and opens the new-comment editor with them as the quote (an image change gets a pending box, a deletion a note at its point). An edit that prints no words there (an `\input` renamed, formatting only, a removed figure with no place) selects nothing and opens nothing; the PDF shows where it is, if anywhere, and says "This edit prints no words in the PDF to select. Select text near it to comment there." (POLISH §0: it did nothing, silently; the button, disabled for an edit with no place, is always enabled now). Saving keeps the Changes tab and the change selected; its row then shows the new comment's chip and counts as handled. A comment saved while no change is selected shows its card on the Comments tab, as before.
- **Mark checked** (V): a per-round flag in the ledger (`check_change`). It applies to any change; the header counts the unrequested ones. V, or a click on Mark checked (in Review; interaction review 2026-10-18: the click did not move on, against the mouse-first rule), then selects the next unrequested change still to check (in list order, wrapping) in the checked one's place (`ScrollMode.slot`, as an answer moves on, §6), and its buttons ignore clicks for 0.4 s (`ClickGuard`), so a double click checks one change (it checked the change and cleared it again); clearing stays, and so does Focus.
- **Switching tabs**: the shown tab's expanded card or change becomes the selection, so V, Return and J/K act on what is expanded, and only it is outlined in the PDF.
- **Finish Round** lists "Other edits not checked" with their count and [Review], which selects the first (§17). The app's words are "no comment asked for" and "other edits", never "unrequested" (audit, 2026-10-17, after Codex's "Other changes").

**Keys** on the Changes tab: J/K next and previous listed change; V marks the selected change checked and moves on, or clears it; Return comments on it (with text selected in the PDF, Return comments on the selection, as anywhere).

**Toolbar**: the PDF toggle for the change marks is called **Change marks** (it was "Changes", like the tab), an icon (`highlighter` since POLISH §2.6; `text.redaction` from design/ui-v2). The cards' underline toggle is View › Underline Changed Words (POLISH §6, 11; it was a `U` button on the Comments tab).

**Engine data** (CONTRACT amendment "Changes tab"): each change has `section`, `checked` and `context: {before, now}`, two card contexts computed at ingest from the previous and the current round's PDF.

**Performance.** The list is the comment list's table (§10): fixed 44 pt compact rows, each one hosting view, and one expanded view laid over the selected row. Rows never observe the app model. Change models, with their rendered text, are built once per applied state.


## 17. Earlier rounds, the papers list and Finish Round
2026-10-16 (BACKLOG F7: only the latest round could be seen in the app; F6: the papers list did not say what each paper waits for). Engine data: CONTRACT amendment "Earlier rounds and next steps".

**Round menu.** The toolbar's round label, `Round 3 ⌄` (it was `Round 3 · open ⌄`: the round is named once, design/ui-v2 §6 addition 10), is a menu of every round, oldest first, each with the date its source came in and its status: `Round 1 · Oct 14 · closed`, `Round 3 · Oct 16 · open` (or `build failed`), the round shown ticked. Picking a round shows it; picking the latest goes back.

**An earlier round** is shown as it was left: its build PDF with its marks, its comments with that round's verdicts and notes, their threads ending at that round, its Changes tab with what was checked then, and its own build status line (§1). A selected comment stays selected when it is in that round, and the PDF shows its mark there (R1-16 is on p.7 in round 1 and on p.16 in round 2). A neutral banner across the top of the panel (design/ui-v2: it was an accent-coloured one across the paper view) reads "Round 1 · closed Oct 15 · read only" with [Back to Round 3] (§1). The toolbar's [final.pdf] (shown for any closed round) opens that round's final.pdf in the default PDF app; its menu has Show in Finder and Copy Email Text (the email.txt the engine still writes; the [Email…] button and its sheet went on 2026-10-26, §32). The round menu's Round 1 Summary… shows the round's card (§32). Nothing can be edited: no verdicts, notes, styles, re-anchoring or new comments, and of the keys only J/K act. Closed rounds' headers state what was left without asking for action: "Left open" for the comments without an answer, "For the student · 9 · in the Round 2 final.pdf", no "press V" on the Changes tab, and no number on either tab (§1). New Round… is about the latest round, so it shows from an earlier round when the latest is closed, and then shows the new one.

**Finish Round** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §3 and mockup §7, 2026-10-17; it replaces the confirmation alert and the email sheet that followed it; since 2026-10-26 without the email, the round's card in its place, §32). Finish Round… in the toolbar (an open round with a PDF), or the done card's (§1), saves an open editor and shows one sheet, 920 × 640, on the sheet colour (`Palette.sheet`, `#F6F6F3`; `#1E1F1E` in Dark Mode, so its grouped boxes, white or `#2A2C2A` with a 0.5 pt hairline, radius 10, stand out; POLISH §2.2): "Finish Round 3" (19 Bold) and "This writes final.pdf, then closes Round 3. A closed round is read only." (it said "cannot be changed" until Resume Round N, §31) It reads top to bottom as a hand-off (POLISH §3 row 11, mockup §9), in two equal columns; each section's name 12 Semibold in the second label colour with its quieter words after it; rows at least 38 pt, a sign in an 18 pt column, the words 13 pt with what they mean under them (11 pt, secondary), the count Semibold on the right, a hairline between rows from the words on (`FinishSummary.left`, `.goes`):
- **Left for you** · you can still finish: "○ Comments still to check · They go out as “still open”. [Review] 2" (Review closes the sheet and selects the first card to check), "✎ Other edits not checked · No comment asked for them. [Review] 2" (the first on the Changes tab), "▍Changed paragraphs not read · Finishing marks them read. [Show] 12" (Show closes the sheet and goes to the next one, as Next does); the signs amber, the bar olive; each only when some are left (the paragraphs only while reading marks are on, §18), and the section only when one is (so a round ready to finish starts with what goes out). Its buttons are 22 pt, 12 pt (`AnswerButtonStyle(small:)`).
- **Words not sent yet** · not in final.pdf unless you send them: each card to check whose field holds text, with its chip, the text in quotation marks, "Unsent draft · kept on this Mac", [↩ Send as follow-up] (sends it as the card's follow-up, as ⌘↩ would, without moving on), [Discard] and Show (a link: closes the sheet and selects it). A draft sent or discarded keeps its row where it was, the buttons replaced by "↩ Sent as a follow-up" or "Discarded" with Undo at the far right (it puts the words back), so the next draft's buttons never slide under the pointer; and for 0.4 s after one of them the sheet's draft buttons ignore clicks (`AppModel.finishDraft`, `FinishSummary.rows`; interaction review 2026-10-18: a double click sent or discarded a second draft the advisor had not chosen, and Discard could not be undone). Nothing goes out silently: a draft left here is dropped when the round is finished. When the column cannot show everything, the drafts scroll in a list of their own, so the rest of the column stays in view (visual review 2026-10-17: with three drafts the rest was cut off), with its scroll bar and track shown before any scroll (`AlwaysShownScroller`; BACKLOG O28: the overlay scroller showed only while it scrolled, so the list looked whole).
- **Goes to the student**, in final.pdf: "↩ Follow-ups · with your notes 3" (the words only when every follow-up has a note), "+ New comments 2", "✓ Closed 8", each only when there are some (visual review 2026-10-18: New comments 0 was listed and Follow-ups 0 not), or "No follow-ups, new or closed comments" when there are none; then, while reading marks are on, "Finishing marks every paragraph read, so the next round's bars show only what changes after it." ("Finishing Round 1 marks every paragraph read."). It replaces the batch box, "Unsent drafts" and "Also" of design/ui-v2.
- **On the right, the round's card so far** under "This round · your card when you finish" (§32): the compact card, "Round 3 so far", asked again with every engine reply, so a draft sent or an answer shows at once.
- **Buttons** (large): [Cancel] (Esc) and [Finish Round] (the default): the engine's `finish` writes final.pdf (and email.txt, which backups and exports keep, and the final.pdf menu's Copy Email Text copies) and closes the round; nothing goes on the clipboard, and the unsent drafts are deleted. The sheet then turns into the round's card (§32): "Round 3 is finished · final.pdf is ready · Show in Finder · Open"; its Done (or Esc) closes it and the window's line says "Round 3 is finished · final.pdf is ready" with [Show in Finder] and [Open] for 10 s (a sheet closed while the engine worked has the line at once). An engine error shows in red on the left of the buttons and the sheet stays. [final.pdf] in the toolbar and Round 3 Summary… show them again later.

**Switching.** An open inline editor is saved first, as when focus leaves it, for another paper too (interaction review 2026-10-18: a paper switch dropped it, plainly when no focus change came first: Add Existing…, New Paper…, Remove from List), to the paper it was begun on (an answer the engine gives after another paper is shown goes to its own paper's drafts and undo history, `AppModel.answered`). While the new-comment editor holds a note that is not saved, the menu stays and the PDF says why (§7), and the sidebar shows its selection again (in Review none: the open paper keeps its fill, §21; checked through the list's own selection, `paperclick`, POLISH §0); an untouched one closes. Change selections are dropped (change ids are per round).

**The sidebar** (POLISH §3 row 2, §6; 2026-10-18; since 2026-10-19 the papers are under their students, with All Students and Your Move above them and the tree folded to the open paper in Review: §22, which wins where this differs): one system sidebar holds the papers and the paper's Contents (§18), which were two columns. At its top, "Jump to…", a field-like button (magnifying glass, the placeholder words, 28 pt, radius 7; POLISH §6: the visible way to the Jump panel, which ⌘K opens too; `AppModel.openJump`, §20). Then **Papers**, the list below, with the open paper in the system's selection (the accent's while the sidebar has the keys, grey otherwise), and **Contents**, the sections, the one the PDF is in on a lighter fill of its own (`ContentsSection`), kept in view as the PDF scrolls with the least scroll, and with the sidebar at its top whenever its row fits in the first screen there, so Papers' header is in view again once the PDF is back near its start (`SectionFollower`, `SidebarFollow`; BACKLOG O30, §19: the least scroll had left the header, "○ 1 more to check", above the view until the sidebar was scrolled by hand); a half-day spike first confirmed that one list with one selection binding shows both at once and that a Contents row's click leaves the selected paper alone (its rows take no selection, `selectionDisabled`). Papers folds with the system's chevron on its header (remembered as `showPapers`, not by a `--background` run); folded, the header says "○ 2 more to check" when other papers wait for you (the toolbar names the open one; `AppModel.othersToCheck`, the papers with a badge). New Paper… and Add Existing… stay at the bottom. The group names, Papers and Contents, are 11 Semibold in the palette's secondary, and a paper's title is in the label colour (the system's when its row has the accent fill), both named: the system's header style, and a title left to the list, drew at 1.9:1 in a window without the keys, the title lighter than its own second line (visual review 2026-10-18; POLISH §2.3).

**Papers list** (design/ui-v2/PROPOSAL.md §3, 2026-10-17: whose move it is, in one line; since 2026-10-19 a paper's row is its title with the badge or a sign, its status in the help tag, under its student: §22). Each paper's title (Medium, one line), and under it one 11 pt line with an icon and the student's name, so it does not rely on colour: "○ To check · Mei Lin" (an amber circle, and beside the title a grey count badge, as the system's sidebars count (POLISH §3 row 7: an amber badge said "your move" a second time beside the amber circle; not on the open paper, whose counts the window's subtitle and the panel's tabs say, §4.4): carried comments to check, without an answer, plus unrequested edits still to check; the help tag splits the two: "2 comments to check · 3 other edits to check"; the digits secondary on the chips' quiet fill), "✓ Ready to finish · Priya Nair" (a neutral check circle: an open round from round 2 on with nothing left to check), "▍Paragraphs to read · Mei Lin" for the open paper whose comments and edits are done while changed paragraphs are left and reading marks are on (an olive bar; the app's count, which the engine's summary does not have, so another paper says Ready to finish then), "• Round 1 in progress · Aiko Sato" (round 1 has nothing to check), "⌛ Waiting for student · Mei Lin" (the latest round is closed), "⚠ Build failed · Jonas Berg" (the only red), "No rounds yet". Only to-check is amber and only a failed build red; no green ticks. The mockup's "Waiting for student · Oct 12" names the day the round closed; `summary` has no date, so the line names the student, as the others do. The selected paper's line follows every engine reply; the others come from one light `summaries` call at launch and whenever the app becomes active (a paper changed from the command line), never from a full state per paper.


## 18. Reading in the PDF view
2026-10-16. The advisor reads only the PDF, so the separate prose view (a web view of the paper as text, with P to switch, KaTeX and its own selection and comments) is gone; what it was for, confirming which paragraphs were read, now happens in the PDF. Engine data: CONTRACT amendments "Prose view and reading" (read state) and "Reading in the PDF view" (what changed).

**What is read.** Paragraphs, list items, reference entries, display formulas, figures, tables and listings, each with its place in the PDF. Section headings and the title and author block are not; the abstract is. A paragraph is read when its text is in the ledger's read set. The read set ignores re-wrapping, `%` comments, moves and commands that print nothing, so a paragraph turns unread exactly when its words change (or its figure file is replaced). A paragraph edited this round is read only once confirmed in this round, even if its text was read before.

**Optional, off by default** (the advisor's decision, 2026-10-18: the bars overlap the Changes tab, which lists every edit, the ones no comment asked for included, to be checked one by one). **Settings** (Paperole › Settings…, ⌘,; `SettingsView`, a SwiftUI `Settings` scene; since 2026-10-20 its **Reading** tab, §23) has one switch, "Mark changed paragraphs to read", with "A bar beside each paragraph changed since the last round, until you mark it read. The Changes tab lists every edit either way." under it, in a grouped form 460 pt wide. It is `AppModel.readingMarksOn`, kept in the app's defaults as `readingMarks` (absent: off). A test run never saves it: `--background` (as every view preference), a `--fixture` run (`swift test`) and `--reading on|off`, which sets it for the run (§13).
- **Off**: no bars in the PDF (nor their pointer and help tag, nor the bolder bar); no reading count, Next or Mark Read in the status line (the build and the page only); no olive dots in the Contents and no Mark Section Read / Unread on their rows (the rows, numbers, comment counts and the current section stay); no "Changed paragraphs not read" in the Finish sheet and no sentence about the bars under Goes to the student; no "changed paragraphs to read" in the window's subtitle and no "Paragraphs to read" in the papers list (`AppModel.unreadCount` is nil, so `withReading`, the done card and Finish Round…'s prominence ignore the paragraphs); N, C and Shift-C are not the app's keys (`KeyState.reading`), the Reading menu is not in the menu bar, the PDF's menu has no Mark Paragraph Read / Unread, and Jump has no Next Changed Paragraph to Read. Should anything still ask for them, it says "Reading marks are off: Settings turns them on." Focus is still the reading mode, without bars (§20). The Changes tab is unaffected (§16): its edits are checked there with V, and an edit whose paragraphs are all read still counts as handled, as in the engine's `summary`.
- **On**: everything below, as before the setting.
- **Switching** shows or hides them at once (the bars, the status line, the Contents, the subtitle, the papers list's line, the done card, the menu bar), from the engine's read state, which the setting never changes: the engine keeps it either way and finishing a round still marks every paragraph read, so turning the marks on in a later round shows only what changed since the last round closed (and any paragraph marked unread). Turning them off forgets the paragraph Next went to.

**Round 1** has no bars: every paragraph is new, and the advisor reads it all while commenting. Finishing round 1 marks every paragraph read. N, C and Shift-C say so instead of acting, the Contents show titles and comment counts only, and the status line says "Round 1: finishing it marks every paragraph read".

**Finishing any round** marks every paragraph read, as round 1 does (the advisor's decision, BACKLOG D5, 2026-10-16), so a bar always means "changed since the previous round": the advisor does not have to press C on every paragraph of every round.

**Bars** (round 2 on). Every paragraph not confirmed read gets a bar in the page margin beside it, so the advisor reads exactly the bars:
- **Which**: the paragraphs the student changed since the previous round, and any marked unread in this one. A changed paragraph left unread when round 2 was finished has no bar in round 3 unless the student changes it again. A move keeps its text, so it has no bar (the Changes tab still asks for V). A review folder whose round 2 was finished before this rule shows round 3 the same way: round 2's paragraphs count as read as of round 2.
- **Where**: 2 pt wide, 3 pt outside the text column (the page's `text_x`), as tall as the paragraph's lines, its last line included when that holds a single word: left of the text for one column and for the left column, right of it for the right column; a float across both columns gets the left margin.
- **Floats wrapped in text** get a bar of their own, so two blocks never look like one: on a one-column page a part starting right of the text's middle, or set beside a wider part and ending at the text's right edge (a `wraptable` or `wrapfigure` on the right, however wide), gets the right margin, away from the bar of the paragraph wrapped round it; a part set beside a wider one whose bar is on the same side (a float wrapped on the left, or two boxes that overlap) has its bar 4 pt further out, 2 pt clear of the other. "Beside": level with it by more than 2 pt, across more than half of it, and at most 90% as wide. Bars keep their places whichever paragraphs are read.
- A paragraph across a column or page break has a bar beside each part. Bars never cover text or a margin ruler (outside `text_x`); id labels placed between the text and a ruler keep 2 pt clear of them (not of a bar moved 4 pt out: the two meet only beside a wrapped float on a page whose labels sit between the text and a ruler).
- **Colour**: olive `#6E7D2A` on the page (white in either mode, 4.5:1), apart from the six mark colours, the status colours, the change greens and the slate teal; in the app's own views (the Contents' dots, the status line, the toast) `#6E7D2A`, Dark Mode `#A3B356`.
- **The paragraph Next went to** (N, or C under a selection) has a bolder bar, 3 pt instead of 2, widened away from the text (POLISH §3 row 3: no fill under it, which the Sage selection would be too close to), until it is read or another is gone to.
- **A click marks it read** (POLISH §6, mouse first; it was not clickable): in an open round the pointer over a bar is a pointing hand with the help tag "Mark read", and a click without a drag marks its paragraph read, in Review and in Focus (§20). The click area is the bar widened to 8 view points, 1 pt above and below, but at most 2.5 pt towards the text (`MarkHit.barArea`), so a click on the text stays a click on the text; id labels win over it, and it wins over the marks under it. No toast: the bar goes and the status line counts down (VoiceOver hears the count). In a closed or earlier round a bar is not clickable and has no help tag. Un-reading stays in the menus (below). Bars ignore the Change marks toggle.
- A bar disappears at once when its paragraph is confirmed; the engine's `read` follows (a refusal brings it back with an alert).

**Keys** (§9; in any review context, never while a text field has focus):

| key | does |
|---|---|
| N | The next paragraph with a bar, in the order the paper is printed (the engine's order of `reading.paragraphs`: page, then column, then down the page, so a float is read where it is set, not where its source is): after the one last gone to while it is in view, else from the first paragraph in view; wrapping. The PDF scrolls so the paragraph is fully visible (16 pt inside the view; the least movement when it is partly on screen, else it lands a third of the way down; one taller than the view gets its top 16 pt below the top; animated over 0.25 s within 1.5 view heights), then it flashes (§6's accent outline, round each part). |
| C | Confirms the paragraph under the PDF selection, else the one last gone to with N while it is in view. On a read paragraph it only flashes it and says so: one press too many never un-reads. |
| Shift-C | Confirms every paragraph of that paragraph's section: from its heading to the next heading at its level or above, subsections included (the title and abstract are one section). |

The status line's **Next** and **Mark Read** do what N and C do (their help tags name the keys; POLISH §6: no key letters on them).

- **Feedback**: the count is said once, in the status line (POLISH §3 row 3, §4.4), so N, C, Next, Mark Read and a bar's click that go or mark read show no toast (they posted "9 changed paragraphs to read · C marks this one read" and "Marked read · 8 to read · N for the next" until 2026-10-18); VoiceOver still hears the count ("9 changed paragraphs to read", "Marked read · 8 to read"). The rest still show a short message at the bottom of the PDF for 2.5 s (and post it to VoiceOver), so nothing does nothing silently: "This paragraph is already read.", "“4.2 Preliminary Results”: 1 paragraph marked read" (Shift-C; the count left is the status line's), "All changed paragraphs are read.", and why it cannot act: "Click Next to go to a changed paragraph, or select text in one first." (mouse first; it was "Press N …, then C."), "The selection is not in a paragraph to read (headings and the title are not).", "Round 1 has no changed paragraphs: finishing it marks every paragraph read.", "Round 2 is read only." (an earlier round, §17), "Round 3 is closed: its reading cannot be changed.", "The paragraphs are still being found…".
- **Menu**: the menu bar's **Reading** menu has Next Changed Paragraph to Read (N), Mark Paragraph Read (C) and Mark Section Read (⇧C). The keys are named in the titles but are not menu key equivalents: a menu item with a plain letter would take that letter from every text field and from the input method.
- **Un-reading** is in menus only: the PDF's right-click menu has Mark Paragraph Read / Mark Paragraph Unread for the paragraph under the click (round 2 on, open round; "Marked unread · its bar is back"), and a Contents row's has Mark Section Read / Unread.
- **Earlier rounds** (§17) show the bars of that round's paragraphs as the read set is now, which for a finished round are the bars it was finished with (finishing marks its unconfirmed paragraphs read from the next round on); N moves through them, C and Shift-C say the round is read only.

**Contents** (the sidebar's second section, §17; POLISH §3 row 2, §6 decision 7, 2026-10-18; it was the outline, a column of its own, toggled with the toolbar's `list.bullet.indent`).
- **Rows** (13 pt; POLISH §2.3, navigation with weight, nothing under 11 pt): "Title and abstract" first, then one per section and subsection heading (References included). The heading's number in a column of its own, secondary ("2.1", "A", "A.1"; `Outline.numbers`: a single capital letter only in a run of appendices from A, so "A Note on Noise" keeps its "A"), then the title: a section Medium, a subsection Regular in the second label colour, indented 24 pt; then a small olive dot (6 pt) when the section has changed paragraphs to read; on the right its open comments (`text.bubble` and a count of New, To check, Not found and Follow-up comments, 11 Medium secondary). No fractions, checkmarks or progress bars (POLISH §6, 7: "5/8" and the bar under the title read as a completion rate; the leading olive bar became the dot, so leading bars stay for mark colours). The title truncates first; its help tag has it whole. Deeper headings count into their section.
- **Current row** (the section the PDF is in): Semibold, on a light fill of its own (`primary` at 4.5%, lighter than the open paper's selection), following the PDF (the point a third of the way down the view), the row clicked or the section N went to. It lives outside the list's data (`CurrentSection`; POLISH §5): each row observes only its own look (`SectionLook`), so scrolling the PDF redraws the row left and the row entered, nothing else (§10.18); a follower keeps the row in the sidebar's view with the least scroll.
- **Click** scrolls the PDF so the heading is 16 pt below the top of the view (the title and abstract: the top of the first page; References without a place of its own: its first entry). The rows take no selection: the open paper keeps the list's.
- **No paragraphs yet** (a review folder from before, or after an engine update): the app asks the engine for them once per round (`prose`), and the Contents say "Finding the paragraphs…" meanwhile. A round without a PDF has no Contents.

**The status line's count** (POLISH §3 row 3; it was the outline's footer, and the toasts after N and C): on the right of the status line under the PDF (§1), "▍12 changed paragraphs to read · ↓ Next · ✓ Mark Read", the olive bar first, counting down as paragraphs are read, with no position beside it ("3 of 12" would mix with what is left); "✓ All changed paragraphs are read" with no buttons. It no longer leads with a completion rate ("171 of 183 read"): most paragraphs count as read because finishing a round marks them read, not because they were read in this one (audit, 2026-10-17). In a closed or earlier round it says what was left, which nothing can change now, with no buttons: "10 changed paragraphs left unread when Round 2 closed" ("All changed paragraphs were read in Round 2"; "Finishing Round 1 marked every paragraph read"); in round 1, "Round 1: finishing it marks every paragraph read". Narrower, "12 to read" (and "10 left unread", "All read"), then Next and Mark Read as icons with their names for VoiceOver (`ReadingStatusView`, a `ViewThatFits`; `Outline.status`). Only that part of the line observes the count (`ReadingStatus`).

**Changes tab and Finish** (§16).
- **Reading counts as checking**: an unrequested change that is not a move is handled when every paragraph it touches is read; its expanded row says "Its paragraphs are marked read". A change that touches no paragraph to read (a heading, a deleted paragraph) still needs V.
- **Finish Round** lists "Changed paragraphs not read" with their count, and says "Finishing marks every paragraph read, so the next round's bars show only what changes after it."; in round 1, "Finishing Round 1 marks every paragraph read." (§17)

**Debug hooks** (`--debug-script`, §13; in-process, and they work with `--background`):

| item | does | writes |
|---|---|---|
| `key n\|c\|shift-c` | the key, through §9's routing | as `key`, plus the `reading` result |
| `reading` | — | `{round, marks, tracked, current, currentFullyVisible, unread, bars: [[block, page, x0, y0, x1, y1]], visible, toast, flash, outlineCurrent, status, statusShort, statusButtons, pdf}` (`outlineCurrent`: the Contents' current section; `status`: the status line's count, which was `footer`; `statusButtons`: Next and Mark Read shown) |
| `outline` | — | the Contents: `[{block, title, number, name, level, read, count, open, changed, tracked, current}]` |
| `outlineclick <block>` | a Contents row's action (the PDF to its heading) | the `reading` result |
| `contentsclick <block>` | a click on that Contents row in the sidebar's own table, the mouse-down and -up to the row's view, as the mouse sends them (its SwiftUI tap gesture needs both) | the `reading` result, plus `{clicked, listSelected}` |
| `barclick <block>` / `barhover <block>` | a click on the middle of that paragraph's bar, as `click` does / the pointer moved there | the `click` result plus `bar` and the `reading` result / `{tooltip, cursor}` |
| `statusclick next\|read` | a click on the status line's Next or Mark Read, as the mouse sends it | the `reading` result plus `clicked` |
| `menubar [<menu>/<item>]` | picks that menu bar item first, if given (the menu updated first, as when it opens) | the `reading` result, plus the menu bar's titles, the Reading menu's items, the item picked with whether it was enabled, and `items`, the titles of the menu it was looked for in |
| `menu page,x,y <title>` | right-click, then the item with that title (Mark Paragraph Unread) | the menu (§13) |

**Tests.** `swift test` (`ReadingTests`, `SidebarTests`, `SettingsTests`): reading marks off by default and on (`ReadingSettingTests`: the `--reading` flag; off, no bars drawn, no status count, no dots or Mark Section Read, the subtitle's and papers list's step, no Finish row, N, C and Shift-C not routed while F, J and B are, no Mark Paragraph Read in the PDF's menu, no Jump command, the read state and the Changes tab's count unchanged, Focus, ready to finish with paragraphs unread; on, all of them; switching both ways, never saved; the Settings view's switch sets it); the Contents' rows, numbers and names, the status line's count and the current row (and that only its rows redraw); N's order (after the current paragraph, from the first in view, wrapping, the printed order when a float is set below later paragraphs); Shift-C's section ranges; the paragraph under a point; the block for the Contents' current row; the bars' place in one and two columns and beside a float wrapped on either side, clear of text and rulers, the bolder bar and a bar's click area; the keys in every context; on the fixture, a click on a bar; decoding the state's reading data (`Fixtures/state-reading.json`, round 2 of the engine's fixture paper).


## 19. Design language (UI v2.1 phase 1)
2026-10-18, `design/ui-v2.1/POLISH.md` §2 and §6 (decisions 1, 3–6, 10–13). Structure and rules are unchanged; the look changes. Built here: the palette's tokens and the accent, type and weights, the card (§2, §3), the rows and the papers badge (§2, §17), the tabs and groups (§1), the toolbar (§1), the selection's outline (§5). The sidebar and the reading in the status line came in phase 2 (§17, §18); Focus and Jump in phase 4 (§20).

**The accent test** (POLISH §2.2, decision 1: Sage if the outline, the olive bar, the change band and the text selection are told apart in both appearances, else the system blue). **PDFKit's text selection follows the app's accent** (Sage `#CDD3CC` on the page, the system's `#B3D7FF`), so `PDFSelection.color` is not set. ΔE00 measured (Sage Light / Dark; system blue in brackets):

| pair | Sage | system blue |
|---|---|---|
| outline ~ olive bar | 17.8 / 15.3 | (61.6) |
| outline ~ change band | 39.1 / 33.1 | (55.7) |
| outline ~ text selection | 35.3 / 31.1 | (26.2) |
| selection ~ olive bar / change band | 34.1 / 21.6 | (51.8 / 35.8) |
| selection ~ the nearest mark's fill on the page | 15.0 (teal) | (**4.1**, blue: a fresh selection looks like a blue comment) |
| outline ~ the nearest mark (fill or ink) | 13.1 / 12.9 (teal ink) | (15.8, blue ink) |
| outline ~ the blue mark's ink (its label) | 38.6 / 41.9 | (15.8) |
| outline ~ unrequested band, decided grey | 19.6 / 18.1, 18.9 / 21.2 | (19.4, 20.2) |
| white on the accent (the default button) | 5.8:1 / 4.75:1 | (4.0:1 / 3.6:1) |

Every pair is at least 12.9 apart with Sage, and white text on it passes AA in both modes; the system blue fails on the selection (4.1) and on white text. **Decision: Sage, `#566B58` / `#5D7A61`.** With an accent picked in System Settings, macOS uses that one. Seen on the screenshots: the white gap keeps the outline off the blue underline and the label; it also covers the top of the change band under the selected mark's last line while it is selected (§5).

**Tokens** (`Palette`, `Ink` for SwiftUI; Light / Dark):

| token | value | use |
|---|---|---|
| accent | AccentColor `#566B58` / `#5D7A61` (`Palette.sage`) | `controlAccentColor`, `Color.accentColor`: the selection's outline and the flash, focus rings, the default button, Finish Round… when ready, the floating toolbar's Comment |
| accent text | `#4D6150` / `#A5BFA7` | the current kind on the floating toolbar and the editor's style row, the first-run tip's lightbulb (links until UI v2.2) |
| link | the system's link colour (`NSColor.linkColor`, about `#0068DA` / `#419CFF`; it follows Increase Contrast) | links in words, `LinkStyle` (UI v2.2; BACKLOG O32: "· 1 edit", Details… and Show all in the accent text, a grey-green as quiet as the secondary words, read as plain words): "· 1 edit", Details… (the status line's and the notes line's), Show all / Show less, Edit, Discard, Cancel, Save, Re-anchor… (the captions' Markdown links), the ⋯ popover's change ids, the Finish sheet's Show and Undo. No underline at rest (on a card an underline means changed words, §3): an underline while the pointer is over it, the pointing hand, a little fainter while pressed. 4.9:1 on the panel and 5.3:1 on the card in Light Mode, 6.0:1 and 5.0:1 in Dark; ΔE00 from every word colour of the panel at least 30, from the change greens 50, from To check's amber about 50, and 18 / 20 from the slate teal of other edits (`LinkLookTests`). The panel toast's Undo keeps the system's link button on its capsule |
| PDF backdrop | `#E2E3E0` / `#151615` | behind the pages |
| panel | `#F6F6F4` / `#1C1D1C` | the comment and change lists |
| card | `#FFFFFF` / `#2A2C2A` (hairline 7.5%) | the expanded card |
| label, label 2 | `#1F2621` / `#E6EBE6`, `#3D473F` / `#C5CDC6` | titles, notes, rows, group headers (since POLISH §7); a row's id, a header's count |
| secondary | `#5F6A61` / `#9EA89F` | secondary words and every date (5.2:1 / 6.9:1 on the panel) |
| tertiary | `#98A199` / `#6C756D` | placeholders only (2.5:1) |
| danger | `#C42121` / `#F98B8B` | errors only (it was `#DC2626`, 4.46:1 on the panel) |
| sheet, group | `#F6F6F3` / `#1E1F1E`, `#FFFFFF` / `#2A2C2A` | the Finish sheet and its boxes (phase 3; they were the native palette's `#F7F7F8` / `#1E1E20` and `#2C2C2E`) |
| accent tint | the accent at 13% / 28% | the first-run tip's fill (phase 3); Jump's highlighted row (phase 4) |
| float, float line | `#FBFBFA` / `#2B2E2C`, 12% black / white | the Jump panel, opaque with a hairline (phase 4; POLISH §2.4–2.5), and the floating selection toolbar (after the visual review, 2026-10-18; it had popover material) |

The surfaces have LCH chroma ≤ 2 (`DesignTests`); the sidebar, toolbar, sheets and popovers are the system's. The marks' tints on a card are 30% / 16% in Dark Mode. Unchanged: To check `#B45309` / `#F59E0B`, the decided grey, the change greens, the slate teal, the olive and the six mark colours. **Known limitation**: To check's amber is ΔE00 9.5 and 6.9 from the yellow and orange marks' inks (9.9 and 9.4 from their fills in Dark Mode), so it never relies on hue: the chip is outlined, with ○ and "To check"; the page's id labels are filled; the card has no bar in the mark's colour beside the chip; the papers badge is grey.

**Type** (macOS pt): papers 13 Medium; the Contents 13, sections Medium, subsections Regular in label 2, the current one Semibold, numbers secondary; the tabs 13 pt, the shown one's name in the label colour, drawn as the system's segmented control at its large size (`PanelTabs`); group headers 14 Semibold in the label colour with a 12 pt sign and an 11 pt count capsule, their quieter words 12 secondary (POLISH §7; they were 12 Semibold in label 2, §1); rows as before (id 11 Medium, word 12, note 13); the card's title by `NoteTitle` (§2); thread labels 11 Medium with the round Semibold; the paper's words New York 13/20, the shown round's 14/21; the advisor's words and the buttons 13; hints and dates 11 secondary; nothing under 11.

**Shape**: chips radius 5 (20 pt), buttons and fields 7 (26 pt buttons), cards 10. A card holds one fill (the advisor's words), one outline (the field, the To check chip) and rules beside the paper's words.

**Screenshots compared** (background, Light and Dark, with `design/ui-v2.1/png/01-window-sage-light.png`, `01b-window-sage-dark.png`, `02-before-after.png`, `05-design-language-*.png`, `06-panel-states-*.png`): the card's header (outlined chip, To check in amber, p.N and ⋯ only), the title, the thread's labels and rules, the field and the buttons, the quiet rows, the group header and the large tabs match the mockup; the buttons have no key letters (POLISH §6, which the mockup predates). Phase 2 (2026-10-18), Light and Dark, with `01-window-sage-light.png`, `01b-window-sage-dark.png` and `11-states.png`: the one sidebar (Jump to…, Papers with the open paper selected, Contents with numbers, olive dots, comment counts and the current section's lighter fill), the folded Papers header ("○ 2 more to check") and the status line's count with Next and Mark Read match the mockup; Jump to… is new since the mockup (POLISH §6). Phase 3 (2026-10-18), Light and Dark, with `06-panel-states-*.png` (e), `07-changes-tab.png`, `08-finish-sheet-*.png` and `11-states.png`: the done card (check circle, the two lines, a plain Finish Round 3…, no To check group), the Changes tab's header ("Other edits to check." in the label colour) and card (no bar, R3's text 14 pt beside the slate-teal rule), the Finish sheet's three sections and email column, the editor's "New comment · p.7" with the selected words beside the colour's rule, the tip under the answer buttons, and the empty states match the mockup, with these differences by decision: no key letters on buttons (POLISH §6), so Mark checked has no "V", the tip speaks of the buttons, not "V, R or W" and "?", and the empty states name Comment and Option instead of Return and the number keys; the editor keeps its kinds and Cancel, and "The note may be empty" for the mockup's key caption; the change card keeps ⓘ (POLISH §3 row 10 does not move it into ⋯); the Changes tab's folded group still has no count (§16: the sentence says it); the default buttons show the accent only in a key window, so the background screenshots show them plain. Phase 4 (2026-10-18), Light and Dark, with `09-focus-*.png` and `10-jump.png`: Focus's window (no sidebar or panel, the page larger on the backdrop, the toolbar's Focus pressed) and the bar's three states (a card to check; with "unsent draft" and the draft in quotation marks; the choice row "Close R1-16 with your draft as a closing remark?" with the draft under it) and the Jump panel ("r2-0" and "entropy", in Review and in Focus) match the mockup, with these differences by decision: the bar has Resolve, Follow up and Keep as is beside Show Card (POLISH §6, mouse first: every action a visible control; the mockup has Show Card only), no key letters; the reading count's line has its Next and Mark Read as in the status line; Jump's find row has no "⌘G for the next" (no key caps; Edit › Find Next in Paper names ⌘G), and with no query it lists the rounds and the commands. The integration (2026-10-18), Light and Dark, every main screen again: the Review window with the tip and the dimmed rows, the answer toast, an unsent draft and the choice row, the Changes tab, the comment editor, Jump, Focus with and without a draft, the Finish sheet with what is left and with nothing left, a closed round and an earlier one, the done card, the empty list, no paper open, no paper listed, and no rounds yet; each as described here. Seen there: after the PDF has been further down and come back to the top, the sidebar's follower (the least scroll that keeps the current section in view, §18) leaves the Papers header, folded or not ("○ 1 more to check"), above the sidebar's view until the sidebar is scrolled up (fixed in UI v2.2: the sidebar goes to its top when the current row fits there, §17).


## 20. Focus and Jump (UI v2.1 phase 4)
2026-10-18, `design/ui-v2.1/POLISH.md` §3 rows 5 and 12, §4.3, §5 and the advisor's final decisions in §6 (the "?" key sheet of row 13 is not built: §6 dropped it). Mockup §10 and §11, `png/09-focus-*.png`, `png/10-jump.png`.

**Focus is the reading mode**: "read down the paper and mark what you see" (POLISH §6). The toolbar's Focus button (a page of text, `doc.plaintext`, with its name, §1), F, or View › Focus (F) switches it at once (`AppModel.setFocus`); only a round with a PDF has it, and it ends when the shown round has none or no paper is open. **After the advisor's use** (POLISH §7, 2026-10-18) the selected comment is on the right, as in Review, and no longer in a bar docked under the page: the bar read oddly there.
- **The window**: the sidebar collapses (the split view's column visibility, `.detailOnly`; showing the sidebar again with its toggle or ⌃⌘S ends Focus; outside Focus the sidebar's own shown state is kept, `sidebarShown`). The panel stays at the width it had (it keeps its width as the sidebar goes, and in Focus goes no wider than that, also in a narrow window, §1), so the page takes the sidebar's room; the status line stays under the page. No pane is animated (POLISH §2.5): the PDF has its new width on the next turn.
- **The panel** shows only the selected card: the same expanded card as in Review (`ExpandedCard`, or the change's `ChangeCard`), at the panel's top, with no tabs, no Changes sentence and no other rows or groups (the list's own table showing that one row: `CommentListController` / `ChangeListController` in Focus, `PanelModel.focus`, `ChangePanel.focus`); a closed round's banner stays over it. The card is the card in every way, but that its conversation never folds and no earlier bubble is cut (UI v2.2, CARD §3.9: the card has the panel, with no list under it to leave room for): its answer row pinned at the panel's foot while it is taller than the panel (§2), its ⋯ menu, its field, its editors, the toast with Undo (§8), which in Focus sits 12 pt under the card, by its buttons, rather than at the panel's foot some 550 pt below them (review 2026-10-18; `ToastPlace`, set by the list's table as it lays the card out and scrolls; at the foot when the card leaves no room under it), and says "Still loading…" there too (§11). With nothing selected the panel says, centred, "No comment selected" / "Click a comment's mark in the page to show it here." (on the Changes tab "No edit selected" / "Click a changed passage in the page to show it here."; `FocusHint`). The first-run tip, which says an answer moves on, waits for Review (`EditState.focus`).
- **The page**: 1.3 times the scale it had in Review (the mockup's), at most as wide as the view (`scaleFactorForSizeToFit`), set in the layout that gives the PDF view its new width (`ReviewPDFView.layout`, `PDFController.viewLaidOut`), and again when the window is resized; the point a third of the way down the view stays there across the switch both ways (within a point), except at the very top or foot of the document. When Focus ends, the page has its old scale (or fits again). With the sidebar already hidden the PDF's width does not change, and neither does the page.
- **Clicks on the page**: a mark or its label shows its comment's card on the right (§6, the mark flashes; the PDF does not move); a change's band shows the change's card; a bar in the margin marks its paragraph read (§18, with reading marks on), as in Review. J and K go through the list's cards (folded groups skipped, §1), one at a time on the right, and the PDF goes to each mark as in Review.
- **Answers stay**: an answer in Focus, from the card's buttons or a key (V, W, ⌘↩, the choice row), moves neither the PDF nor the selection (`answersAdvance` false; §8), so reading goes on; Mark checked (V on a change) does not move on either; ⌘Z leaves the PDF where it is.
- **The field is in Focus** (POLISH §7; until then what needed the field left Focus, §4.3): R, Return, Follow up with no draft, Edit and a new comment's note put the cursor in the card's field or editor on the right, and Focus stays; a follow-up sent from there stays on the card. While the new-comment editor holds a note that is not saved, Focus does not switch either way, and the PDF says why (§7; interaction review 2026-10-18: the page's new scale left the popover pointing at the backdrop). Delete's question (⌫ on a new comment) is the window's, so it asks in Focus too.
- **Leaving** gives the whole list back with the card where it was on screen, then centred (§6); the tabs and the sentence come back.
- **Cost**: a J step in Focus lays out the next card, as in Review (§10.23; with the bar it drew no card). Review pays nothing for Focus but three flags set at the switch.

**Jump** (`JumpModel`, `JumpPanel`): the sidebar's "Jump to…" (§17) and ⌘K (View › Jump to…) open one panel over the window, 620 pt wide, 44 pt below the toolbar, opaque (`Palette.float`) with a hairline, radius 14 (POLISH §2.4–2.5): a 17 pt field (its last query kept and selected, so typing replaces it), the groups, and under them what it searches, "Comment ids, your words, other edits, sections, rounds and commands". ↑ and ↓ move the highlighted row (the accent tint), Return goes there, a click on a row goes there, the pointer highlights; Esc, ⌘K again, or a click outside closes it, and the keys go back to the PDF. Every word of the query must be found (any case, any accents):
- **Comments**, in the list's order (To check, For the student, Closed this round), by id ("r2-0"), note, the words the comment is on (the shown round's sentence), or what you wrote this round: the sign and id, the note's first line, and where the words were found when the note does not have them all, "on “…when boat traffic is low…”" or "you wrote “…”"; the page on the right. At most 8.
- **Other edits** (those no comment asked for): the place and "Moved: “Boat traffic peaks…”", at most 5. **Sections**: the Contents' number and title, at most 6. **Rounds**: the round menu's titles, the shown one marked "shown". **Commands** that can act now: Focus (or Leave Focus), Finish Round N…, Next Changed Paragraph to Read (with reading marks on), Show the Comments / Changes (they end Focus), Show / Hide Change Marks, Underline Changed Words, the closed round's Summary… and final.pdf, New Round…, New Paper…, Add Existing Paper….
- **In the paper**, last: "Find “boat” in the paper". The paper's own text is not indexed (POLISH §3 row 12): this hands the query to the toolbar's find (§32; until 2026-10-26 the PDF's own find, with a toast): the query goes into its field, the first match at or below the top of the view becomes the PDF's selection (so the floating toolbar can comment on it) and is shown as a mark is (§6), and the field's counter says "1 of 7" (or "No matches"); the same query again goes on to the next. Edit › Find Next in Paper (⌘G) and Find Previous in Paper (⇧⌘G) go on with it.
- With no query, the rounds and the commands are listed, so the mouse can start there. The query's words are Semibold in the label colour in each row. Going to a comment or an edit selects it as a click on its mark would (in Focus the panel shows its card); a section scrolls the PDF as a Contents click; a round shows it as the round menu does.


## 21. The overview and navigation (OVERVIEW-v2, first step)
2026-10-19, `design/overview/v2/OVERVIEW-v2.md` §3.3, §5.1 and §8 (the advisor's "as recommended", all 14 decisions), FEATURES OV-6; mockup `png/03-paper-light.png` and `png/11-into-review-light.png`. The first step built the navigation and the overview's scaffold; the second (same day) the paper map: the page strip with its thumbnails and ruler, the lens, the map, the readout with its Sage line, folding, rows without sections and a failed build (below, **The paper map**); the third (same day) its interactions: the panel of what is followed, the help tags and the previewed path, the keys, the table view and VoiceOver (below, **Following, the panel, the keys and the table**). The rest of OVERVIEW-v2 §7 is next (**Not yet**).

**Two levels for the selected paper** (`Level`, `AppModel.level`, set only by `setLevel`): its **overview**, and **Review**, the PDF and the panel of §1–§20, unchanged.
- **Opening**: a click on a paper in the sidebar selects it, as any row of the list, and shows its overview (`AppModel.openPaper` through the list's selection, `sidebarSelected`); a double click on the row or Return (the list's primary action, `contextMenu(forSelectionType:primaryAction:)` → `sidebarOpened`; the double click's first click has already shown the overview, without waiting), or the row's context menu **Open in Review**, opens Review at the latest round. The rows have no tap gestures of their own: on macOS they kept the list from selecting a paper with a click (the advisor, 2026-10-19: a paper opened only with a double click, while a student could be selected). In Review the list selects nothing and the open paper's row has a fill of its own, the list's selection as it looks without the keys (`OpenPaperFill`, `Palette.openPaper`), so a click on it selects it and shows its overview again. The second click of a double click selects nothing (`isSecondClick`: when its first click unfolded the tree under the pointer it lands on another row), and only the paper the list selects opens in Review; a selection not taken (that click, a note being written, Unassigned) is put back to the model's. The rows keep their own context menus (the list's item menu is empty). In the overview, the toolbar's one Sage button **Continue Round N** (with an arrow; §5.1) opens Review at the latest round; it says **Show Log…** when that round did not build (Review shows the log) and **Open Round N** once it is closed, both plain. A double click in the overview opens Review **at a position** (`openReview(ReviewTarget)`, `reviewTarget(for:)`): a section's cell at its round with its heading in view (the round's first-level heading with the span's title, spaces and case aside, as a Contents click: the engine's section ids are its own keys, not the paragraphs' blocks; its first page when no heading has that title or the round's paragraphs are not known), a round (its rail or its readout) at that round, a page at that page, a comment at the strip's round when it is there (else the last round it has a page in: placed, or its passage deleted there) with its card selected on the Comments tab. The panel's **Open in Review** does the same. Another round is asked for first, and the position is shown when its state comes (`pendingReview`; the round's PDF loads first, `PDFController.reveal`); a paper whose state has not come yet waits likewise. A launch with `--paper` or `--fixture` opens in Review, as before, unless `--level overview`.
- **Back and Forward**: ⌘[ and ⌘] (the **Go** menu: Back, Forward, Overview, and Continue Round N / Show Log… / Open Round N) and the toolbar's ‹ › at its leading edge, in both levels (`HistoryButtons`: borderless, 24 pt each). One history across papers and levels (`NavHistory`, at most 64 places back), as the mockup's: a click on another paper, Review opened, All Rounds and Back / Forward are places; following something inside the overview is not. Since 2026-10-29 jumps in the PDF are places too while you stay in that paper's Review, and Review's place keeps the PDF's (§36). Each place keeps the paper, the level, the overview's place (what it follows, the strip's round, the older rounds unfolded, its scroll offset from the top; `OverviewPlace`) and Review's (the round shown, the selected comment or change and the tab; `ReviewPlace`). Back to the overview puts it back as it was left, scrolled where it was (`OverviewModel.restore`: the offset is put back once the overview is long enough, as its data may still be coming). Back or Forward to Review of the paper shown finds Review as it was left; another paper's Review opens at the place's round and selection. Going to Review leaves the overview as it is, so All Rounds finds it as it was last left. A paper removed from the list leaves the history too.
- **All Rounds**: the first item of Review's round menu (`RoundPicker`), over the rounds: the paper's overview as it was left. It is a place, so Back returns to Review.
- **Review is never rebuilt** (FEATURES OV-6): the overview is an overlay over PaperView, which stays in the window with its PDF view, its panel's lists and their scroll positions. SwiftUI's own ways to hide it would not do: `.hidden()` or a branch rebuilds its views, and `.opacity` or `.allowsHitTesting` moves them to another superview and leaves their AppKit tracking areas, cursor rects and help tags acting through the overview (checked 2026-10-19 with a probe view). So Review is hidden in AppKit (`PDFController.setReviewHidden`): the nearest split view round the PDF view, the PDF's and the panel's (never the window's sidebar split view), with `isHidden`; a PDF view made while hidden (another paper, a round that now builds) is hidden as it joins the window. Under the overview Review is hidden from VoiceOver. Leaving Review ends Focus, saves an open editor as a click elsewhere does, drops the choice row, leaves re-anchoring, the panel's toast, Jump, the floating toolbar and an empty new-comment popover, and takes the first responder from Review's views; while a new comment's note is being written the level does not change (as for a paper switch, §8). A PDF that loads behind the overview does not take the keys.
- **Keys and menus in the overview**: Review's keys do nothing there (`performKey` → `overviewKey`): Esc follows nothing; while the map has the keys (below, **The keys**) ← → ↑ ↓ move what it follows and Return opens Review there; any other key passes on (the sidebar's arrow keys go on choosing papers while the sidebar has the keys). The Comment menu, View › Focus, Find Next / Previous in Paper and the Reading menu are disabled. Jump (⌘K) works in both, and lists what Review shows (its round's comments, edits, sections): a comment, an edit, a section, a round, a find or a Review command opens Review first as it was left (`enterReview`, a place), New Paper…, Add Existing Paper…, New Round… and final.pdf do not.
- **The sidebar**: Contents only in Review (it follows the PDF); the overview has its map. Since the hierarchy (§22) the papers are under their students, and in Review the tree folds to the open paper. Its clicks (**Opening**) are checked in `HierarchyModelTests` (2026-10-19): the model's path (a click, the primary action, a click on the open paper in Review, Back and Forward, a second click landing on another row, students and pages as before) and the sidebar itself in a window never shown (the table's selection reaching the model, Return, each row's own menu, nothing selected in Review); the fill measured against the system's selection on background screenshots, light and dark. A background window's list takes no synthetic click, so a real mouse's click and double click are tried by hand.
- **Title and subtitle**: the paper's title in both levels. From macOS 15 the toolbar's own title (`ToolbarTitle`) is whole in a wide window and truncated in a narrow one, to leave the items after it their room (the paper's width, `PaperWidthReader`, less 460 pt in Review, 560 in a closed round, 300 in the overview and 210 more with its token): with ‹ › before it, a long title had pushed Review's Finish Round… into the toolbar's overflow at the window's minimum width, where it fitted before (checked on background screenshots of this build and of the one before, 960 × 640).
