At 1440 and 1280 every section but a quiet segment's has a name; a crowded paper's floored sections at 1280 show two or three letters ("Pr.", "Rel.", "Li.", "Et.") and its abstract one ("A"), a paper with its appendix shown at 1440 some single letters; at 960 most cells hold their count alone. The copies, bundles, caches and screenshots were deleted after.

**Map names after the audits** (2026-10-21, the code and UI audits of the labels above): a quiet segment named; a wider cell a longer form of its stem; "X." before a lone letter and no "I" or "l" beside a count; cuts never a letter or three short of the word, from the first word that is not a small word, before a hyphen, "…" for Chinese; initials only of three words or more (two as a way out of a look-alike); lettered appendix sections led by their letter; no longer form that reads as another section's; the ruler and the hovered row's names with the map's stem; the hovered row's names over the chips; the readout's third column only from 1360 pt, so the map has about 106 pt more at 1440 and 1280; the lens at the same row's scale as the axis; the axis 11 pt; "N pp" secondary. **Checked**: `MapLayoutTests` (the ladder's new steps, `extends`, `pick` with `avoid`, the cuts, the crowded paper's names reading as one stem down the rounds and its quiet segments named, the geometry's columns), `MapFoldTests` (`testAQuietSegmentsName`, the sonar paper's quiet segment named in the name ink inside it), `OverviewReviewTests` (every section of the open row named while hovered, at three widths, none over a chip or label).

## 24. Writing tags, Reopen, the key page and the last place (2026-10-20)
`design/tags/TAGS.md` with the design critique's fixes (the advisor's requests 1, 2 and 5 of 2026-10-20); the engine's side is CONTRACT amendment "Tags, key page and Reopen", the app's "App, tags, key page, Reopen and the last place". Synthetic data only in the repo: `Fixtures/overview-tags.json` (three fictional papers of one fictional student the engine built, paths `/synthetic/…`).

**The catalogue** (`Tags.swift`: `TagCatalog`, `TagStore`, `TagModel`): ten built-in tags in three groups and Well done (`grammar`, `wording` · `clarity`, `logic`, `evidence`, `structure` · `figures`, `math`, `format` · `praise`), each with a stable id, a name the advisor may change, a group, an SF Symbol with no colour (Clarity's is `text.magnifyingglass`, not an eye: the critique), a one-line definition (the help tag everywhere: "Logic & claims · Thinking", then the definition) and a For the next draft line. The advisor's own tags get `u-` and 8 hex digits, group Other, an optional sign from 16 that no built-in tag or the Not tagged row uses (`TagCatalog.palette`). The catalogue's order is the picker's and every table's; it is never sorted by count. **Where it lives**: `~/Library/Application Support/Paperole/tags.json` in real use (Paperole Dev uses its own `Paperole Dev` folder), written at the first change (atomically); a `--background` run, a fixture run and `swift test` keep a catalogue of their own in a temporary folder, or `--tags <file>` (`TagStore.location`, the critique's fix: never the advisor's file). No file is the defaults; a file missing a built-in tag gets it back at its end. The ledger holds only each comment's ids (engine `tag`, `comment` with `tags`), with the advisor's own tags' names as a backup (`names`).

**The picker** (`TagViews.swift`: `TagPicker`, `TagChip`, `TagSelection`, `NewTagControl`): the same chips in three places, at the same width (416 pt; the ten built-in tags in three rows) and in the same places. A chip is a 22 pt pill on a quiet neutral fill (6 % ink, 7.5 % white in Dark Mode), 12 pt words with its sign; **on**: the accent's tint (15 % / 22 %), its words in the accent's text colour, Semibold, a check in place of the sign, so it never relies on colour; **suggested**: a dashed outline in the accent's text colour, its reason in the help tag ("Suggested from “因果” in your note"). Each chip's width is measured once with its Semibold label, so turning one on moves no chip (`TagChipMetrics`; `TagPickerTests`). One click turns a tag on or off; several or none; at most 12. Amber, red and the mark colours are never a tag's. **New Tag…** (a link at the bottom left) becomes a name field where it is, with a sign menu (optional); Return adds the tag at the end of the catalogue and turns it on (Return while an input method has marked text is the input method's); Esc closes only the field; a name a tag already has (case aside) turns that tag on, shown again if it was hidden, and says so under the chips. The chips' help tag: "Only you see tags: they never go into the email or final.pdf." No key glyphs; the ⌃1–⌃9 keys were deferred (the critique: Mission Control's shortcuts take them, and they sit next to the recolour keys).
- **Writing a comment**: the new-comment popover (§7) has the chips under the note, then New Tag… at the left and Cancel and Save at the right (444 pt wide, the note editor 96 pt high, so the popover with a three-line quote and three rows of chips stays under 420 pt: `testThePopoverHeight`). ⌘↩ saves the note, its style and its tags in one `comment` (a name typed in New Tag… is added first, as Return would). A tag picked counts as a change: a click outside keeps the popover, and Cancel asks "Discard this note?" first. While the New Tag… field is open, Esc is the field's, not Cancel's. Suggestions appear as the note is typed (0.3 s after the last key).
- **On a card** (`CardTagRow`, under the title, in any round, closed and earlier ones included: tags change nothing the student receives): the tags as words with their signs in the secondary colour (the critique: tags are secondary in Review, so no pills), then **+**; with none, "+ Tag", and first a suggestion as a dashed chip that one click takes (`by: suggestion`). The + or a tag's name opens the picker in a popover at the + (`CardTagPopover`, the footer "For R1-08 in every round · only you see tags"); its tags are written **when it closes** (a click outside, Esc, another card selected, another round, paper or level, quitting), in one `tag` request with the round shown (the critique's fix: a request a click went through the engine's queue and redrew the card under the open popover); the card shows them at once (`AppModel.setTags`, the state's comment updated before the engine's reply). The card's `⋯` has **Tags ›** (a checked item per tag, Choose Tags…).
- **Rows** (§2's compact row): the tags' signs only, at most three and "+2", in the secondary colour (not tertiary: POLISH keeps tertiary for placeholders), before the page; the row's height and words are unchanged; VoiceOver adds "Tags: …".
- **Tag Comments…** (`TagSheets.swift`: `TagCommentsSheet`, `TagCommentsModel`; from the paper page's Topics header "Tag 2 Comments…", the overview's More menu, the Not tagged panel, or a student's page): one sheet for every round at once (the critique's fix), the comments not tagged yet under "Round N" headers (by paper first on a student's page), "Show all comments" to change tagged ones; a click on a row shows its chips (the picker's, with its suggestions); the row before is written when another is clicked or Done is pressed; a row tagged in the sheet stays where it is. No bulk "Accept suggestions" (the critique: what counts is what the advisor clicked). The overview and the round shown are asked again when it closes.

**Suggestions** (`TagSuggester`, on this Mac only, no network, nothing written): at most two, never a tag that is on or hidden; first where the comment is (the creation anchor's `env`: figure, table, wrapfigure, subfigure, tabular… → Figures & tables; equation, align, gather… → Math & notation; thebibliography or a .bbl → Format & LaTeX; a box drawn on the page, known to have no PDF words, → Figures & tables; a `$` that is not `\$` in the source of a comment made on selected text (`new_quote`, method selection, never the PDF words, which print no `$` for math) → Math & notation; since 2026-10-24, §30), then its kind (a strikeout with a note of at most 8 CJK characters or 6 words → Wording), then its note's words (TAGS.md §5.2's table: English as whole words, case aside, a LaTeX command not a word, so `\cite` is Format only; Chinese terms of two characters or more), earliest in the note first. Well done only for a note of at most 12 CJK characters or 6 words without a negation (不, 没, 但, not, but, however, no, n't; its own words taken out first, so 不错 counts). **Settings › Tags › Suggest tags as you write** turns them off (on by default; `suggestTags`, never saved by a test run). Learning from the advisor's own tags (TAGS.md §5.3) is not built.

**Settings › Tags** (`TagsSettings`, `TagEditor`): "Your tags" in the picker's order, a row each, under column headers Tag · Group · Comments · In Picker (§25): sign, name (its definition in the help tag and in the editor below, no longer cut in the row; "hidden" after it when hidden), group, the comments with it in the listed papers (each once; from the hierarchy's `overview`, asked when the tab opens and again after every edit while it is open; "–" until then) and a **Show in Picker** checkbox (the critique: no eye). Drag to reorder (VoiceOver: Move Up / Move Down). Under the list: + (a new tag, selected), − (only one of the advisor's own that no listed comment uses, by counts asked after the last edit with every listed paper read: §25; a tag in use is hidden instead), Restore Default Names (the built-in tags' names, definitions and next-draft lines; the order and the advisor's own tags stay). The selected tag below: Name (Return or leaving the field keeps it; another tag's name is refused with "Another tag has that name."), its sign, Group, Definition, For the next draft (English: it is what Copy for the Email copies) and **Merge Into…**: "Merge “Vague” into “Clarity”?", with what happens: every listed comment with it gets the other instead (one with both keeps one), through the engine's `retag` per listed paper, which reads each comment's tags from its ledger as they are now (§25; until then whole lists sent through `tag` from the hierarchy's overviews, which could be stale); then, only when every paper took it, it leaves the catalogue (a built-in tag is hidden), else it stays and the papers that refused are named; the papers' records change, so it cannot be undone, and papers not in the list keep it ("To keep them apart, hide it instead"). This replaces TAGS.md §3.3's reversible merge, whose engine alias was deferred with the critique; a merge no longer waits for the hierarchy's data (`retag` reads the ledgers). A tag renamed shows everywhere at once (cards, rows, picker, tables).

**Topics on the paper page** (`TagAnalytics.swift`: `TopicsTable`, `TopicsView`, `TopicsGrid`), under the legend while nothing is followed (or a tag is), once a comment of the paper has a tag (none tagged: no Topics, the More menu's Tag Comments… is the way in; OVERVIEW-v2 decision 9): "Topics · new comments in each round, by your tags", at the right "15 of 17 tagged · Tag 2 Comments…" (a link). A row per tag that has comments (a hidden one too: history counts), grouped under LANGUAGE, THINKING, PRESENTATION, NOT A PROBLEM, OTHER (small capitals in the secondary colour; the groups in the order their first tag comes in the catalogue, the tags in the catalogue's order), then Not tagged (grey) and New comments (Semibold); a column per round (the comments written in it with the tag; a short warm-stone bar, one scale a table, at most 30 pt; "–" for none; the oldest rounds folded into one "R1–R3" column when they do not fit), Open now, Written in (three sections at most, all in the help tag). The note under it: counts of comments each in the round written; two tags, two rows, totals once; later rounds look only at what changed, so fewer new comments there is no trend; "Round 4 has no new comments yet." No shares, no ranking, no trend words between rounds of one paper. Rows are one line of fixed columns, so the row followed is one band on the accent's tint, the pointer's row on the hover fill.
- **Following a tag** (`OverviewItem.tag`, the fifth followable object; a click on its row, again on the row to follow nothing; Esc or the token's ×; "Following Logic & claims" in the toolbar): the map's numbers and fills never change; the cells holding none of its comments step back, with no outline and no paths (the critique's fix); its edits stand out; each round's readout has a Sage line ("3 here · 1 new · ✓ 1 so far", "none here"), under the caption "Logic & claims only". Topics stays with the row on the accent's tint, and under it the tag's panel: "2 comments over all rounds, each counted once · 1 open · written in Methods 1 · Results 1", its comments as lines by the section they were written in, each "Methods · R1–R4 · ○ to check", "· reopened in R4"; Not tagged's panel has Tag Comments…. The arrows have nowhere to go from a tag; a double click opens Review as it opens.
- A comment's panel also says its tags and "reopened in Round 4".

**The Student page's writing profile** (`StudentTagsProfile`, after Where the comments land, Where the time goes and How comments closed), once a comment of the student has a tag (before: one line, "Topics, first drafts and For the next draft start when you tag comments." and Tag Comments…):
- **Topics by round**: the opening sentence, the papers and rounds then at most two single facts in the advisor's order, never Well done, never two papers' counts side by side (the critique's fix; `ProfileText.fact`: "Structure: none since Reef choruses’s Round 2.", "Logic & claims: 6 of its 14 comments took two rounds or more.", "Wording: 0 comments in Round 3 of Reef choruses, on 380 new words."); the table: a row per tag, a column per round of each paper (the paper's name over its rounds, Round 1 with its month), Both papers / All papers (each once), Open now (the papers' open comments, each once), Not tagged, New comments; its note says why it has no trend words.
- **First drafts**: each paper's Round 1 with its pages ("Shrimp · 11 pp"; a draft not compared is lighter, its reason in the help tag), the counts, Trend: fewer, more, about the same, no clear trend (the engine's words with its rule: three comparable first drafts of similar length, a steady halving or doubling; 3 → 2 → 1 is no clear trend), else "after a third", "too few to compare", "too few tagged", "lengths differ"; never better or worse.
- **For the next draft**: the engine's picks (at most two, never Well done or a hidden tag), each a card: the tag and its sign, "5 comments · 1 needed a follow-up · 2 open", the advisor's line for it, the student's own words where the comment is (New York, a rule at the left; "Mei · Kelp holdfast survey p.1 · R1-03") and the advisor's note in the grey bubble; **Copy for the Email** copies "For the next draft:", each tag's English line and the student's sentences with the paper and page, never the advisor's own words (`ProfileText.email`; a test run's own pasteboard); nothing is sent.
- **Done well**: how many, and the latest of the advisor's words with paper, round and page.
- **How comments closed, by topic**: each tag's comments closed after 1, 2, 3+ rounds you answered in, open, followed up; "a comment you reopened counts as open until closed again" when there is one.

**Reopen** (TAGS.md §9 with the critique's fixes; the engine's `revive`, as the verdict `reopened` is Follow up): a comment closed (Resolved or Kept as is) in an earlier round that the student broke since comes back into the open round as To check. **Where**: on its card in the earlier round shown (read only), in place of the answer row: [Reopen in Round 4] "Brings it back as To check" (plain, no circular arrow: ↺ meant follow-up in final.pdfs before 2026-10-17), and in the card's `⋯`; in the overview, the comment's panel (Reopen in Round 4); and, the main way (the critique: core, not optional), the **Changes tab**: an edit in the sentence of a closed comment (the engine's `near_closed`) says "near R1-07" on its row and "✓ Near R1-07 · resolved in R2 [Reopen R1-07…]" on its card (`NearClosedLine`). **Then** Review shows the open round with the card selected in To check and the cursor in its follow-up field, the toast "R1-07 is back in Round 4, to check · say what broke in its field"; its row's word is "reopened"; its conversation says "R4 · you reopened it" before what the student did since it closed (compared with the closing round's text, by the engine). **Undo Reopen** in its `⋯` while the round is open (with a follow-up or a draft written for it, asked first: "Undo Reopen discards your follow-up?"); the comment goes back to closed in its round. The email and final.pdf are the engine's ("Reopened (k)", "R1-07 · reopened", no sign).

**Set as Key Page** (TAGS.md §7): a thumbnail's context menu on the paper page (Set as Key Page, or Use the Busiest Page on the chosen one; Open in Review at p.N; Follow This Page) and Review's PDF menu in any round ("Set p.5 as Key Page", last). Kept in papers.json as `key_page` {round, page} (app side, like `buckets`; forgotten with Remove from List; a test run, a `--paper` paper or `--no-list` keeps it for the run only: `persist` no longer writes in any `--background` run), sent to `overview` as `key_pages` (the paper's and the hierarchy's requests; a change asks again), and the engine's `key_page` (followed into later rounds by its figure or paragraph, else the same page number; the busiest without a choice) is what the Student page's cards, All Students' tiles and Your Move's cards show (`KeyPageStack`). In the strip, the key page's number has a small bookmark before it, its help tag "Key page: chosen by you · …" or "the busiest page".

**Where you left off** (`LastPlace.swift`; Settings › General "When Paperole opens": Where you left off | All Students, `openAtLaunch`): the window's place — a hierarchy page and its scroll, or a paper at its level with the overview's place (what it follows, the strip's round, older rounds unfolded, the parts shown, its scroll) and Review's (the round when an earlier one was shown, else the latest; the selected comment or change, the tab, the PDF's page when nothing is selected) — kept as `lastPlace` (JSON) in the app's defaults a second after the window last changed and when the app quits, and opened at launch: the paper when it is still listed and its folder has a ledger (else All Students, as before), a round no longer there the latest (`AppNavigation.reveal`), what was followed or selected when it is still there. Launch arguments (`--paper`, `--hub`, a fixture, a debug script) win, and a test run (`--background`, a fixture, `swift test`) never reads or writes it; nothing is kept for a paper given on the command line. The window's size is macOS's.

**Debug hooks** (§13): `--tags <file>`; items `tags` (the catalogue, the selected card's tags, suggestion, Reopen and turns, the picker open, Tag Comments…, the overview's numbers and Topics rows, the key page, a student's lede, trends, picks and email copy), `tagpicker <id>|close`, `tagclick <tag>|new <name>` (the open picker: the popover's, the card's or the sheet's row), `settags <id> <tag,tag>|-`, `revive <id>` / `revive undo <id>`, `keypage <round> <page>|off` (page from 1), `tagsheet [student <name>|select <id>|all|close]`, `tagsetting rename|hide|show|move|group|merge|delete|restore|suggest|add …`, `settings tags <id>` (that tag selected), `ovclick tag:<id>`.

**Checked** (2026-10-20): `TagTests.swift` (synthetic): the default tags and their signs (one tag each, SF Symbols on macOS 14), adding, renaming, hiding, ordering, deleting and restoring; the store (no file before a change, a file missing a tag, a test run's place); the suggestions (words, places, kinds, Well done's guard, hidden and on); the picker's working set, New Tag… (a hidden name shown again), chips the same width off, on and suggested, three rows, the popover's height; the engine's new keys decoded (and an older state without them); a card's tags, suggestion, "reopened" and "you reopened it"; the overview fixture's tags, key page and student profile; Topics (order and groups, folded columns, totals, no shares); following a tag (numbers unchanged, cells dimmed, no paths, the Sage line, the tag's panel, Reopen on a comment's panel); the profile's words and the email copy (English only); the hierarchy's request (`key_pages`, `tag_order`) and papers.json; the strip's key page; the model: tags at once, the card's picker written once on closing and when another opens, a suggestion taken, a rename on the cards, no last place in a test run, the last place's tokens and opening it again. Fixed from them: chips' words cut (a spacer's spacing), Topics' followed row drawn as a box per cell, a total of 0 shown as "–", "R4 · you reopened it · closed in R2" (said twice). How often the advisor would take them can be measured only once the advisor tags (TAGS.md §5.2). The user's defaults and papers.json were unchanged (checksums), no tags.json was written to Application Support, and the copies, screenshots and caches were deleted after.

## 25. The audit fixes (2026-10-20)

The code and UI audits of the day's work (tags, Reopen, the key page, the v3 map), fixed with tests (`AuditFixTests`, `TagTests`, engine `test_tags.py` / `test_revive.py`).

**Correctness.**
- **The tag suggester** searches the note itself, case aside (`TagSuggester.wholeWord` with `.caseInsensitive`): a range found in `note.lowercased()` sliced the note past its end once a letter grew when lower-cased (İ → i + U+0307), and the app crashed on opening any paper with such an untagged note, at every launch with Where you left off.
- **Settings › Tags and Tag Comments… decide from current data**: `HubModel.pending` says a reload is on its way (`status` stays `.loaded` meanwhile); `AppModel.hubCurrent` (loaded, nothing pending, asked with the current `hubKey`, which counts `ledgerEdits`) gates Delete (also refused while a listed paper's overview failed); Settings asks again when `ledgerEdits` changes while it is open (`ledgerEdits` is published); Tag Comments… opens from overviews asked after the last edit (`withCurrentHub`, or the paper's overview asked again, `OverviewModel.afterLoad`). **Merge Into…** goes through the engine's new `retag` (CONTRACT amendment "Audit fixes"), per listed paper, so a tag put on since is kept; the tag leaves the catalogue only when every paper took it.
- **Names the catalogue lacks**: `state` and `overview` return the ledger's `tag_names`; the app keeps them (`TagCatalog.ledgerNames`, learned from every reply before it is shown), so a tag deleted or merged while a paper was out of the list, or from another Mac, shows its name, never "u-1a2b3c4d". **A tags.json that does not read** is set aside as tags.json.bad (tags-2.json.bad …) and never written over (`TagStore.setAsideIfUnreadable`).
- **A tag's name** is at most 32 characters and 64 code points (`TagCatalog.clean`), the engine's limit, so a name of emoji sequences no longer makes every request carrying it fail.
- **Restore Default Names** while a tag is open in Settings: the editor's fields follow the tag field by field (`TagEditorFields.follow`), so leaving it no longer writes the old words back.
- **Undo Reopen** forgets the comment's answers of the round on the ⌘Z stack (they went with its record; ⌘Z stuck on them). **Reopen** and Undo Reopen act on their reply only while their paper is still shown (an id like R1-03 is in every paper).
- **The key page** chosen beyond a round's pages (built again shorter) is its last page in the strip and the PDF menu, as the engine takes it (`PageStripView.clamp`).

**Look and words.**
- **A closed comment's PDF label** is an outline on the page, the id in the label's colour (`MarkAnnotation.labelStyle`; 4.9–6.0:1, before 1.8:1).
- **The paper map**: a dimmed cell's name and number step back together in `MapRole.dimText` (light #566057, dark #9EA89F; ≥ 4.5:1 on the darkest step dimmed), as do the texts of a dimmed row (`MapCanvas.text` multiplies opacities: it replaced them, so names stayed full and numbers fell to 1.9:1). The light ramp is calm (§21 Colours; the small maps #DDDCD6 #C8C6BF #B0AEA5). The map's links (the axis's Show and Fold, Show all n rounds) are drawn in the system's link colour (`MapRole.link`), as Full Key and Profile now are (`LinkStyle`).
- **One sign per meaning** (`BuildSign`): did not build is the octagon everywhere (sidebar, hub chips, Your Move, the map's failed row, the status line, the failed-build page); the triangle only for Not found and a build with errors; position uncertain has a question mark (amber, with its words). Kept as is is "–" in the overview as in Review and final.pdf ("✓ 4 · ↩ 2 · – 1"; a comment's line says "kept as is" in words since 2026-10-21, as "– R2" after "R1–R2" read as a range); nothing there is "none" or an empty cell, never "–".
- **The failed-build page** in the palette's red (`Palette.danger`) and secondary, its heading 17 Semibold, its sentence at most 620 pt wide.
- **Words**: "1 comment waits for a PDF", "No comment waits for a PDF"; "came in", never "imported"; "Structure: none since Round 2 of Reef choruses."; the typographic apostrophe in every UI string (a source test); title case for buttons (Finish and Copy Email, Show Final PDF, Mark Checked, Full Key) and sheet titles (New Paper, Build Round N Again), every sheet title 17 Bold; page titles 24 (`PageType`); no text under 11 pt; the Topics groups' names as the sidebar's headers (12 Semibold, no capitals); footnotes at most 620 pt wide.
- **Fitting**: a group header's quieter words shorten before they are cut ("goes in the Round 2 email", "in the Round 2 email", "Round 2 email"; `SectionHeader.forms`); the overview's first sentence takes two lines.
- **The toast** keeps off the expanded card's answer row and field (`PinState.height`, `ToastPlace.bottom(answers:)`): 8 pt above it when it is in the toast's place.
- **Popovers** (the new-comment editor, the card's tag picker) on the opaque `Palette.float`, for which the ink is tuned.
- **The sidebar**: the students' chevrons in secondary (3:1 for a control); Your Move's ring amber only when something waits. **The email preview's** opening lines in `Ink.secondary`, at 12 pt. **The overview's comment panel** shows the advisor's note as the card's title (SF), New York being for the paper's words. **The reopen turn** comes after the round's student turn ("R4 · Mei revised · 1 edit", then "R4 · you reopened it"). **Tag signs** sit in one box (`TagSign.box`, 1.6 × size; a wide one, Grammar's, set smaller), so names line up. **Dark Mode thumbnails** at 88 % white (`PageThumb.darkDim` 0.12).

**Not changed** (the audit's low items left as they are): zeros are "–" in the count tables (Topics, How comments closed) and 0 in the First drafts table, where 0 is a value of the trend.

## 26. The app icon and Settings › Appearance (2026-10-20)

*Since 2026-10-27 (§34) Paperole is the only icon, light and dark; its style is in Settings › General, the Appearance tab, The Round's two icons, `--icon`, the `appIcon` setting and Finder's custom icon are gone. Below is the design as it was.*

The advisor's decision of 2026-10-20 (design/logo/LOGO.md §7): **The Round** is the app's icon; **The Round ¶** ships too; each has a light and a dark form, and Settings chooses the icon and its style. Since the advisor's decision of 2026-10-26 (LOGO.md §9) **Paperole** is the app's icon and the default; The Round and The Round ¶ stay as choices.

**In the bundle** (`build_app.sh`): `Resources/AppIcons/paperole.icns` and `paperole-dark.icns` (since 2026-10-26, LOGO.md §9: made by design/brand/paperole/round6/src/build_icns.py from round 6's `final`; 16, 32 and 64 px from the hand-tuned pixel masters, 128 and up from the vector master), and `round.icns`, `round-dark.icns`, `round-pilcrow.icns`, `round-pilcrow-dark.icns` (vermilion since 2026-10-25, LOGO.md §8: made by design/logo/red/src/build_icns.py from the vermilion SVGs, the green ones kept in design/logo/final; 16 and 32 px from the hand-tuned pixel masters, the dark ones the same shapes on the dark page) are copied into Contents/Resources; `CFBundleIconFile` is `paperole` (`round` before 2026-10-26), so Finder, Spotlight and the Dock before launch show Paperole, light (PaperCollab's bundle; PaperCollab Dev's own icon is its DEV-marked form, `paperole-dev.icns`, §29). Nothing is generated at build time: the build copies six files (seven in Dev).

**A built bundle shows its icon at once** (the advisor's report of 2026-10-21: the app in mac/build often had no icon). Cause, reproduced with the system's icon lookup (`NSWorkspace.icon(forFile:)`): the old script deleted the bundle and made it again in place, and for about a second (while `actool` ran) it was a bundle with an executable and no Info.plist; anything that looked then (Finder showing mac/build, Spotlight, the Dock) got the generic app icon, and Finder and Launch Services kept it, since writing inside Contents never changes the bundle's date (a lookup during a rebuild left it generic after the build, every time). The Round ¶'s custom Finder icon, which a rebuild drops, is not the cause: without it Finder shows The Round. Now `build_app.sh` makes the bundle in a hidden folder beside its place (`.PaperCollab-build.XXXXXX`, the same volume), signs it there, then moves the old bundle out and the new one in (two renames: the path is never a half-made bundle; the old one is deleted with the folder, and a failed build leaves the old bundle as it was), touches it (a rename keeps the date it was made with, and an icon cached for it or the path's last bundle is looked up again), and, for the bundle in mac/build only (no `APP_OUT`; since §29 that is PaperCollab Dev, and `release.sh` registers the PaperCollab it installs), re-registers that one bundle with Launch Services (`lsregister -f`, never a reset of the database), so its record and icon are this bundle's at once. A test bundle (`APP_OUT`) is not registered. It adds a few milliseconds. Checked: a lookup every 0.1 s during two rebuilds each, with the old script and the new (`APP_OUT` bundles in the scratchpad): the old left the generic icon after the build, the new The Round; `lsregister -f` alone refreshes a bundle whose generic icon was cached; `codesign --verify --strict` passes.

**Settings › Appearance** (`AppearanceSettings`, the second tab): under "App icon", three tiles of 112 pt, Paperole, The Round and The Round ¶ (two before 2026-10-26), each the icon with its name below; a click chooses it; the chosen one has a 3 pt accent ring 4 pt outside the icon's body and its name in Semibold. Below, "Style" Automatic | Light | Dark (segmented), with "Automatic follows the system's light or dark appearance." The tiles draw the style in effect, so changing the style, or the system's appearance in Automatic, previews it at once. The footer: "The Dock, the About window and alerts show this icon; Finder shows its light form." Defaults: Paperole (The Round before 2026-10-26; a saved choice stays), Automatic.

**At run time** (`AppIcons.swift`, `AppIconModel.shared`, started in `applicationWillFinishLaunching`): the chosen form (`paperole`, `paperole-dark`, `round`, `round-dark`, `round-pilcrow`, `round-pilcrow-dark`) is set at launch and whenever the icon, the style or, in Automatic, the system's appearance changes (`NSApp.effectiveAppearance`, observed):
- **The Dock and the App Switcher** while the app runs: `NSApp.applicationIconImage` (in PaperCollab Dev with the DEV capsule drawn over it, §29).
- **The About window**: PaperCollab › About PaperCollab passes the icon (`aboutOptions`), and since 2026-10-26 the credits: "Handwriting: Marcel Proust (public domain); traced from a photo by Warburg, Wikimedia Commons, CC BY-SA 3.0" (word for word as SOURCE.md gives it), small, secondary, centred, "Wikimedia Commons" linked to the photo's page (`AppIcon.credit`; the credit design/brand/paperole/hand/SOURCE.md requires wherever Paperole ships, so it is there whichever icon is chosen).
- **Alerts**, SwiftUI's too: AppKit draws them with the image named NSApplicationIcon, the bundle's icon, which `applicationIconImage` does not change (checked: an NSAlert laid out after setting it still had the bundle's). `NamedIcon` makes that one name give the chosen icon (`NSImage(named:)` replaced for it only, a copy of the image, behind a lock).
- **Finder**: macOS 14–15 have no dark app icons, and Finder shows one icon whatever the appearance: the light form of the chosen icon. Paperole (The Round before 2026-10-26) is the bundle's own; for The Round and The Round ¶ PaperCollab sets a custom icon on its bundle (`NSWorkspace.setIcon`, which writes `Icon\r` into it and flags it, as Get Info does; the ad-hoc signature still passes `codesign --verify`, though `--verify --strict` rejects the Finder info on the bundle until the icon is removed or the app rebuilt; PaperCollab Dev never sets the plain one, §29), and removes it when the advisor goes back. Only when it differs (`syncFinder`; a change from one custom icon to the other replaces it), and only in a real run. A rebuild or a reinstall (`build_app.sh` and `release.sh` make the bundle anew) drops it; the app sets it again at its next launch. At launch it is only ever set: a custom icon found there with the bundle's own icon chosen is left alone (Finder's Get Info may have set it). A style never changes Finder's icon.

**Kept** in the app's defaults (`appIcon`: paperole | round | round-pilcrow, `appIconStyle`: automatic | light | dark). **A test run** (--background, a fixture, `swift test`, `--icon` or `--icon-style`, or anything but the app's own bundle: `AppIconModel.isRealRun`) reads and saves neither and never sets Finder's icon; `--icon paperole|round|round-pilcrow` and `--icon-style automatic|light|dark` set them for a run.

**Later: macOS 26.** Its app icons have default (light), dark, clear and tinted appearances, drawn by the system from one Icon Composer `.icon` file (compiled by actool into Assets.car and named by `CFBundleIconName`; `CFBundleIconFile` stays for macOS 14–15). The layers are ready (design/logo/final/layers/round-bg.svg, round-fg.svg, and round-pilcrow's; their foregrounds are still green, so make vermilion ones first): make `The Round.icon` in Icon Composer, add it to the build with `--app-icon` and merge actool's partial plist into Info.plist; then the system follows the appearance in Finder too, and the style setting can step back to the icon only (Automatic being the system's). Paperole has no layers yet.

**Checked** (2026-10-20): `AppIconTests` (the choice: four forms from icon × style × the system's appearance, Automatic alone following it; the four .icns each with the 10 sizes 16–1024, light ones light and dark ones dark, all different; the About window's icon the one shown; `build_app.sh` copies the four and names `round`; the defaults saved and read back through an in-memory store, an unknown value the default; the flags; a test run, the flags, `swift test` and another bundle never remember nor set Finder's icon, with a made bundle of the app's id that is a real run only without them; Finder's icon with a fake: set for The Round ¶ with its light form, again after a "rebuild", removed for The Round, never for a style; the tab's Style control setting the style), `MapFoldTests.testTheSettingsWindow` (five tabs), and the whole suite. Background runs of a bundle built in the scratchpad, `--appearance light` and `dark`, through the `icon` item over every form: the Dock's image, the named icon, an alert's icon and the About window's option the icon shown, Finder's would-be icon right, nothing remembered, nothing set; screenshots of Settings › Appearance in both appearances (`tools/shot.sh --pid … --all`). The user's defaults and papers.json unchanged (checksums), no `Icon\r` on the built bundle.

**Paperole** (2026-10-26, LOGO.md §9): the third icon and the default. `AppIcon.paperole` first in the tiles; `AppIcon.bundled` is `.paperole`, and an unsaved choice is Paperole (a saved `round` or `round-pilcrow` still works); `build_app.sh` copies the six and names `paperole` (Dev: `paperole-dev`, made by `tools/dev_icon.sh`, which now takes the icon's name, `paperole` by default; `round-dev.icns` stays in the repo, no longer copied). With two icons that need a custom Finder icon, `syncFinder(changed:)` replaces it when the choice moves from one to the other. Checked: the three .icns through `iconutil -c iconset` (ten sizes each, 64 px and up identical to the round-6 PNGs, the opaque pixels of 16 and 32 too), `AppIconTests` and `VariantTests` (six forms, the credits and their link, the Finder icon replaced between The Round and The Round ¶, `paperole-dev` = DevMark over `paperole`), the whole suite, and the icon extracted from a freshly built PaperCollab Dev bundle.

## 27. Tags as a way to organise the comments (2026-10-21)
The advisor's question of 2026-10-20 ("can tags also be a way in to the comments?"), with TAGS.md's rules: counts only, the advisor's order, Not tagged as a bucket of its own, nothing the student sees. No engine change: Review uses the state's tags and paragraphs, the Student page each paper's `overview`.

**The line over the comment list** (`CommentFilter.swift`: `CommentListBar`, `ListMenuButton`, `ListMenu`, `ListBar`, `ListBarMetrics`): on the Comments page under the line of student notes, above the list's rule, while the shown round has comments; not in Focus, whose panel is one card. **One row, 22 pt high whatever it shows**, inset 16 pt as the notes line, so turning a filter on or off moves nothing under it. (Until the advisor's review of 2026-10-21 it was a row of tag chips with "By status ⌄" beside them: after a click on a chip, the caption under them ran over the second row of chips and the list's first header, in Dark Mode at the default width; and the menu at the right already had the tags. The row read its width from itself, so once too wide it kept its widest form; the line now takes its width from a `GeometryReader`, which takes the width offered whatever its content.)
- **The menu** at the right: one compact button, "By status ⌄" (12 Medium, secondary, as the headers' Show › and Hide ⌄; the hover fill under the pointer), an AppKit menu that opens under it by a click or by Space with keyboard navigation on (`ListMenu.popUp`, under an empty `MenuAnchor` view that takes no click). Two sections with their headers: **Arrange By**: Status, Tag, Section; **Show** (while some comment of the round has a tag, or a filter is on): All Comments, then each tag the round's comments have, in the catalogue's order (never by count), its sign as the item's image and how many of the round's comments have it as the menu's own badge (every group, folded ones too; a comment with two tags counts in each), then **Not tagged** with its count when some have none. A check marks each section's choice; each tag's help tag is its name, group and definition. With no tag in the round the menu arranges only. The arrangement is the viewer's choice, kept in the app's defaults (`commentGrouping`; a test run neither reads nor saves it).
- **Its title says what is shown**: "By status ⌄", "By tag ⌄", "By section ⌄"; while one tag is shown alone the button is a token, the tag's sign and name in the label colour and ⌄ on the accent's tint (`Palette.accentTint`), e.g. "ƒ(x) Math & notation ⌄", "Not tagged ⌄" with the tag sign. The tag shown alone stays in the menu with 0 when no comment has it any more.
- **While a tag is shown alone**, at the left of the same row: "1 of 16 comments" (12, secondary; of all the round's comments, as the tab counts only those to check) and **Show All** (a link in the link colour, the pointing hand), which shows every comment again. Short of room the count says "1 of 16", then the tag's name is cut with "…" (never under 48 pt while the count is there), then the count goes; Show All, the sign and ⌄ always stay whole, and at least 12 pt between Show All and the token (`ListBarMetrics.fit`, measured with the fonts drawn, each part framed at its measure). At the panel's 320–560 pt nothing overlaps, with long names ("Statistics & reporting quality") and CJK ones too.

**What the list shows** (`CardGroups.shown`; `toCheck`, `forStudent` and `closed` stay every card by status, which the tab's number, the done card, Jump and Finish use):
- **By status** with a filter: the three groups with only the cards it keeps; a group that keeps none is not listed (no plain "For the student" header nor the commenting hint under a filter); the folds as before, their counts the cards kept.
- **By tag**: a plain header a tag (its sign where the status groups have theirs, its name, the count in the capsule), in the catalogue's order, then Not tagged; a row under it does not repeat the header's sign, only its other tags' (`CardModel.listedUnder`). Each comment once, under its first tag in the catalogue's order (the filter's tag when it is filtered on), so a list with a selection and J/K never has a card twice; the filter finds a comment under any of its tags, and the menu counts it in each.
- **By section**: a header a top-level section of the shown round, its number where the signs are ("1 Introduction"; the title and abstract without one), in the paper's order; then "No section", "not placed in this round" (or "the sections are not found yet" until the round's paragraphs are in; `ListSections.make` from the state's `reading` and each comment's `paragraph`, as the sidebar's Contents count them).
- By tag or by section nothing folds and every comment is a 44 pt row, a closed one too (its bar faded): a 30 pt line would turn into a row when a card is reopened, and its words are the closing remark, not the note. The cards in the paper's order (page, column, height on the page). A filter that keeps nothing says "No comment of Round 3 has this tag now." ("Every comment of Round 3 has a tag now." for Not tagged).

**The held rules and the keys** (§1 "Nothing moves under the pointer"):
- J and K go through what is listed (`PanelModel.listed`); an answer moves on to the next card listed to check (`CardGroups.nextToCheck`: by status the To check group as shown, by tag or section the next card to check in the list's order, wrapping; none left, the selection stays).
- The selected card stays in the group it was shown in while it is selected (by status `PanelModel.held`, as before; by tag or section `heldKey`, set where it is first listed while selected), so a card retagged while selected (its picker closed with Esc or a click outside) moves only when the selection leaves it.
- A card the filter hides that is selected another way (its mark in the PDF, Jump, a chip on the Changes tab, ⌘Z) is listed while it is selected, in its own group, and leaves the list with the selection; a filter chosen while a card it hides is selected leaves no card selected, as a folded group's Hide does (what is not listed takes no keys).
- Another paper shows all its comments; another round keeps the filter while some of its comments have the tag. A switch of the arrangement shows the selected card in its own group of the new one, scrolled into view. Focus keeps both (its J and K go through what the list lists), though its one-card panel has no line to show them.
- **The table's pin** (`PanelTable.scrolled`, `withoutAnimation`): a scroll the table makes inside its own update (the list got shorter than its scroll, as a switch of the arrangement can make it) leaves the answer row's pin for the update's end. Asking for the card's row there re-entered the table's heights: AppKit logged "performed a reentrant operation in its NSTableView delegate" (found with a backtrace of a background run: `endUpdates` → the table shrank → the clip view scrolled → `scrolled` → `updatePin` → `rect(ofRow:)`).

**The overview** is unchanged: following a tag (§24) already keeps its comments' cells and steps the others back, with its Sage readout lines and its panel.

**One tag across a student's papers** (`StudentTagList`, `StudentTagPanel`, in TagAnalytics.swift; the Student page's Topics by round): a click on a tag's name in the table, or on Not tagged, lists its comments under the table, the row's name on the accent's tint (the pointer's on the hover fill); a click on it again, no list (`HubModel.studentTag`, kept for that student while the app runs). "Logic & claims · 3 comments in 2 papers, each counted once · 3 open", then each paper in the student's order: its title, "2 comments · 2 open", and a line a comment as the paper page's tag panel has them (its colour, id, the advisor's words, "Introduction · R1–R3 · ○ to check", by the section it was written in). A click on a line opens Review at that comment in the round it was last shown in (`AppModel.openInReview`: the paper, then `reveal`), with › on the line under the pointer; Back returns to the student's page. Not tagged is a list of its own; its Tag n Comments… is the table's, just above. The table's note ends "Click a tag for its comments." Since the UI audit of 2026-10-21: each tag's name has a › after it (⌄ while listed), so it reads as something to click without the pointer over it; the page scrolls the list's first 220 pt into view when a tag is picked (`ScrollIntoView`: the table is the page's last box, so the list was below the window's bottom and the click seemed to do nothing); the closed states in words, "✓ resolved", "kept as is" (it was "– R2", which read as a range after "R1–R2").


**Debug hooks** (§13): `tagfilter`, `listmenu`, `listmenuclick`, `groupby`, `list`, `panelwidth`, `studenttag`, `studenttagopen`; `--group-by status|tag|section` and `--tag-filter <tag>|untagged` set the arrangement and the filter for a run (never saved).

**Checked, the menu in place of the chips** (2026-10-21): `TagOrganizerTests` (synthetic): the Show section's tags (order, counts, Not tagged, none untagged, the filtered tag kept at 0), the line's words ("1 of 16 comments", "1 of 16"), `ListBarMetrics.fit` from 320 to 560 pt every 10 pt with six names (long, CJK) and three counts (the row never wider than the room, the name never cut under 48 pt beside a count; at 330 pt "1 of 16" with Math & notation whole, a long name cut; whole at 400); on the model the menu as built (its headers, the items, the checks, the badges, the signs, the help tags), its items doing what the chips did (a tag, Not tagged, All Comments, Tag with the filter kept), the title and the caption. `listmenuclick`: a click reaches the button (the hit view is the hosting view, not the anchor) and the menu would open under the button's 22 pt frame. The user's defaults and papers.json were unchanged (checksums); the copy, bundle and screenshots were deleted after.

**Checked** (2026-10-21): `TagOrganizerTests` (synthetic): the filter keeps the status groups and their folds, the selected card listed whatever the filter, the groups by tag (each card once, the catalogue's order, the filter's tag first, Not tagged last, a retagged card held) and by section (the paper's order, No section, before the paragraphs), the next card to check by tag and under a filter, the chips (order, counts, Not tagged, none untagged, the filtered tag kept at 0, named / short / signs), the sections from a round's reading (the top level, numbers, the title and abstract, a right column after the left), one tag across the fixture's three papers and Not tagged; on the model (state-reading.json with tags): the chips, the caption, J/K through what is listed, a hidden card clicked in the PDF listed while selected, a filter dropping the selection, the same chip again, a card retagged under a filter and while grouped by tag, another round keeping or dropping the filter, the arrangements' headers and rows, and that a test run saves no arrangement. In every run 1 card body a step, no list body, and rows only on the first step (the dimming). The user's defaults and papers.json were unchanged (checksums); the copies, bundles and screenshots were deleted after.

## 28. Data & Storage: export, import, backups and safe data (2026-10-21; rebuilt 2026-10-22 as data v1)

The advisor's reviews of 2026-10-20 ("数据的导入导出，存储位置") and 2026-10-22 ("开始实现吧": backups optional, no per-student settings, keep it clean). Design: `design/data/DATA.md`, whose §0 overrides the rest (pictures: `design/data/mockup.html`); engine: CONTRACT amendments "Data foundations" and "Export and import files". Code: `DataSettings.swift` (the tab), `DataStore.swift` (`DataModel`, the work), `Transfer.swift` and `TransferViews.swift` (Export, Import, Put Back an Earlier Ledger…, the window's bottom line, the context menus' items), `Backups.swift` (opt-in backups, the daily check), `PaperStore.swift` (the list). The engine stays the only writer of ledgers, analysis.json and PDFs, and writes and reads the export files; the app only renames a folder the engine unpacked into the place it was. Nothing is uploaded; nothing is written into a student's folder.

**The tab** (`externaldrive`, 560 × 560 pt, a grouped form, every action a button, the status line at the bottom as before): places and totals only, no row for a student or a paper (§0.2), so it is as long with forty papers as with one.
- **Backups**: one switch, "Back up automatically", **off by default**, its caption on or off ("Each day you work and after each Finish Round, when something has changed."). Turning it on asks for the folder first (an external drive's "Paperole Backups" when one is connected, else ~/Paperole Backups; Dev's "Paperole Dev Backups"; an existing folder under the old PaperCollab name is still preferred, and a chosen folder is kept; Cancel leaves it off; a synced folder asks first). Only while on: the folder (Show in Finder, Choose…; "On the same disk as your review folders: this guards against mistakes, not against losing the disk."), "Last backup: today at 16:40 · 3 papers · 2.3 MB · checked" (a partial one "2 of 3 papers", and on the caption "Left out: 1 on “Lab SSD”, not connected."), or why it fails in the error style ("Couldn’t back up since Oct 14: “Lab SSD” isn’t connected.", never amber), Back Up Now, "Keeps 7 daily, 4 weekly and 6 monthly backups, within 5 GB, and always the last copy of each paper." While off, nothing about backups shows anywhere.
- **Export and import**: Export… and Import…, a line each.
- **Review folders**: New review folders (~/Reviews by default, made when first used; Dev: ~/Paperole Dev Reviews, ~/PaperCollab Dev Reviews until the move of §34; a test run has none; Choose…, Show in Finder, ⊗ back to the default; a folder synced to iCloud Drive, Dropbox and the like asks first); "Replaced by Import: 1 copy · 1.6 MB" with Show in Finder, only while there are any. The header says to right-click a paper or a student to show it in Finder or export it.
- **Writing tags** (count, Import…, Export…, Show in Finder) and **Paperole’s own files** (Papers list, Unsent drafts, Page thumbnails with Clear), as before.
- Gone: Back Up…, Restore…, Review Folder as .zip…, the per-paper rows and their Export ⌄ menus, "Chosen each time", `ditto`.

**Context menus** (the sidebar's rows; All Students' paper tiles and student blocks; Your Move's rows; a student's page, its header and paper cards). A paper: Open in Review, Edit Paper…, Mark as (Not) Finished, Show in Finder, Export…, Export Comments ▸ CSV… / JSON…, Put Back an Earlier Ledger…, Restore from Last Backup… (only while backups are on, for a missing or damaged paper that one of this app's backups holds), Remove from List. A student: Open X’s Page, Rename Student…, Show / Hide Papers, Show in Finder, Export…, Export Comments ▸, Remove from List. Remove from List leaves the folders and says so on the window's bottom line with Undo ("Removed “Kelp drift” from the list. Its folder stays where it is.").

**A paper's problems** show where the paper is: its sidebar row says "missing" in red (its folder is gone; "not connected" in grey for an unplugged drive) or "damaged" in red (the daily check), the reason in the help tag; on the hierarchy's pages a missing paper's line says "Its folder is missing" with the red sign, its key page dimmed, and it waits on no one (not counted in "papers wait on you", no amber). A missing or unreadable paper is shown by the list's own copies of its title and student (papers.json keeps them, and they follow the ledger while it can be read: Edit Paper…, Rename Student…, a change on the command line), under its student, never as its folder's name under Unassigned; its own page says only "Its folder is missing." under its title (no pages strip), and the window its student, with no alert.

**Export Papers** (a sheet; from Settings, File › Export… or a context menu): a tree All students › each student › their papers with checkboxes (a dash when some are ticked; from a context menu only that student or paper ticked), each paper's line ("Round 2 open · 14 comments", "Round 1 finished"; a missing one greyed), one sentence of what goes in (each review folder whole, the tags, those papers' drafts; the settings too when every paper is ticked), "2 papers · up to 4.9 MB" (the folders on disk; the file is smaller). Export… opens the save panel ("Paperole – Maya Chen – 2026-10-22.zip", "… – All Papers – …", a title up to its colon); the steps show with Cancel; "Saved and checked: … (2.7 MB)." with Show in Finder: the engine read every file back against its checksum.

**Import…** (Settings, File › Import…, the empty list's Import…, the sidebar's Add Existing…; File no longer has a second item for it) takes an export or backup file (or a backup of the Back Up… before this), a review folder (listed where it is), a review folder zipped in Finder, or any folder (searched three levels down). The engine reads it without unpacking anything (`archive_check`); one review folder on disk is listed and shown at once. Otherwise the **preview**: "Made Oct 22 at 13:40 by Paperole · 3 papers · every file intact" on one line (another year's date with its year; a damaged file: the red sign; a Paperole Dev file: "Test data: none of it replaces yours unless you choose." under it, small), then Will be put back where it was and Will be added (checkboxes), Already here (a pop-up, both copies' round, comments and time), Can’t import (greyed, with the reason). Each row is "Title · Student" (a long title cut, never the student) and short facts ("3 rounds · 6 comments"); a place only where it is not Put them in ("Back where it was: ~/Reviews/kelp", "Still at ~/Reviews/kelp" for yours still on disk but not listed), on one line, the home folder as ~, cut in the middle, the full path in the help tag; Keep Both adds "This copy is added with “(copy)” after its title." "Use This Copy keeps yours in “Replaced by Import” beside it." shows only when a row offers it, the tags' line only when the file adds tags. Defaults (§5.3 as §0 amends it): a newer copy, or yours damaged, is used (yours kept in "Replaced by Import" beside it) unless the file is Paperole Dev's; older, the same, or changed on both sides: Keep Mine; yours on an unplugged drive: Keep Mine; a paper whose folder is still where it was but not listed: Keep Mine lists yours there (the default while the list is empty, as after losing it), Keep Both also adds this copy with "(copy)" and an id of its own, Leave Out (the default otherwise). Then Put them in (Choose…), the tags and drafts that come with it, and "Also use this file’s settings" (ticked only for an empty list). Import brings one paper at a time, each listed as soon as it is in (Cancel stops between papers), and the report says what was done as the preview does: a heading for each kind ("Put back where it was", "Listed where it is", "Added to ~/Reviews", "Added as a copy, “(copy)” after the title", "Used this copy; yours is kept in “Replaced by Import” beside it", "Kept yours (yours is newer)", "Left out: can’t be imported"), a row "Title · Student" for each paper, then the drafts, tags and settings added, and "Nothing else changed."

**Put Back an Earlier Ledger…**: the paper's saved versions, newest first ("Oct 22 at 09:12 · 3 rounds · 14 comments · a round started"), none chosen until one is clicked; Put Back makes it the newest version, keeping the one it replaces; the engine's refusals show in the sheet.

**Automatic backups**, while on: an engine process of their own, at the first quiet moment (a minute after launch, the review's engine idle, no Data & Storage work): daily and after each Finish Round, skipped while no ledger changed and the last backup's file is still in the folder (a new folder gets one at once); written only while the folder's drive, recorded by its volume, is connected (never a stray folder in /Volumes); the same checked file as Export (kind automatic, never over another file). Then the rotation deletes only this app's own automatic backups (this variant and install), only after the new one was checked, and never a paper's last copy. Restore from Last Backup… opens the import preview of the newest of this app's backups holding the paper, only that paper ticked.

**The daily check** runs whether backups are on or off: once a day after launch, the engine's read-only `check` on the listed folders; only damage marks a row; a marked paper is checked again after Put Back, Use This Copy, and whenever the list's numbers are read again.

**The list's safety**: an unreadable papers.json is moved aside (papers.json.bad), never replaced by an empty list; a save error is shown; the day's first save keeps the list as it was in "papers copies/papers 2026-10-22.json" beside it (14 kept). Each paper's three facts (finished, Map Headings, key page) moved into its ledger once (`adopt_facts`) and are written through `edit_paper` since. Ids stay unique in the list: of two listed folders with one id (a folder copied in Finder) the later gets its own (`fork`), said on the window's line. At launch the app removes what an interrupted import or backup left over a day ago (the engine's hidden `.Paperole-import-…` folders beside the review folders, including `.PaperCollab-import-…` leftovers from before the rename, its hidden partial .zip in the backup folder) and nothing else.

**Test runs** (`--background`, a fixture, `--no-list`, `swift test`; one rule since 2026-10-24, `LaunchOptions.isTestRun`; `--list <papers.json>` gives a `--background` run a list of its own, read and never saved) never save the list, a setting, the backups' state, an install id or the last place, never read or write the app's tags.json, drafts folder or thumbnails (a `--no-list` run without `--background` did until then), never schedule backups or the check, never sweep, and never send `adopt_facts` or `fork` to the papers of the list a background run read, nor fill in their paragraphs (`AppModel.fillIn`: the overview's Backfill skipped no listed paper until then, §30); a unit-test model reads no papers.json; their papers are temporary copies. A visual run that opens All Students, Your Move or a student's page asks for every listed paper's overview, so it uses `--no-list` or a synthetic `--list`. The `data` debug items (`DataModel.debug`) run every action without a panel.

**Comments** as CSV or JSON (Export Comments ▸) are as before: the engine's `export_comments`, the app writing the file (UTF-8 with a byte-order mark, a field that would start a formula quoted with a ' first).

**Checked** (2026-10-22): engine 358 tests and `swift test` 575, both passing (`tests/test_archive.py`, `tests/test_data_safety.py`; `DataTransferTests`, `BackupTests`, `DataStorageTests`). End to end on the three synthetic reviews (`samples/promo-demo/tools/build_reviews.py`) in a scratch folder, through a background Dev bundle's `data` items and the engine's CLI: comments answered and a round finished; everything exported, the folders and the list deleted, imported: every primary file byte for byte, each round's state and the overview the same; again for one student and for one paper put back where it was; an older copy over a newer one and the reverse (the defaults, and "Replaced by Import" holding the replaced copy byte for byte); an earlier ledger put back; backups turned on in a scratch folder, a backup, a deleted paper restored from it; a lost list brought back from a file by Keep Mine. Screenshots light; Dev's own papers.json and defaults untouched.

**Trimmed** (2026-10-22, the advisor: clean, only what is needed): the preview's and report's rows as above, no path repeated per row, no sentence repeating a heading; the window's line, the backup's failure words and the fork notice without paths (the path is shown once where it belongs); the history sheet and the Export sheet one short line each. `swift test` 577 passing (`testAMissingPaperKeepsTheListsNames`, `testShortDatesAndATestRunsList`); before and after screenshots of a background Dev bundle on synthetic copies, light and dark.

## 29. PaperCollab Dev and PaperCollab: two apps, two sets of data (2026-10-21)

*The apps are Paperole Dev and Paperole since 2026-10-27 (§33), and so are their bundle ids, data folders and engine package (§34: `com.paperole.app[.dev]`, …/Paperole[ Dev]/, engine/paperole); the names below are the ones of 2026-10-21.*

The advisor's decision of 2026-10-21, before reviewing real student papers. Until then one bundle, mac/build/PaperCollab.app (`com.papercollab.app`), served both the agents' tests and real use, with one data folder and one defaults domain, so a test could reach the advisor's papers, tags and settings. Now there are two apps from the same code. The papers list of that day (three test papers) moved to **PaperCollab Dev**; **PaperCollab**, the official app, starts with an empty list and is installed in /Applications.

| | PaperCollab Dev | PaperCollab |
|---|---|---|
| For | the agents' tests, the advisor's experiments | real reviews |
| Bundle | mac/build/PaperCollab Dev.app (`build_app.sh`) | /Applications/PaperCollab.app (`release.sh` only) |
| Name (`CFBundleName`, `CFBundleDisplayName`) | PaperCollab Dev | PaperCollab |
| Bundle id = defaults domain | `com.papercollab.app.dev` | `com.papercollab.app` |
| Executable | Contents/MacOS/PaperCollabDev | Contents/MacOS/PaperCollab |
| Data (papers.json, tags.json, drafts) | ~/Library/Application Support/PaperCollab Dev/ | ~/Library/Application Support/PaperCollab/ |
| Caches (thumbnails) | ~/Library/Caches/PaperCollab Dev/ | ~/Library/Caches/PaperCollab/ |
| Engine | the repo's (engine/papercollab, the venv's editable install) | a frozen snapshot in Contents/Resources/engine/papercollab |

**The runtime rule** (the safety core; `AppVariant`, a small type in its own file): the variant comes from `Bundle.main.bundleIdentifier`. Only exactly `com.papercollab.app` is PaperCollab; everything else is Dev: the Dev bundle, `swift test` (xctest), `swift run`, an `APP_OUT` test bundle, no id at all. So no test, debug run or Dev build can read or write the official app's data. `AppVariant` holds the name shown, the data folder, the caches folder and `isDev`, and every place that named Application Support/PaperCollab or Caches/PaperCollab takes them from it: papers.json (`PaperStore`), the drafts (`Drafts`), tags.json (`Tags`), the thumbnails (`Thumbnails`), and Data & Storage's places (`DataStore`, §28). The defaults follow the bundle id by themselves. The test-run guards stay on top, in either app: a `--background` run, a fixture run and `swift test` keep drafts, tags and thumbnails in temporary folders of their own, or in `--drafts`, `--tags`, `--cache`. **Paths elsewhere in this document** (Application Support/PaperCollab/…, Caches/PaperCollab/…) are PaperCollab's; PaperCollab Dev's are the same under "PaperCollab Dev". A backup's `backup.json` says `"app": "PaperCollab"` in both: it names the format, so a backup made in one app restores in the other (§28). A review folder is the same in both; the agents' rule that Dev never opens the advisor's real review folders is in the project's CLAUDE.md.

**The Dev marks**, quiet but plain at a glance; PaperCollab looks exactly as before:
- **The name**: every place the UI names the app (the main window's title while no paper or page is open, the menu bar, About, alerts' titles, "When PaperCollab Dev opens") says "PaperCollab Dev"; with a paper or a page of the hierarchy open the window's title is that page's own, in both apps, as before. DebugScript's window lookup and its alert use that name.
- **A "DEV" capsule on the icon**, bottom right, about a quarter of the icon's width: graphite, near black, "DEV" in white bold, a thin light outline, so it reads on the light and the dark icons. On the Dev bundle's own Finder icon (its `CFBundleIconFile`, a DEV-marked .icns made once by a script and committed), and on the Dock icon while it runs, drawn over whichever of the six icons Settings › Appearance shows (`AppIcons.swift`, `NSApp.applicationIconImage`, §26). Not the Dock's red badge, which reads as a count of things to do; not amber, which means your move in this app; not red, which means something is broken (§2 Status words).
- **Finder's custom icon** (§26, The Round or The Round ¶ chosen): PaperCollab as before; PaperCollab Dev never sets the plain icon on its bundle: any custom icon it sets is DEV-marked.
- **Settings › General**, Dev only: one quiet footnote line, "This is PaperCollab Dev, for testing. Its papers, tags and settings are separate from PaperCollab."

**The engine snapshot**: PaperCollab carries a copy of the engine package in Contents/Resources/engine/papercollab (the .py files only, never the repo's `__pycache__` or .pyc), taken by `release.sh`. Wherever the app starts the engine (`EngineClient.startIfNeeded`, also for the overview's `Backfill`, which has an engine of its own), a bundle with that folder puts it first on `PYTHONPATH` and sets `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`: a `PYTHONPATH` entry is searched before the venv's editable install, so the copy is the engine that runs, and nothing is ever written inside the installed bundle. Engine edits made during development therefore reach real reviews only at the next `release.sh`. Without that folder (PaperCollab Dev, tests) nothing changes. The Python is the venv's in both (`EngineClient.defaultPython`, or `enginePython` in the app's own defaults domain); "Engine Python not found" names the running app's own domain (`defaults write com.papercollab.app.dev enginePython …` in Dev). **Known limitation**: PaperCollab still needs the repo's venv, its Python and PyMuPDF; a fully self-contained app would need a bundled Python (BACKLOG).

**The scripts**:
- **One place makes a bundle**: `build_app.sh`, `VARIANT=dev` (the default) or `release`. Dev: "build/PaperCollab Dev.app" with the Dev Info.plist and icons. Release: the official Info.plist, the six icons (`CFBundleIconFile` `paperole` since 2026-10-26, `round` before) and the engine snapshot. Both keep §26's way of making it: built in a hidden folder beside its place, signed there, two renames, touched, and `lsregister -f` only without `APP_OUT`. `APP_OUT` still builds anywhere (test bundles; Dev unless `VARIANT=release`); `VARIANT=release` builds only into `APP_OUT` (without it the script stops), so the official bundle never lands in mac/build. Every path is quoted: the Dev bundle's name has a space.
- **`release.sh`** (new): builds `VARIANT=release` into a new temporary folder (`APP_OUT`), then installs it as /Applications/PaperCollab.app the same way: staged beside it under a hidden name, the old bundle moved aside, the new one renamed in, touched, `lsregister -f`; the old one is removed only after the swap succeeded. It refuses (non-zero, "Quit PaperCollab first") while PaperCollab runs, found by its exact executable name (`pgrep -x PaperCollab`) and by its bundle id, never by a pattern that could match the script's own command line; PaperCollab Dev's executable, PaperCollabDev, never matches, so Dev may run meanwhile (any other process named exactly PaperCollab, a `swift run` or `.build/*/PaperCollab` binary, also makes it refuse). No sudo: when /Applications is not writable it stops and says so. It never touches a data folder or the defaults. It prints the installed path and where the engine snapshot came from.

**What stays identical**: everything but the marks above. The windows, the review, the overview and Settings are the same in both; so are the data formats (papers.json, tags.json, drafts, backups), the review folders and the engine's contract. The agents' rules (project CLAUDE.md): build and test only PaperCollab Dev; never launch, quit, rebuild, replace, read or write PaperCollab or its data unless the advisor asks in that session; `release.sh` only when the advisor asks.

## 30. The code review fixes (2026-10-24)

The full review of commit `2d2925a` (`design/review/2026-10-24/REVIEW.md`: 150 confirmed findings, 17 of them major), fixed in 17 batches with tests; the engine's side is CONTRACT's amendment "Code review fixes". What the app now does differently, by area, with the review's ids. Where an earlier section differs, this wins.

**Review and the PDF.**
- **A paper opens at the top of p.1** (M12, UI-01): once the panes have settled after a paper is opened in Review (the sidebar, Continue Round N, a paper switch), unless something placed the view first (a selected comment or change, a section, the last place, `--select`, a Contents click) or it was already scrolled to another page (`PDFController.opensAtTop`, cleared by `willMove`).
- **The floating Comment toolbar** (M10, A2-01) is made once and moved into whichever PDF view is current, so it shows after a paper switch and after All Students and back.
- **A new reveal or place stops the scroll still running** (M11, A2-02; `PDFController.willMove`): two J presses within 0.25 s, a held J/K or a Contents click during an animated reveal end on the latest target.
- **⌥-click and ⌥-drag** that start off the page (between pages, beside one) do nothing (A2-03). **A right-click with nothing selected**: once the menu has closed and its item has run, the word PDFView selected for Look Up and Copy is cleared if it is still the selection, so no Comment toolbar is left over it (A2-05; since 2026-10-29 the app builds the menu itself and no word is selected at all, §35).
- **Jump** (A2-06): ↑/↓ and a new query scroll the list the least that shows the highlighted row; a hover highlights without scrolling. Closing Jump over the overview gives the keys back to the map, never to the hidden PDF (A2-04).
- **R, Return or Follow up on a card taller than the panel** whose end is above the view scrolls too: the end 16 pt above the bottom (A2-07, `ScrollMode.end`).
- **Id labels** take each page's text extent and ruler bounds from the engine (`round.build.text_x` and `rulers`, `PDFController.labelBounds`) instead of reading the page's text on the main thread, which cost 0.1–0.3 s per paper or round switch; PDFKit's `page.selection` is only the fallback for a page without them (A2-10). Labels land where they did within 0.5 pt for shipout rulers (ICLR, NeurIPS, eso-pic) and papers without one; for `lineno` numbers on the left they now sit outside the numbers (PDFKit reported text where nothing prints).
- **At most 4 round PDFs stay open for crops** (A2-09, `CropCache.openLimit`, least recently used closed first).
- **Review's single-letter keys** act only in the review window, never while Settings is key (A1-02).
- **The refused re-anchor callout** is white on a fixed #C42121 in both modes (5.9:1; A2-11). **Change marks** in the toolbar is disabled without a PDF (no rounds, a failed build), as Focus is (UI-11).

**Answers, cards and the panel.**
- **An answer in flight** (A1-01): its toast and Undo, the move to the next card and the click guard belong to the paper and round it was given on; if another paper or round is shown before the engine replies, it gets none of them, while the draft and the ⌘Z step still go to the paper answered. The same holds for the toasts of ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z.
- **The panel's toast** (A3a-04): after Undo (or Redo) it stays, now with Redo (or Undo), while the pointer rests on its button, and goes 1.5 s after the pointer leaves; a toast without a button is never held.
- **⋯ › Add Closing Remark** (Add Follow-up Note on a follow-up), on a carried comment of the open round answered without words, opens the empty editor in place, as Return does (A3b-06). **Edit** under this round's words is not disabled while the engine works; it says "Still loading…" (A3b-08, §11).
- **The inline editor's typing undo** is cleared when the app replaces the field's text (Discard), so a later ⌘Z can no longer reach text that is gone and crash (A3b-01).
- **Position uncertain** (A3b-02, `ExpandedCard.uncertainWords`): "The (faint) tint shows where the mark sits now…" only when the caption's section is the shown round's own text; when the shown round is only a tag on an earlier section: "The mark may be in the wrong place. Check it on p.N; if it is wrong, choose Re-anchor…".
- **A section with no mark and no cut** (a note's text) is trimmed from its start to its last underlined word, so an underline never hides behind Show all (A3b-03).
- **The Answers and "you asked about it in" chips wrap** on a change's card instead of being cut to "R…" (A3b-04). The Changes tab's deletion triangle is the card's, 9 pt at −2.5 (`CardContent.caret`, A3b-09; it was 6.5 pt).
- **A change's Show all** closes when its change card collapses: another change expanded, its group hidden, another paper or round (A3b-07).
- **A hint row** whose words changed is measured again before the table asks for its height, so a longer hint is never cut (A3a-02).

**Changes and Finish.**
- The Changes tab's first group reads "No comment asked for", grey, once every edit no comment asked for is handled (UI-02, §16).
- **The Finish sheet** (§17): Review beside "Comments still to check" shows the first card whose status is to check, skipping one answered while selected and still listed under To check (A3a-01, `FinishSummary.firstToCheck`); when nothing goes to the student, the one line "No follow-ups, new or closed comments" has no sign and no count (A3a-06, UI-03). A Finish and Copy Email refused after the sheet was closed (Cancel or Esc during Finishing…) shows the alert "Round N was not finished: <reason>", and the round stays open (A3a-03). A Finish Round… that reaches the model while the engine works says "Still loading… try again in a moment." (A3a-05).
- **Your Move's Finish Round N…** opens the sheet once that paper's latest open round is shown and the engine is free (after an engine update, once its paragraphs are found, or found to fail); the request is dropped when the advisor opens another paper or a hub page, goes to the overview, picks another round, or the round closes (A1-04).

**Starting and building rounds.**
- **Start Round 1 refused** (M16, E2E-04): the sheet "Start Round 1" opens with the engine's words ("Several files could be the main file: draft.tex, paper.tex. Choose one."), the chosen source kept, an empty Main file field with the keys, and Engine "Automatic" while no engine is known, so the engine's own detection still runs. It stays open while the round builds, with its progress; a second refusal shows in it; it closes once Round 1 exists. The sheet's title is "Build Round N Again" when it replaces the open round, else "Start Round N".
- **Build Round N Again…** (E2E-05, `AppModel.canBuildAgain`): a link in the build's Details popover while the round shown is the latest, open, and has no comment written, verdict given or comment reopened in it (CONTRACT §6's re-ingest rule), after any build with a PDF, with errors or not; it opens the same sheet with the source, main file and engine filled in. A failed build keeps Retry….
- **A paper that cannot be opened** (A1-06): "This paper could not be opened", the engine's words and [Try Again] (§11), with no alert; the round menu is hidden while there is no state.

**The overview, the paper map and the hub.**
- **Thumbnails** are read back from the cache folder once the in-memory cache has emptied, instead of staying blank until the next launch (M9, A4b-01).
- **The map**: no Fold under the axis when folding gains nothing, while a part the viewer showed keeps its Fold (A4a-02); a part the strip's round lacks, such as a latest round without its appendix, is shown and folded under the top shown row that has it (A4a-04); the folded older rounds, their Fold and the axis's Show and Fold are VoiceOver buttons whose action is a click there ("Show the appendix, pages 17 to 29"; `MapSpeech.controls`, A4a-05), and an open round says "n followed up so far" (A4a-06); the Full Key's first item says that a short section is drawn wider (A4a-07); the table view's names give way first (to 100 pt), then its columns (to 52 pt), and a table that still does not fit scrolls sideways (`MapTableView.widths`, A4a-08); no two cells of a row show the same short name: a section whose every fitting form is another section's name shows only its whole name or fixed short form where one fits, else just its count, and the hover names still give it as much as fits (`SectionName.nameless`, UI-10). Turning Settings › Overview's fold switch redraws the map at once (A4a-03). The map's regex, fonts and date formatters are made once (M17).
- **The strip**: a cell is exactly as wide as its thumbnail, and the row under it (○, the key page's bookmark, the number) is centred on it and may run past it; on a crowded strip the key page shows its bookmark alone (A4b-04, `StripLabels.shows`). "No PDF for Round N: the build failed." only when that round's build failed, else "No pages yet." ("Reading the rounds…" while loading); a strip round the data no longer has, after Put Back, falls back to the latest that built (A4b-06).
- **The hub**: Back to a hub page scrolled near its bottom puts it back at once (A4b-05); a Your Move chip's title gives way to its words' measured width, so the chip stays in its column (UI-06); a student's revision done the same day "took under a day", and "You returned each round the same day." (UI-08); Your Move takes a paper's round from the papers list until the hierarchy comes, and never says "Round 0" ("Finish Round…", "Open Review"; A4b-10). Mark as (Not) Finished reloads the hierarchy page shown, and a summaries or hierarchy reply asked before an edit no longer writes its older facts back over the list's (A4b-02). Map Headings… Done saves only the papers whose answers changed and asks for the hierarchy once; Rename Student… asks once after all its edits (A4b-08). Rename Student's sentence agrees with the count: "Maya Chen’s paper gets this name.", "All 3 papers of Maya Chen get this name." (UI-07). A new round clears a paper's Finished mark (the engine's ingest, A4b-03).

**Tags and Settings.**
- **Settings › Tags** asks for the counts again whenever `AppModel.hubKey` changes (ledger edits, the picker's order, the papers, Map Headings answers, key pages, activation, rounds found), so − is no longer left disabled after +, a reorder or an app switch (M14, A5a-01). **Tag Comments…**: Return in the New Tag… field adds the tag; Done is the default button only otherwise (M15, A5a-02).
- **Restore Default Names** is disabled when it would change nothing (`TagCatalog.restorable`), else asks first ("Restore the default names?", with Restore and Cancel); definitions and next-draft lines come back, and a default name that another tag has meanwhile stays as it is, so no name is ever there twice (A5a-06, A5a-10).
