Emptying a tag's name field puts its stored name back, with nothing written (A5a-07). The sign menus offer only signs no other tag has, each by its name ("Question Mark", "Letters"; `TagCatalog.signNames`; A5a-08, A5a-13). When the papers cannot be read, the counts' help says "Your papers could not be read: <reason>" (A5a-12).
- **Suggestions** (§24): the box rule needs PDF words known to be none, and the `$` rule reads the source of a comment on selected text (A5a-03, A5a-05, E3-10). Tag Comments… suggests for the shown paper's comments as their cards do, and from the note and kind only for other papers' comments, so their boxes no longer all get Figures & tables. A note's word hits are kept per note (A5a-04).

**Data & Storage** (§28; `design/data/DATA.md`).
- **Restore and Import defaults** (M2, A5b-03): Use This Copy is preselected only when the copy is newer or yours is damaged; an older, identical or diverged backup stays at Keep Mine, so one click never puts an older copy over yours. A Finder `.DS_Store` no longer makes a finished round damaged (the engine, M1).
- **Backups after a move to a new Mac** (M13, A5b-02): a recorded volume that no longer matches means "“X” isn’t connected" only for a folder under /Volumes; for a folder elsewhere the next backup records the new volume, and Restore from Last Backup… stays offered.
- **The import preview**: the first group is "Will be listed again", each row saying where ("Back where it was: ~/…", "Listed where it is now: ~/…", "Its old place is taken or gone: into the folder below."); a listed paper whose folder was moved in Finder is reported under "Listed where it is" (A5b-11). A "yours damaged" row shows both copies, then "Yours is damaged: <the first problem that is not harmless> This copy is intact." (A5b-05). A paper listed through a symlinked parent is "The very folder in your list." (A5b-12). Use This Copy closes the paper if it was opened while earlier papers came in, or stops that paper, yours unchanged, while a new comment's note is being written (A5b-04).
- **Cancel** is a quiet line, never an error: "Cancelled. Nothing was written."; an import stopped between papers reports "Imported n of m papers" (A5b-13). **Export and Import** send their archive requests to an engine process of their own, ended after each job, so the review never waits behind an archive; Cancel goes to the process running the request (A5b-10). **Show in Finder** of something not there says so on the window's bottom line from the window (the paper's context menu, the sheets), on the status line from Settings, and never while a job runs there (A5b-07). **Unsent drafts** in Settings and in the quit question count only listed papers; a Put Back to a new place moves the paper's drafts (A1-05, A5b-06). Restore onto a folder that lost its ledger.json works (the engine, A5b-01).

**Test runs** (§28): one rule, `LaunchOptions.isTestRun`; a background run never fills in the paragraphs of the listed papers it read (the diff review of 2026-10-25 found the overview's Backfill did).

**Not done** (BACKLOG "代码审查之后"): Put Back an Earlier Ledger… still lists the current ledger as an earlier version right after an ingest, Finish or Reopen (UI-04, it needs the current rev from the engine); dropping a .zip on the window (E2E-09) waits for the advisor's decision; the review's §5.3 left the rest out on purpose.

**Checked** (2026-10-25): engine 413 passed, 2 skipped; `swift test` 662 passing (new: `ModelFixTests`, `PDFPaneFixTests`, `PaperMapFixTests`, `CommentListFixTests`, `RenameStudentTests`, `CrossFileFixTests`, `LabelExtentTests`, plus cases in the existing classes). A final pass of a bundle built from the fixes, on synthetic copies of `samples/promo-demo` (`--background --no-list`), light and dark: Review open at the top of p.1, the Comment toolbar after a paper switch, a card, the Changes tab, the Finish sheet ("No follow-ups, new or closed comments" without a sign), All Students, the paper map, Settings › Tags (Restore Default Names disabled with the defaults) and › Data & Storage, and final.pdf's summary and first page.

## 31. Resume Round N (2026-10-25)

The advisor's request of 2026-10-25: after finishing a round they sometimes find more to change. Until the student's next version starts the next round, the latest round, once finished, can be opened again as it was: no source is loaded again and nothing is built. The engine's side is CONTRACT's amendment "Resume a finished round" (`resume_round`). "Resume", not "Reopen": Reopen is a comment's (§24). Where §17 ("Nothing can be edited" in a closed round) differs, this wins.

**Where it shows.** Only for the paper's latest round, only while it is finished, as the engine says it (`round.resumable` for the round shown, `summary.resumable` for the paper; `AppModel.resumable`, `canResumeShown`): once the next round is ingested, the round before it stays finished for good. Never for an earlier round shown (its box keeps Back to Round N), an open round, or a state without the flag (a fixture, an engine before Resume). Mouse first, in four places, each named "Resume Round 2":
- a plain small button in the read-only box at the top of the panel, the lock, "Round 2 · closed Oct 21 · read only", and [Resume Round 2] at the right (in a narrow panel the box drops "· read only", then the day, never the button);
- the round menu's last item, under a divider;
- the overview's More (⋯) menu, after Open Round N final.pdf;
- the paper's context menu (the sidebar, All Students, Your Move, a student's page), after Open in Review; for a paper not open, from the papers list's summary.

**The confirmation** (an alert on the window; `ResumeAsk`): "Resume Round 2?" / "You can add and change comments, then finish the round again. The final.pdf and email from Oct 21 stay in the round’s folder." [Cancel] [Resume] (the default). While the engine works (a Resume already sent, the next round being built) Resume only beeps. Resume sends `resume_round`: the engine moves r2/final.pdf, email.txt and the checksums into r2/earlier/ ("final 2026-10-21 1530.pdf", …), keeps the ledger's version from before in its history for good, and opens the round with its comments, answers and their notes, Reopens, checked changes and tags as they were (the unsent drafts were dropped by Finish, §17; the paragraphs Finish marked read are unread again). Review then shows the paper's latest round, open, on the Comments tab, from the overview, a hub page or another paper's context menu too (Back returns there), and the PDF's toast says "Round 2 is open again · finish it again when you are done". A refusal is the window's alert, and nothing changes.

**Then** the round is open as any open round: Finish Round… in the toolbar, the cards' answers, new comments, edits and deletes; only Build Round N Again… is not offered (the engine's ingest refuses to replace a round finished before, which would delete rN/earlier/). Undo starts afresh: the answers before the earlier Finish are not undone from here. The paper is your move again (amber) in the sidebar, All Students and Your Move, from its summary; the hierarchy asks again as after any edit (`ledgerEdits`). The Finish sheet of a resumed round has one quiet line under its first sentence, in the secondary colour: "Finished before on Oct 21. Those files are kept in the round’s folder." with Show in Finder (a link that selects the kept final.pdf and email; `FinishSummary.finishedBefore`, `keptFiles`). Finish and Copy Email writes a new final.pdf, email.txt and checksums as usual. Nothing in the email or final.pdf mentions the earlier finish: the advisor adds their own words when sending. The round can be resumed again until the next round comes in. No colour of its own: the buttons are plain, amber stays "your move", nothing is red.

**Elsewhere.** Put Back an Earlier Ledger… names that copy "before a round was resumed"; put back while the round is open again, it finishes the round as it was, its files and its list of changes back. Export, backups and the quiet check carry and check rN/earlier/ (the engine's `earlier_changed`, shown as any damage, §28). The debug item `resume [ask|cancel|yes] [<path>]` (§13) asks, cancels or resumes as the button and the menus do.

**Checked** (2026-10-25): `swift test` 671 passing (new: `ResumeTests`, 9: the new state fields, where Resume shows and where it never does, the confirmation's and the Finish sheet's words, the history's label, Undo afresh and no Build Again in a resumed round, and with the engine a synthetic round 1 resumed from the overview, open in Review on the Comments tab, its kept files in r1/earlier/, then finished again). Two background runs of a bundle built from this (`--background --no-list`, synthetic copies of `samples/promo-demo`, Sara Okafor's round 1 finished), light and dark: the read-only box with Resume Round 1 (and at the panel's 320 pt, "Round 1 · closed"), the confirmation over the overview and over All Students (as the paper's context menu), the round open again (Finish Round… in the toolbar, Your Move 1 → 2, "Round 1 · first draft, in progress"), a new comment R1-06 in it, the Finish sheet's "Finished before on Oct 25. Those files are kept in the round’s folder. Show in Finder", Finish again (Resume Round 1 back in the box); on disk r1/final.pdf, email.txt (with R1-06) and the checksums new and `shasum -c` clean, r1/earlier/ holding the earlier final.pdf, email and checksums.

## 32. Find in the paper, re-editing marks, Finish without the email, the round's card (2026-10-26)
The advisor's requests from real use: a search in the PDF while revising; no email at Finish ("its text is mainly the comments, and the PDF alone is enough"); a card at the round's end that shows what the comments came to, "with a sense of achievement", exported as an elegant image; and a mark easy to change after it was made, a highlight's colour above all.

**Find in the paper** (`Find.swift`: `PaperFind`, `PaperSearcher`, `FindBar`, `FindRules`). Over the PDF's top right corner, clear of its scroller (moved there from the end of Review's toolbar on 2026-10-29: the advisor found a search box far from the page it searches odd): a small round magnifier button while closed; open, a bar with the search field "Find in Paper", "3 of 12", ˄ ˅ and × (its background solid, not a material, which went a muddy grey over the white page in Dark). The button or Edit › Find in Paper… (⌘F) opens it and puts the cursor in the field, its text selected; Jump's "Find … in the paper" opens it too; Esc in the field or × clears the query and closes it back to the button. Typing searches the shown round's PDF 0.15 s after the last key, any case and any accents (PDFKit's `findString`), in a document of its own made from the PDF's bytes on a queue of its own, so a long paper never holds up the window; a search asked for while another runs waits, and is skipped when a newer one comes first. The matches are drawn in the find's neutral grey (`Palette.find`, never a mark's colour) as the PDF view's highlighted selections, laid about 12 ms at a time with the window's events between: no annotation, so the marks, a click on one and the reading bars are untouched. **Only while no page has more than 25 matches** (and there are at most 1,500; `FindRules.highlightsAll`): PDFKit spends about 1 ms on each highlight in view whenever it draws or is handed them, so one letter typed in a 32-page paper (7,900 matches, some 250 a page) held the window up 100 to 600 ms at a time (measured in the review of 2026-10-26); with more, only the current match is shown. The current match is the PDF's selection, so the floating toolbar comments on it (§7), has the accent's 2 pt outline as a selected comment's mark (§5; PDFKit draws the selection grey while the field has the keys), and is shown as a mark is: scrolled to a third of the way down when it is off screen, then flashed. A query typed on stays on the match shown while it is on screen (or the next one that still matches), else starts at the first at or below the top of the view. Return and ˅ (⌘G) go to the next match, ⇧Return and ˄ (⇧⌘G) to the one before, wrapping; Esc or the field's ⊗ clears the query, the highlights and the match's selection (a selection made since is left alone), and Esc gives the keys back to the PDF. Another round or paper keeps the query and finds it again there, with none shown until Return (the PDF opens where it opens). The bar keeps its width whatever it shows, so nothing moves as a query is typed. Jump's last row (§20) and Edit › Find Next / Previous in Paper are this find.

**Re-editing a mark** (`MarkToolbar.swift`, §8). A click on a new comment's mark in an open round selects it, as ever (§6), and shows a toolbar by the mark, laid as the selection's toolbar is (§7: centred 8 pt above it past its outline, or below it; `FloatPlace`): the six swatches (the current one ringed), the kinds of a text comment (a box or a note keeps its kind), Edit Note (the note opens in its card) and Delete… (the panel's dialog). A swatch or kind applies at once (`AppModel.setStyle`, the engine's `edit_comment`), the mark is drawn again and the toolbar stays with it. It goes on a click elsewhere, a scroll or zoom, Esc, a text selection or a box (whose toolbar takes its place), the new-comment editor, re-anchoring, another round or paper, and when the comment is gone. A carried comment's mark shows none: its card is answered, and clicking marks is how a round is read. In the expanded card's header, in an open round, the mark's **colour dot** (10 pt) before the page opens the same swatches and kinds in a popover, for any comment of the open round (a carried one: colour only); a pick closes it. **Undo**: a restyle from any of these, `⋯`'s menus or 1–6 is a step of the open round's history with the answers (§8, `UndoStep`): ⌘Z puts back what it changed ("Undone · R2-01 is yellow", "… is an underline", "… is a teal highlight") and ⇧⌘Z makes it again; typing in a card's field drops only that card's answer steps, and deleting a comment drops its steps.

**Finish without the email** (§17). The Finish sheet's right column shows the round's card so far in place of the email; its default button is [Finish Round], which writes final.pdf and closes the round. Nothing goes on the clipboard. The sheet then turns into the round's whole card, headed "Round 3 is finished · final.pdf is ready · Show in Finder · Open"; Done (or Esc) closes it and the window's line says the same with [Show in Finder] and [Open] (`WindowNotice.second`). The engine still writes email.txt (backups and exports keep it; `email_preview` is no longer asked for); a closed round's [final.pdf] in the toolbar is a menu button whose click opens the file and whose menu has Show in Finder and Copy Email Text, for the rare case. The [Email…] button, its sheet and Jump's Round N Email… are gone, and the words that said where things go now say final.pdf: "Goes out in the Round 3 final.pdf", "Closing remark · in final.pdf", "For the student · goes in the Round 3 final.pdf", the follow-up's help tags, and "sent Oct 17" (was "emailed Oct 17") on an earlier round's turn.

**The round's card** (`RoundStats.swift`, `RoundCard.swift`; design/stats-card, the "Ledger" card). Every number comes from the round's state (`state {paper, round: n}`), so the engine needs nothing new:
- **The words** large in New York: the words of the round's new comments' notes and of this round's follow-ups and closing remarks, counted by the engine's rule (overview.WORD: a CJK character is a word, a run of other characters holding a letter or digit is one; `CardWords`), "in your notes, follow-ups and closing remarks" naming only the kinds present.
- **The figures** beside it: round 1 New, "Pages with a mark · 3 of 5" and Done well (the praise tag, when some); later rounds New, Resolved, Followed up and Kept as is, and with no new comment the three answers and Left open (when some).
- **What they were about**: the new comments' tags but praise, in the catalogue's order, a row each with a vermilion square per comment and the count (a comment with two tags counts in both); past seven rows (six on the compact card) the smallest fold into "Also Structure 1 · Math & notation 1 · 1 not tagged" ("Also 3 more tags", the names in its help tag, on the compact card); ★ "2 done well" on the right. No tag section without such a tag.
- **Where your pen went**: a small page for each page of the round's PDF, in its shape, two columns of faint lines as texture (never the paper's text), and a vermilion stroke where each comment written or followed up this round starts, at its height and in its column (a frame for a box, a dot for a note, a shorter stroke for a follow-up).
- **The student** (from round 2): "The student · 27 edits, 6 of their own · 5 notes on your comments"; **the facts**, the first two that hold, after a vermilion ¶ in italic New York: "R1-01 is resolved after two rounds.", "All four earlier comments answered, every follow-up with a note.", "Busiest page: p.3, with five of your comments.", "Longest note: 52 words, on R3-03.", "Finished the day it came in." Only facts: no better or worse, no comparison with another round or student.
- **The look**: on a warm paper (`CardPalette`, #F7F3EA; #1D1E1C in Dark Mode), the brand's brushed ring in vermilion (#C04734; #DB6A50 dark) with the round's numeral, vermilion only for the marks, the ring, ¶ and ★ (never for words), the words in the palette's label colours; "Paperole" and a small ring at the foot. Two forms: the compact card in the Finish sheet ("Round 3 so far · Oct 21 – today · 3 days", asked again with every engine reply) and the whole card in the Summary sheet.
- **Its height** (2026-10-26: a round with one new comment left the lower half of a fixed 4:5 card empty): 540 pt wide and as tall as what it holds, from a square (540 pt) to 4:5 (675 pt; `RoundCardView.heights`). A sparse card at its square gives the room to its pages first (a bigger proof sheet: pages up to 96 pt wide without a tag section, 54 with one, in the same rows; `PageStrip.roomyPage`), then to the gaps between its parts, evenly, never one stretch; a full one at 4:5 lets its facts give way (two, then one, then none). The image is that card, not a fixed 4:5 with the composition scaled up: what the sheet shows is what is exported, a small round stays a small round, and a square and 4:5 are both shapes a feed shows whole.
- **The Summary sheet**: right after Finish Round, and later from the round menu's Round N Summary… (the shown round, when finished), the overview's More (Round N Summary…, or Round Summary › Round N… once several are finished) and Jump. "Show on the card: [ ] Paper’s title [ ] Student’s name", both off every time (the card leaves out who and what unless asked); Copy Image (PNG and TIFF; a `--background` run's own pasteboard) and Export Image… (a save panel, "Round 3.png", the title first only when it is on the card): 1080 px wide and 1080 to 1350 px tall, twice the card, at 144 dpi, in the window's appearance. The sheet is as tall as the card needs, within the window's content height, and the card is scaled down when the window is short.

**Checked** (2026-10-26): `swift test` (new: `FindRulesTests`, `PaperFindTests`, `MarkToolbarTests`, `RoundCardTests`); the engine suite; a background instance on synthetic reviews in Light and Dark (the find, the mark's toolbar and colour dot, the Finish sheet and the card, the toolbar at 1100 and 1440 pt in a closed round). Debug hooks: `find`, `findkey`, `findclick`, `markbar`, `markbarclick`, `dot`, `summary`, and `finishsheet … finish|done` (§13).

## 33. The rename to Paperole (2026-10-27)

*Completed the same day (§34): the ids, the data's places, the formats' names, the Swift package and the engine package are Paperole's too, and the app moves its data at its first launch. What this section kept is §34's starting point.*

The advisor's decision: the app is **Paperole**, and its development copy **Paperole Dev**. Only what the advisor sees is renamed; whatever names stored data keeps the name PaperCollab, so the papers, tags, settings, drafts, thumbnails and backups made before open as they were, and the official app's frozen PaperCollab (until it is replaced) still reads Paperole Dev's exports.

| | Paperole Dev | Paperole |
|---|---|---|
| Bundle | mac/build/Paperole Dev.app (`build_app.sh`) | /Applications/Paperole.app (`release.sh` only) |
| Name (`CFBundleName`, `CFBundleDisplayName`), menus, About, alerts, window title | Paperole Dev | Paperole |
| Executable | Contents/MacOS/PaperoleDev | Contents/MacOS/Paperole |
| Bundle id = defaults domain (unchanged) | `com.papercollab.app.dev` | `com.papercollab.app` |
| Data and caches (unchanged) | …/Application Support/PaperCollab Dev/, …/Caches/PaperCollab Dev/ | …/Application Support/PaperCollab/, …/Caches/PaperCollab/ |

- **Two names in `AppVariant`**: `name` (Paperole, Paperole Dev) wherever the app shows itself; `storageName` (PaperCollab, PaperCollab Dev) for the folders and for backup.json's `made_by.variant`, which the app keeps writing so older apps still tell Dev's files. A file's variant is read by either name (`AppVariant(stored:)`) and shown as the app's name now ("Made … by Paperole Dev"); Restore from Last Backup… and the rotation take either. The format's name, backup.json's `"app": "PaperCollab"` (engine `archive.FORMAT`), is unchanged.
- **Renamed, as shown**: the menu bar, About, the window's title, alerts, Settings' text, the round card's footer, the names suggested for new files ("Paperole – Maya Chen – 2026-10-27.zip", "Paperole Tags.json", "Paperole Dev Backup … .zip"), and the engine's text: the summary page's header ("Paperole · Round N review · <date>"), the PDF's Creator and Producer, an export's README.txt and its error messages.
- **Kept**: Dev's default review folder `~/PaperCollab Dev Reviews`; a backup folder of the old name ("PaperCollab Dev Backups") is the one Back up automatically suggests where it is, else "Paperole Dev Backups"; an existing backup's folder is kept in its settings as it was. The engine's hidden `.PaperCollab-import-…` folders, the temporary folders' names, the log lines, the Swift package, target and module, and the engine package `papercollab`.
- **The scripts**: `build_app.sh` makes the new bundle names and never removes an old `build/PaperCollab Dev.app`. `release.sh` refuses while Paperole or the old PaperCollab runs (same bundle id, same data), installs /Applications/Paperole.app, never removes /Applications/PaperCollab.app and, while it is there, says so: the advisor checks Paperole, then moves the old app to the Trash. `tools/shot.sh` without `--pid` looks for windows of "Paperole Dev" only.

## 34. Completing the rename: ids, data, migration, one icon (2026-10-27)

The advisor's decision, later the same day as §33: finish the rename in code and data, with the migration the data needs; Dev's default review folder may move, with what is in it; the old icons stay in design/logo as history but leave Settings and the app (Paperole only, light and dark; the style setting stays); the promo video is left as it is. Kept on purpose: the repository's folder and the GitHub repository's name (the venv's paths, `EngineClient.defaultPython`).

| | Paperole Dev | Paperole |
|---|---|---|
| Bundle id = defaults domain | `com.paperole.app.dev` (was `com.papercollab.app.dev`) | `com.paperole.app` (was `com.papercollab.app`) |
| Data (papers.json, tags.json, drafts) | ~/Library/Application Support/Paperole Dev/ | ~/Library/Application Support/Paperole/ |
| Caches (thumbnails) | ~/Library/Caches/Paperole Dev/ | ~/Library/Caches/Paperole/ |
| New review folders by default | ~/Paperole Dev Reviews (was ~/PaperCollab Dev Reviews) | ~/Reviews |
| backup.json `"app"` / `made_by.variant` written | "Paperole" / "Paperole Dev" | "Paperole" / "Paperole" |
| Engine | engine/paperole of the checkout, on PYTHONPATH | Contents/Resources/engine/paperole |

The Swift package, target and module are `Paperole` (Sources/Paperole, Tests/PaperoleTests); the engine package `paperole` (`python -m paperole.cli`); the import's hidden folder `.Paperole-import-…`; queue labels, log lines and temporary names say Paperole.

**Reading the old names, forever.** backup.json `"app"` "PaperCollab" or "Paperole" (`archive.FORMATS`, `AppVariant.readsFormat`); `made_by.variant` by either name (`AppVariant(stored:)`), shown as the new one; `.PaperCollab-import-…` leftovers swept with the new ones; a backup folder of the old name ("PaperCollab Dev Backups") still suggested where it is, and a chosen backup folder never moved. Ledgers, papers.json, tags.json and drafts hold no app name: no schema changed. The frozen old PaperCollab.app refuses the new files cleanly ("not a Paperole export"; its check is as old as the format), so **release.sh must install the new Paperole before any paper moves from Dev to the official app.**

**Where the data is** (`DataHome`, worked out once per run, after the migration): a data folder of the new name, else one of the old, else the new name. Both, and no finished migration in the new one: the old one, nothing moved or merged, and an alert once ("Found both … Nothing was moved or merged; Paperole Dev uses …"). Caches: the new folder, else the old. Dev's default review folder: the new one once the migration moved it, else the old one where it is, else the new. A test run takes the new names and never looks at the disk for them.

**The migration** (`Migration.swift`), at the start of `App.init`, before anything reads the data or the settings:
- **Only at a real launch of an installed bundle** (`Migration.gate`): the exact new bundle id at its exact place, links resolved (`/Applications/Paperole.app`, or the main checkout's `mac/build/Paperole Dev.app`, written into the code: a test bundle, a worktree's build or a copy elsewhere never matches), no launch argument but the system's `-psn_…` (so `--background`, `--paper`, `--fixture`, `--no-list`, any `--debug-…` all keep it off), not a test run, not `swift test`. Another copy of the same app running under the new or the old id (or `swift run` for Dev): an alert, "Quit … first, then open … again.", and the app quits having touched nothing. A lock on the data folder against two copies opened at once.
- **Before anything changes**: the plan (each move: the old place a real folder, not a link; nothing at the new place; the same disk), and copies in a new folder `pre-paperole-<date>-<time>/` in the old data folder of papers.json, every draft file to be rewritten, and both settings domains as plists; then the journal `migration.json` (state "running", the plan, the steps begun). A failure here: nothing moved, the old places used.
- **The steps**, each written in the journal before it is done: the data folder renamed (`renamex_np` with `RENAME_EXCL`: never over anything, never across disks, never a copy); Dev's review folder renamed the same way; papers.json's `path` of each paper in the review folder rewritten (the folder itself or a path inside it: "…Reviews2" is not), nothing else in it changed; each such paper's drafts file renamed to the name its new path hashes to, its `paper` rewritten; read back (as many papers, every folder that was there is there); the settings merged into the new domain until it has a `migratedFrom` marker: missing settings come from the old domain, explicit new app preferences are kept, and the old `installID` follows the data so existing automatic backups stay this installation's. Paths from both domains are rewritten (`reviewFolderRoot`, `lastPlace`, `backups`, inside their JSON too); `appIcon` is dropped, `migratedFrom` added, the domain synchronized and read back; a synchronization failure is an error. The old domain is never written. Last the journal says "done": the marker. Then the thumbnails' folder, best effort.
- **A step that fails** puts back everything done, last first (the settings from the copy, the drafts and papers.json from the copies, the folders renamed back). The settings must synchronize and read back correctly; `migration.json` must say "rolled_back" and read back as written before the app may continue. A failure in either stops the launch, including when all files are already back but the journal cannot record it. A successful rollback runs on the old places and registers the old settings as fallback values for that process (not another persistent copy), so backups, the engine path and the installation identity remain available. New preferences saved during that run no longer prevent the next settings migration.
- **Killed midway**: the next launch finds the journal "running" and recovers it before retrying. Support is moved first and restored last: if it is already at its old place, recovery only records the completed rollback and never replays snapshots over newer drafts, paper-list edits or settings. This also works for older journals without file hashes. For a move still in the new data folder, changed file contents, two draft filenames for one draft, or conflicting review folders stop recovery; it never skips a changed file and then declares the rollback complete. Copies and journals are kept for recovery.
- Not moved, and left as it is, with a line in the log: a link or a file where the old data folder was, another disk; the review folder a link, on another disk, its new name taken, or papers.json unreadable (the review folder and its paths stay; the data folder moves; the app sets the list aside as ever) or a draft's new name taken.
- **`migration.log`** in the data folder in use (paths, counts, the result), a copy in the copies' folder; NSLog has the steps and counts only. A move that worked says nothing in the UI. The app never deletes the old domains, the copies' folder or old thumbnails: the advisor may, once satisfied.
- Ledgers are never rewritten: build paths are relative, a round's `source` is history (Build Round N Again… may suggest a source that has moved), and synctex's absolute input names are resolved in the moved folder (engine `tests/test_moved_folder.py`). Thumbnails are drawn again under the new paths' hashes. A backup's `was_at` is matched by the ledger's id. macOS asks again for folder access (a new bundle id).

**The old apps afterwards**: the frozen /Applications/PaperCollab.app finds no data folder (an empty list) and reads none of the new files: move it to the Trash and replace it in the Dock; never import into it. An old Dev bundle (build/PaperCollab Dev.app, or a Paperole Dev.app built before this) finds no data and cannot start the engine any more (`-m papercollab.cli` is gone), so it cannot write anything. The new apps ignore an old folder made again.

**The icon**: `AppIcon` is Paperole's two .icns (`paperole`, `paperole-dark`; Dev's bundle icon `paperole-dev`), the only ones in Resources/AppIcons; The Round's four and its DEV icon are in design/logo/red/icns. Settings has five tabs; General has "App icon" Automatic | Light | Dark (segmented, "Automatic follows the system’s light or dark appearance."). The Dock, About and alerts follow the style as before; Finder shows the bundle's own light icon (the app never sets a custom one any more; a rebuilt or reinstalled bundle has none). `--icon` and the debug item's icon names are gone (`--icon-style` and `icon automatic|light|dark` stay). An old export's `appIcon` setting is ignored.

**The scripts**: `build_app.sh` makes the new ids, copies `engine/paperole` into the release bundle, copies only Paperole's icons, and without `APP_OUT` refuses while Paperole Dev or the old PaperCollab Dev runs. `release.sh` installs with the new id, refuses while either official app (or `swift run`, whose binary is now `Paperole`) runs, and says what the first launch will move. `tools/dev_icon.sh` makes paperole-dev.icns from `DevMark.swift` alone.

**The advisor's switch-over**, after the migration fixes pass review and tests and Dev has been rebuilt without being launched:

1. Quit all Paperole / Paperole Dev and old PaperCollab copies yourself. Before the first new launch, use Finder or Time Machine to back up `~/Library/Application Support/PaperCollab Dev/`, `~/PaperCollab Dev Reviews/` and any other chosen review locations that exist. The old Dev cannot export: its `papercollab` engine entry point is gone. The migration's own copies protect files it rewrites; they are not a second copy of every review folder.
2. Open the rebuilt `mac/build/Paperole Dev.app` from Finder or the Dock, without arguments. This is the real Dev migration. Allow macOS folder-access prompts. Check paper and student counts, unsent drafts, tags, the last place, the chosen review folder, automatic backups (enabled state and destination), and `migration.log` in the data folder in use. If recovery stops, keep the folders, journal and `pre-paperole-*` copies for diagnosis.
3. When ready, quit both official app names and run `mac/release.sh` from the repository root. It installs `/Applications/Paperole.app` without launching it, replacing a display-only Paperole build there too. Open it yourself without arguments; check the same data and settings. An old `/Applications/PaperCollab.app`, if present, is left in place by the script.
4. Only after the new official app is installed and checked, export from Dev and import into it. An official list that points inside Dev's renamed default review folder needs those folders located again; Dev never reads the official list to update it.
5. After both apps are checked, move the obsolete Dev/official bundles to the Trash yourself and update the Dock. Keep migration copies and old settings domains until satisfied. The ignored old engine cache and editable-install metadata do not affect the new app and need not be removed for the switch.

**Checked**: `MigrationTests` (on a Mac made up in a temporary folder, the settings in memory: the gate; another copy running; the path rewrite; nothing to move; Dev's first launch, renamed not copied, the two papers' paths and drafts only, the settings rewritten without `appIcon`, the copies byte for byte, the log, and a second launch changing nothing; the official app's; never over anything there; a failure at each step putting everything back; a failed undo stopping; a migration cut off at four points put back and done at the next launch; an unreadable list; another disk, links and files never copied; settings merged until marked, rollback completion written and read back, completed rollback retried without replaying snapshots (old and new journals), partial-recovery conflicts, defaults restoration failure, fallback preferences, lastPlace saved before retry and the old install identity), `VariantTests`, `AppIconTests`, `BackupTests` and `DataTransferTests` (both names read, the new written), engine `test_archive.py` (writes "Paperole", reads "PaperCollab", refuses others; the new staging name) and `test_moved_folder.py` (a review folder built, renamed whole, then read, commented through synctex, re-anchored and finished as before).

## 35. The PDF's menu by target, zoom out of the menu (2026-10-29)
Steps P0.1 and P0.2 of `design/pdf-reader/PLAN.md` (§3, §3.6, §4.1; its §7 has the advisor's decisions). App only; no engine change.

**The right-click menu** (`PDFController.menu`, `PDFMenus`). The app builds it itself; `super.menu(for:)` is no longer called, so PDFKit's items are gone (zoom, the four page layouts, Next and Previous Page, Search with Google and Translate, which send the text away) and a right-click selects no word (A2-05's clearing went with it). The first target under the pointer with an item that can act decides, the targets tried one after another (a later one's builder never runs, so the mark's selecting its comment never clears a selection that won); items that cannot act are left out (the card's own ⋯ menu keeps its greyed ones). Off the pages there is no menu.
- **On the selection** (the pointer on a selected line): in an open round, not busy, not re-anchoring, Comment…, Strike Out (a strikeout with no note: "delete this") and Replace With… (the new-comment editor titled "Replace with", kind strikeout with no style row for kinds, the field's placeholder "The new words"; saved, the note reads "Replace with “were”."; with no words it is a plain strikeout); then Copy (`AppModel.clipboard`), Find “…” in Paper (the find opened with the words, from their first match in the paper: `PaperFind.find(_:fromStart:)`) and, for one word, Look Up “word” (the system's Look Up, `showDefinition`; it may search online). A right-click away from the selection is on what is there.
- **On a comment's mark** (any of its marks or its label; not while re-anchoring): the right-click selects it first, as a click does (the one already selected while it is among the marks there; no cycling), and in a click's order: id labels first, then on the Changes tab the changed words before the comments' marks (changes that answer a comment lie under its mark), on the Comments tab after them. A carried comment in an open round: Resolve, Follow Up…, Keep as Is (the current answer ticked; again clears, as on the card), answered in place, moving nothing on, as in Focus (decision 3; `AppModel.press(_:for:stay:)`), nor does the choice row such an answer asks for on a card with an unsent draft (`EditState.Choice.advance`); Follow Up… with no draft puts the cursor in the card's field. Then the card's ⋯ items that can act (`CardMenus.more`, the same code; `PDFMenus.actionable` drops the greyed ones and submenus with none). Where marks overlap, Other Comments Here ▸ "R2-09 · its note…", which selects that one. **On a change's band** (the changed words on the Changes tab; on the Comments tab the band under them, with no comment's mark there), in an open round: the change is selected (Changes tab), then Mark Checked (ticked once checked; again clears) and Comment on It….
- **On a link** (the PDF's own link annotations): Go to p.N (the target's page); in an open round Comment on “<its words>”… (its words selected, then the editor); for a URL Open in Browser and Copy Link, each a group of its own. Under a mark the mark's menu wins. Since P0.3 (§37), with the round's link table (and on the links the engine found in the source), Preview <Label>, Go to <Label> · p.N (the target, flashed whole) and Keep <Label> Open come first, then a citation's Copy Reference; a figure the table knows has its own menu, led by Back (§37).
- **On the page**: in an open round Add Note Here and Draw a Box (B's twin: box mode armed for one drag); Look Up “word” over a word; in an open round, not busy, Mark Paragraph Read / Unread while reading marks are on (§18); Set p.N as Key Page (or Use the Busiest Page), in any round, last. Since P0.4 (§36) Back to p.N · <section> comes first while Back is a step in the PDF.
- Not built: Comment on Figure N… (a figure's box): the app has no float boxes without an engine change (the reading paragraphs carry no kind); it came with the engine's link table (P0.3, §37: on a figure, table or algorithm the table knows, Comment on Figure N…, Keep Figure N Open and Cited on ▸). Copy LaTeX Source, Show Source, Copy Figure as Image (P1.2), Search Google Scholar (P1.3).

**Zoom** (`PDFController.setZoom`, `zoomStep`, `zoom(by:at:)`, `pinched`, `applyZoom`; `ZoomRules`). View › Zoom In ⌘+ (⌘= too: `ReviewPDFView.performKeyEquivalent`), Zoom Out ⌘−, Actual Size ⌘0 (100%), Fit Width ⌘9; ⌘-scroll zooms at the pointer (the point under it stays), from 50% to 400%; a pinch zooms as PDFView does it and its scale becomes the zoom, held within 50–400% as the others. Zoom In and Out step through 50, 75, 100, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300 and 400% and Fit Width's own scale where it falls between (a step within 1% of it is Fit Width). Fit Width is the default (`autoScales`) and refits with the window; any other zoom is a fixed scale. A zoom stays with its paper (by path, every round) until the app quits; nothing is stored. A zoom from a menu keeps the point a third of the way down the view where it was (`putAtThird`, shared with Focus's switch). **Focus** multiplies the zoom by 1.3 (at Fit Width as before: 1.3 times Review's fit, never wider than the view); a zoom chosen in Focus is the Review zoom times 1.3, and Review gets it back when Focus ends. **The status line**, at any zoom but Fit Width, shows it after the page number, "125% ⌄", a menu of Fit Width and 100, 125, 150, 175 and 200%. The page stays one continuous column; the other layouts left with PDFKit's menu. The selection's toolbar and the mark's go on a zoom, as before; the outlines, the find's ring, labels and bars are annotations or are laid again on the scale's change (`PDFViewScaleChanged`).

**Link boxes** (PLAN §4.1): hyperref's green and red link boxes are drawn with no border, in memory only, when a PDF loads (`PDFController.hideLinkBoxes`: a `PDFBorder` of width 0). Not `shouldDisplay = false`, as the plan had it: PDFKit then takes the link as not clickable, and a click on it no longer went there. The links still act as before (a click goes at once; since P0.3 a click previews, §37). The cards' crops (`Crops.swift`) render from a document of their own and still draw the boxes.

**Debug items** (§13): `menu page,x,y[ <title>]` lists the whole menu (titles, ticks, separators, submenus) with the selection before and after; `zoom in|out|actual|fit|<percent>` and `zoom by <factor> page,x,y` drive the zoom and give its state.

**Checked**: `PDFMenuTests` (each target's items on the round-2 fixture, no PDFKit item and no word selected; the selection, a selection over a mark kept, a new and a carried mark, overlapping marks, a change's band, a change under a mark on either tab, a link with Go to, the choice row asked from the menu staying; a hidden link still going; the words; the card's items that can act; the zoom's steps, the third kept, Focus's 1.3, ⌘-scroll at the pointer, the zoom kept across a reload, a pinch held in the range), the updated `SelectionTests`, `PDFPaneFixTests` (A2-05 now: no word selected) and `SettingsTests` (Mark Paragraph left out while busy), and a background run on the synthetic paper (implementation-notes.md, 2026-10-29).

## 36. The way back: one Back for the PDF and the window (2026-10-29)
Step P0.4 of `design/pdf-reader/PLAN.md` (§5; decision 2). App only; no engine change.

**Steps** (`PDFSteps`, `NavHistory.pushStep`/`fold`, Navigation.swift; `PDFController.willJump`, `AppModel.recordPDFStep`). A jump in the PDF records the place it leaves (`PDFPlace`: the page, the point a third of the way down, the paper's zoom and its label "p.4 · 2.1 Data", the page and the Contents' section there) as a place of the window's history, marked `step`, while you stay in that paper's Review. A **look-up** records each time: a click on a link within the PDF (the app follows it itself now: `ReviewPDFView.mouseDown`, `PDFController.followLink`, the target a third of the way down; a URL's link still goes to PDFView, which opens the browser; a drag from a link selects; with the round's link table a click previews and Go to records, §37), the link menu's Go to, a Contents row, Jump's comment, edit and section, Find's first match (and Find “…” in Paper), a card's page number and an edit's link. A **run** (a card clicked, J/K, an answer moving on, N) records one step, where it began, so Back skips the cards; your own scroll (a wheel or trackpad, PDFView's keys, the scroller dragged) or a look-up ends it. Find's first match is a look-up that begins a run from the place it leaves (`PDFSteps.Kind.find`): its next matches (Return, ›, ⌘G, typing on from a match shown) and cards right after it record no step of their own once it has one, the chip following them, so one Back returns to where reading left off, never to a match of a half-typed query. Neither records when the target is in view or less than a view away from the place left (for a run, from where it began). Entering Review is a window step already: the moves that open it (the overview's double click, Continue, Back into another paper, Jump from the overview, its find too, and Reopen in Round N from the overview) record none. PDFKit's own history stays unused.

**Folding**: on leaving Review (another paper, the overview, a hub page) the visit's steps go from the history and the Review place pushed keeps the PDF's place, so Back into Review returns there (`ReviewTarget.pdf`: the selection put back without moving the PDF, then the place). Showing another round drops the steps too (they named the other round's PDF; the round menu is not a place). At most 20 steps a visit (`NavHistory.stepLimit`, the oldest going first), within the history's 64. Back or Forward to a step of one Review keeps the place left a step; two Review places of one paper that are not steps (a paper removed from between them) stay two visits. A double click on the sidebar row of the paper in Review, whose first click showed the overview and folded the steps, takes them back with that place (`NavHistory.dropLast`); the chip, gone with the overview, does not come back, but ‹ and its help tag still name the step. Back to a step moves only the PDF: the selection stays.

**Back to a place** (`PDFController.go(to:)`): the paper's zoom then, the point put back a third of the way down, and the text line nearest it flashed with the existing flash (none over a figure); nothing when it is there already (Review shown again). It waits for its PDF while another loads.

**Controls**. The chip (`BackChip`, `BackChipView`): "‹ Back to p.4 · 2.1 Data" at the bottom left of the PDF, 8 pt above the status line and lifted above a reading toast while one shows; it appears after a recorded jump, goes quiet after 4 s (secondary words, no edge, still a button) and goes after Back or Forward, a scroll that puts the jump's target more than two views from the third of the view, or the next jump; the later cards of a run that has its step keep it, the chip following the run. Deviation: quiet, not plain text: plain words in Dark Mode's light ink would not show on the white page, so it keeps its ground, solid as the find bar's (a material over the white page goes a muddy grey in Dark Mode). The toolbar's ‹ › and ⌘[ ⌘] step through the PDF's steps first; their help tags name a step ("Back to p.4 · 2.1 Data (⌘[)"; `AppModel.backLabel`, `forwardLabel`). A mouse's side buttons (3 back, 4 forward) anywhere in the review's window (`AppModel.sideButton`, a local monitor). Back to p.N · <section> is the first item of the page's menu while Back is a step. Not built: holding ‹ to list the steps (PLAN §5.1). The last place (§24) still keeps only a page. Performance (§10): the chip is a light overlay observing only itself and the toast; on scroll only arithmetic runs while it shows, and nothing is published unless it goes.

**Debug items** (§13): `link <page> <n>` (a click on the n-th link within the PDF on that page, both from 0, as the mouse sends it: {link: {words, to}, nav}); `sidebutton back|forward` (a side button posted to the window, through the monitor); `nav` now also gives `steps` and `forwardSteps` (each place's step label or null), `place` ({label, page, y}), `chip` ({label, faded} or null), `flash`, `backHelp`, `forwardHelp`.

**Checked** (2026-10-29): `PDFStepRulesTests` (a look-up records unless near, a run records one step where it began and ends with your own scroll or a look-up, near, Back's line), `NavStepTests` (the 20-step cap, folding on leaving Review, Back between steps, two visits of one paper's Review, a double click's first click taken back with its steps), `BackFlowTests` on the reading fixture in a PDF view (a link click records and Back returns a third of the way down with a flash, Forward again; Go to and the page's Back item; a near target records nothing; a run of cards is one step and the chip stays through it, Back keeps the card; Contents and Back; leaving Review folds the steps and Back into Review is at the place; the chip after a scroll of more than two views; the side buttons in the review's window only; Find's Return and typing on to far matches add no step and one Back returns; Jump's find from the overview records none). A background test bundle on a copy of `samples/promo-demo/review` (see implementation-notes.md).

## 37. Reference previews (2026-10-29)
The app half of step P0.3 of `design/pdf-reader/PLAN.md` (§4, §3.3, §3.4; decision 1). The engine's half (the link table, `rN/links.json`, and the read-only `links` request) is in `engine/CONTRACT.md`, amendment "Reference previews".

**The table** (`LinkTable`, Previews.swift; `AppModel.requestLinks`). Asked once per round shown (paper, round, created), for every round with a PDF, open or earlier, outside `call`: it changes nothing, has no state and does not make the app busy. Until it comes, and when there is none (an engine before it answers "Unknown method 'links'.", a table that could not be built, any error), a click on a link goes there at once with a step of Back, as in §36. A table for another round than the one shown is never used. The decoding is lenient: a link pointing at no target is left out.

**A click previews** (`ReviewPDFView.mouseDown`, `PDFController.showPreview`). A click without a drag on a link the table knows (hyperref's, or one the engine found in the source: "??" and links without hyperref) shows its preview; a drag from it still selects. It previews when its target has a place, when it is missing, or for a URL the app opens (http, https, mailto only), the PDF's own link as well as one the engine found in the source: the address first, the browser only on a second click or from the preview; a URL the table does not know still goes to PDFView, and any other link goes there as without the table. A link under a mark previews (the link is tried first, as in §36). While a preview shows, a click on its link again goes there (a URL: the browser; a missing target: the preview closes), and so does the second click of a double click (spent, so it selects no word); a press on its link is tracked as any press, so a drag from it closes the preview and selects; any other click in the PDF closes it and acts as usual (a click on another link shows that one's preview). Esc (first, before §9's keys), a click outside the PDF view (a local monitor while it shows), a drag, a scroll, a zoom, the right-click menu, Review hidden and another paper or round close it. The pointer is a pointing hand over the links it previews (PDFView's own over the PDF's links). No hover preview: resting 0.15 s on a link only draws its crop into the cache (`hoverLink`, `prefetch`), so the click shows the picture at once.

**The preview** (`LinkPreviewView` in a `FloatView` over the PDF, as the floating toolbar): "Figure 3 · p.7" (the target's label and page; a URL's "Link"), the pin and Go to › on its right; then the target as printed: a crop of the round's PDF through the cards' `CropCache` (off the main thread, `CropPicture`), look `.paper`, on white paper in a hairline frame, with no mark outline (no rects), the target's box 4 pt round it. Its size (`PreviewLayout`): the page's scale, at most 1.5×, smaller so it fits 90% of the PDF view's width and 70% of the window's height and the room on its side; it goes above the link's line when it fits there, else below, else on the side with more room, 6 pt from the line and never over it, centred on the link and kept 8 pt inside the view across. Its words stay within the same 90% (`AtMost`): a long title is cut in the middle, a note wraps, "Cited" pages run on in rows; their height is measured with the pictures at no size, so "continues on p.9" and a long note count against the 70% and the room. Under it, by kind: a figure, table or algorithm and an equation "Cited on p.3 · p.8"; a citation "Cited 3×: p.2 · p.5" and Copy Reference (the entry's words as printed; left out when none are found in its box, so the clipboard is never emptied); a section, theorem, appendix or footnote nothing; a missing target its note ("No \label{fig:old} in this round's source.") and, in an open round, Comment… (its words selected, then the editor); a source URL its address, Open in Browser and Copy Link. Each "Cited on" page is a link: it goes to the link on that page that cites the target (another than the one clicked when there is one), a third of the way down and flashed, a step of Back. A `\cite{a,b,c}` shows each entry the link lists, the clicked one first, at one scale; an author-year entry's box is widened to its first line where it hangs left of the others (`hanging`). A table across pages shows its first part and "continues on p.9".

**Go to** (`goToTarget`): the Go to ›, a click on a picture (that entry), a second click on the link, or the link menu's Go to <Label> · p.N. The place left is a step of Back (§36's rules: none when the target is near), the target's top a third of the way down, centred (`putAtThird`), and all its parts flashed.

**Keep open** (`pinPreview`, `keepOpen`): the pin, or a drag of the title row more than 6 pt away (the preview follows the pointer and becomes a window where it is let go), makes it a small resizable utility window titled as the preview: the picture scaled to fit and Go to › (a click on the picture goes too). It floats over the review window while the app is in front and hides when it is not; it never takes the keys (`becomesKeyOnlyIfNeeded`) and is not a review window (its delegate is the PDF's controller, not `QuitOnClose`). Several can stay; they close when the PDF shown changes (another paper or round), when Review is hidden (the overview) and when a hub page shows, and are never saved. A test run's stays behind, as its window does.

**Menus** (§35). On a link with the table: Preview <Label> (a missing target's Preview “??”, its printed words), Go to <Label> · p.N, Keep <Label> Open; a citation's Copy Reference; in an open round Comment on “<its words>”…, taking in the name printed before a number hyperref links alone (“Figure 1”, “Eq. (4)”), so the note is not anchored on one digit; a URL's Open in Browser (http, https and mailto only: a link to a file or an app is not opened from it) and Copy Link. On a figure, table or algorithm the table knows (its box, before the page's menu): the page's Back to p.N · <section> first while Back is a step (Go to puts the figure under the pointer); in an open round, not busy, Comment on Figure 1… (a pending box round the whole float, then the editor: the ⌥-drag in one click) and Add Note Here; Keep Figure 1 Open, Cited on ▸ p.N.

**Performance** (§10). Nothing new redraws while the PDF scrolls: the preview closes at the first scroll; the kept windows are not tied to it. The crops render off the main thread; on the pointer's move only a binary search of the page's links runs.

**Not built**: Search Google Scholar (P1.3); the mark's menu ending with Preview <Label> under a mark (PLAN §3.3); a subfigure outlined in its figure; `\label` before `\caption` naming the section (the engine gives a missing target). Holding a kept window over another app.

**Debug items** (§13): `preview [<what>]` (a click on a table link, the link again, a double click, Go to, a picture, the pin, a drag, a Cited on page, Copy Reference, a scroll: the preview's kind, title, entries and their sizes and whether drawn, footer, side, gap to the line, its shares of the view and window, the kept windows); `link <page> <n>` gives the preview too.

**Checked** (2026-10-29): `PreviewTests` on the reading fixture with `Fixtures/links-round2.json` (the engine's link builder run on the fixture's PDF, its labels and float boxes set by hand: the table decoded, a lenient decoding, which links preview; the layout rules; a click previews and moves nothing, never over the line, within 90% / 70%, at most 1.5×; a second click and a double click go, a step, the flash, Back; Esc, a click elsewhere, a scroll and a zoom close it; no table or another round's: a click goes at once; a figure and Go to with its top a third of the way down, Cited on; a citation of two keys, the clicked first; the pin and a drag keep it open, a small drag does not, closed with another round's PDF; resting on a link draws its crop; a missing reference from the source with Comment…; the link menu and its items; the figure menu, its box and editor, busy; review fixes 2026-10-30: the words within the width, Copy Reference only with words, a drag from the preview's link selects, kept windows close with Review hidden and with no paper, a hyperref URL previews, Open in Browser not for a file: link, a table across pages in a short window, Comment on “Figure 1”, the figure menu's Back), and the earlier PDF classes (`BackFlowTests`, `PDFMenuTests`, `SelectionTests`, `PDFPaneFixTests`, …). A background test bundle on a copy of `samples/promo-demo/review` (implementation-notes.md, 2026-10-29).

## 38. The reader's next four (2026-10-30)

`design/pdf-reader/PLAN.md` P1.1, P1.2, P1.4 and P1.6, as planned; no setting.

- **Go to Page (P1.4).** The status line's "p.4 of 12" is a button (help tag "Go to Page"; VoiceOver "Page 4 of 12"): a popover "Page [4] of 12", its field focused with the number selected, so typing replaces it; Return goes there (a page the PDF has not beeps), and the keys go back to the PDF. Jump takes "p.12", "p 12", "p12" or "page 12": a "Pages" group first, "Go to p.12 · of 20" (a bare number stays a search). Either puts the page's top at the view's top, a look-up, a step of Back (`PDFController.goToPage`).
- **The same place across rounds (P1.1).** The round menu, Jump's rounds and "Back to Round N" open the other round where you were reading: the block at the view's third (`Reading.place`: a paragraph, float or heading, and how far below its part's top the third was) is followed by the engine's `follow` (CONTRACT amendment "The same place across rounds") and put a third of the way down that round's PDF, as far below the same part's top (at most its height; a neighbour's top when `exact` is false), its line flashed; no step of Back (another round ends the PDF's steps). Without paragraphs or anything to follow, the same page and point. A selected comment comes along and is selected there; the PDF goes to it only when its mark was on screen, else to the paragraph (the card is still shown). A reveal in another round (Back and Forward, the overview's double click, Continue) goes to its own target and follows nothing. A reply that comes after you have scrolled, pressed a key or selected something else (the engine may first be finding the round's paragraphs) is dropped.
- **Clean Copy (P1.2).** Copy in the selection's menu and ⌘C give the words as you would quote them (`CleanCopy`): lines joined into paragraphs, a word hyphenated at a line's end mended, the margin's line numbers dropped, ligatures spelt out, soft hyphens gone. A paragraph (a blank line) starts where the engine's paragraph, float or heading changes (`reading`'s parts; no space between paragraphs in most classes), and, between lines outside them, where the next line starts lower on the same page by more than 0.6 of a line. Line numbers are those of `round.build.rulers`: a line of digits within a ruler, or digits as a word of their own at a line's start (end) when it reaches the left (right) ruler; a page without rulers keeps every digit. A line-end hyphen stays after a word that has a hyphen already ("state-of-the-"), before a capital ("BERT-Base"), or for a compound: the paper's own text has "temperature-softened" elsewhere; else not when it has "temperaturesoftened" or that is a word ("further-" "more"); else kept when both halves are dictionary words of four letters or more (`NSSpellChecker`, English). The paper's text is read only when such a hyphen comes up, and the menu's Copy cleans only when chosen. A dash or slash that ends a word joins without a space ("3–5", "and/or"); one after a space keeps it ("the model – which").
- **Markers beside the scroller (P1.6).** A 6 pt strip at the PDF's right edge, left of the scroller (a mouse's, which takes room, or where an overlay scroller shows, so a drag on its knob never lands on a tick) and from 46 pt down (below the find's button): ticks drawn 6 × 3 pt and clicked 14 × 11 pt. In an open round the comments to check are amber (your move, as the panel's amber); while change marks are shown the edits are green and those no comment asked for slate teal (under the amber), named by their float or section; while Find has a query, only its matches, in the find's grey. A tick's place is its share of the pages' heights, so the strip is laid again when the marks or the matches change (`PDFController.updateStrip`), never on a scroll. Ticks of a kind within 4 pt merge ("2 comments to check · p.5–6"); hover names it, a click selects the comment or edit as Jump does (a look-up, a step of Back), or shows the match as Find's first (its next matches go on from it).

**Debug items** (§13): `gotopage <page> [<y>]` (Go to Page, or that y a third of the way down), `copytext <page> <x0> <y0> <x1> <y1>` (a selection from one point to the other and ⌘C: `clean` and `raw`), `strip` (the items, and the ticks of a 600 pt strip), `stripclick <i>`, `windowclick <x> <y>` (a click at a point of the window, with the view hit); `round` gives `third`.

**Checked** (2026-10-30): `ReaderNextTests` (page queries, the place read and where it goes, Clean Copy's joins, dashes, hyphens, paragraphs by block and by gap, rulers' numbers and decimals, ligatures, the strip's shares and merging); engine `test_follow_*`. A background test bundle on a copy of `samples/promo-demo/review` (results after the review fixes in implementation-notes.md). Not seen: the Go to Page popover itself (a synthetic click opens no SwiftUI popover in a background run, the build line's Details… neither).
